### [1] [Loop Equations for Multi-Matrix Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25095)
*Aravinth Kulanthaivelu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper revisits and refines the combinatorial loop-equation method to compute planar disc amplitudes in multi-matrix models with polynomial potentials; it organizes Schwinger-Dyson constraints by boundary insertion level, yields algebraic equations for disc functions, recovers known Potts model results, and obtains new results—including for unequal couplings—and extends applicability to previously unsolved two- and three-matrix models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a systematic, combinatorial approach to solving planar disc amplitudes in multi-matrix models beyond the standard one- and two-matrix cases, where saddle-point or direct analytic methods fail or become unwieldy.

Method: Combinatorial loop-equation method: reorganizing Schwinger-Dyson constraints by the 'level' of boundary insertions; identifying closure conditions under which the system reduces to an algebraic equation for the planar disc resolvent (i.e., disc partition function).

Result: Recovery of known planar disc partition functions for fixed, mixed, and free boundary conditions in the three-state Potts model; derivation of a new algebraic equation for the fixed-boundary resolvent with unequal quadratic and cubic couplings; successful computation of disc partition functions for several unsolved two- and three-matrix models.

Conclusion: The refined loop-equation method is a practical, algebraic tool for solving planar disc amplitudes in broader classes of multi-matrix models, significantly extending solvability beyond conventional limits.

Abstract: We revisit the combinatorial loop-equation method for computing planar disc amplitudes in multi-matrix models with polynomial potentials. We develop a procedure which organises the Schwinger-Dyson constraints by the level of their boundary insertions and, when the resulting system closes, produces an algebraic equation for the planar disc function. Using this method we recover the disc partition functions associated with fixed, mixed, and free boundary conditions in the three-state Potts model, previously obtained by saddle-point methods. As a novelty we derive an algebraic equation for the fixed-boundary resolvent of the three-state Potts model with unequal quadratic and cubic couplings. We further show one can compute the disc partition function for several unsolved two- and three-matrix models. These examples demonstrate that loop equations provide a practical route to identifying and solving sectors of multi-matrix models beyond the standard one- and two-matrix cases.

</details>


### [2] [On Thermodynamic Universalities and Optics of B-deformed Reissner--Nordström--AdS Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25161)
*Adil Belhaj,Maryem Jemri*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses machine learning to analyze thermodynamic (Van der Waals criticality, Joule-Thomson expansion) and optical (shadow) properties of B-deformed Reissner–Nordström–AdS black holes—string-theoretic deformations tied to the NS-NS B-field—and constrains deformation parameters using EHT data from M87* and Sgr A*.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how non-commutative geometry-inspired stringy deformations (via the NS-NS B-field) affect black hole thermodynamics and optics, and to assess whether ML can reliably detect universal critical behavior and constrain fundamental parameters using observational data.

Method: Numerical computation of thermodynamic critical ratios (P-V criticality, Joule-Thomson inversion curves) and optical shadow profiles; construction and training of a fully connected neural network to classify Van der Waals behavior; parameter inference via EHT observational constraints on M87* and Sgr A* shadows.

Result: The trained neural network accurately identifies Van der Waals-type thermodynamic phase behavior; B-deformation modifies shadow size and shape; B-field parameter is constrained using empirical black hole shadow data.

Conclusion: Machine learning serves as a robust tool for detecting universal thermodynamic features and extracting physical parameters in string-motivated black hole models; B-deformation induces observable deviations in both thermodynamics and imaging signatures, testable with current EHT capabilities.

Abstract: Using machine learning techniques, we study the Reissner--Nordström--AdS black holes in the framework of extended space-time derivatives inspired by non-commutative geometry in string theory where the deformation parameters are related to the inverse of the NS-NS B-field. We refer to these solutions as B-deformed Reissner--Nordström--AdS black holes. Precisely, we examine the thermodynamic and the optical behaviors of such stringy deformed AdS black holes. Exploiting advanced numerical computations, we first examine the corresponding thermodynamic Van der Waals behaviors by calculating the universal ratios describing the P-V criticality and the Joule-Thomson expansion features. Applying such numerical results, we construct reliable training data for a fully connected neural network being developed to determine whether such stringy black hole configurations exhibit thermodynamic Van der Waals aspects. Concretely, we reveal that the fully connected and trained neural network accurately detects Van der Waals behaviors. Supported by the observational data reported by the Event Horizon Telescope international collaborations, we investigate the optical shadows of such B-deformed Reissner--Nordström--AdS black holes using machine learning methods. In order to provide corroborated models, we constraint the involved parameters including the stringy one B by making use of M87* and Sgr A* black hole empirical data.

</details>


### [3] [The landscape and the swampland of O(3,3) gauged supergravities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25203)
*Mariana Graña,Diego Perugini*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper systematically classifies half-maximal 7D gauged supergravities, links orbifold twists to generalized fluxes via double field theory, and identifies non-orbifold-realizable gaugings as swampland candidates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the string theory origin of geometric and non-geometric fluxes in gauged supergravities and delineate which gaugings lie in the swampland versus the landscape.

Method: Constructs a precise dictionary between orbifold twists (on group manifolds with fluxes) and generalized fluxes in double field theory; analyzes the full set of half-maximal gaugings in seven dimensions using group-theoretic and duality-covariant techniques.

Result: Identifies the subset of 7D half-maximal gauged supergravities that admit (a)symmetric orbifold realizations; the remaining gaugings—lacking such a construction—are classified as swampland candidates.

Conclusion: Not all consistent half-maximal 7D gauged supergravities have a known string embedding; the absence of an orbifold realization signals potential inconsistency with quantum gravity, supporting the swampland paradigm.

Abstract: Compactifications of string theory on group manifolds with fluxes, performed via the Scherk-Schwarz mechanism, give rise to gauged supergravities. Using double field theory, this procedure can be made T-duality covariant, yielding a set of geometric and non-geometric fluxes whose string theory origin is often unclear. In this paper, we systematically analyze the complete catalog of half-maximal gauged supergravities in seven dimensions. By constructing a dictionary between orbifold twists and generalized fluxes, we identify which gaugings admit a realization as (a)symmetric orbifolds, and which do not, establishing the latter as candidates for the gauged supergravity swampland.

</details>


### [4] [Quantum Clausius relation beyond de Sitter equilibrium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25206)
*Jinn-Ouk Gong,TaeHun Kim,Junghwan Lee,Chang Sub Shin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives a quantum version of the Clausius relation for conformal fields in quasi-de Sitter spacetime, confirming entropy–heat consistency at leading order and extending it to a scheme-independent entropy balance including Wald entropy beyond leading order—without invoking gravitational equations of motion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a quantum field theoretic foundation for dynamical horizon thermodynamics beyond exact de Sitter equilibrium, avoiding reliance on classical gravitational equations.

Method: Computes net heat flow across the apparent horizon from the renormalized stress-energy tensor; independently computes time evolution of renormalized von Neumann entropy via the replica method; employs Hubble-flow expansion for perturbative analysis.

Result: Exact agreement between heat flow and entropy change at leading order in Hubble-flow expansion; a scheme-independent entropy balance emerges at higher orders, incorporating Wald entropy.

Conclusion: The quantum Clausius relation holds nontrivially in dynamical quasi-de Sitter spacetimes, realizing horizon thermodynamics directly from QFT—establishing a robust link between quantum information (entropy) and energy flux (heat) in cosmological horizons.

Abstract: We establish a direct quantum Clausius relation for conformal fields inside the apparent horizon of a quasi-de Sitter spacetime. We compute the net heat flow across the horizon from the renormalized stress-energy tensor, and independently determine the time evolution of the renormalized von Neumann entropy using the replica method. At leading order in the Hubble-flow expansion, the two calculations agree exactly, without using the gravitational equations of motion. Beyond leading order, we find a scheme-independent entropy balance that includes the corresponding Wald entropy. Our results provide a quantum field theoretic realization of dynamical horizon thermodynamics beyond exact de Sitter equilibrium.

</details>


### [5] [A Quantum-Mechanical Model for the M5-Brane](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25207)
*Manuel Artime,Ralph Blumenhagen,Thomas Raml*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a maximally supersymmetric extension of the BFSS matrix model using the 5-Lie algebra A₆, yielding a Lagrangian theory with Chern-Simons-like dynamics for structure constants; it arises from truncating the lightcone M5-brane action on S⁵ and is interpreted as a quantum-mechanical model for lowest-lying M5-brane modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a consistent, maximally supersymmetric matrix model capturing essential features of M5-branes—beyond the BFSS model’s D0-brane description—by leveraging higher-algebraic (5-Lie) structures and connecting to known M5-brane worldvolume physics.

Method: Constructs a Lagrangian theory based on the 5-Lie algebra A₆, promoting BFSS 2-bracket structure constants to dynamical fields governed by a Chern-Simons-type kinetic term; derives it via consistent truncation of the single lightcone M5-brane action on S⁵ to zero-modes in spatial directions.

Result: A new maximally supersymmetric matrix model that reproduces expected M5-brane features (e.g., correct symmetry enhancement, quantum-mechanical interpretation), is fully Lagrangian, and admits a direct geometric derivation from M5-brane dynamics on S⁵.

Conclusion: The A₆-based model provides a viable quantum-mechanical framework for the lowest-lying modes of the M5-brane, generalizing BFSS in a way that respects M-theoretic expectations and higher-algebraic structure.

Abstract: We present a maximally supersymmetric extension of the BFSS matrix model based on the 5-Lie algebra ${\mathcal A}_6$. The theory is Lagrangian, promotes the BFSS 2-bracket structure constants to dynamical objects governed by a Chern-Simons-like kinetic term, and displays features expected from a theory of M5-branes. We show that it can also be obtained from the single lightcone M5-brane action on a spatial five-sphere after a truncation to the time coordinate. The resulting theory is a consistent truncation and, analogously to the $U(2)$ BFSS model, supports an interpretation of the $\mathcal{A}_6$ theory as a quantum-mechanical model for the lowest-lying modes of the M5-brane.

</details>


### [6] [The Double Copy of the Black Ring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25209)
*Damien A. Easson*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs an exact Weyl double copy for the 5D singly spinning Emparan–Reall black ring, showing its Weyl curvature decomposes into two source-free Maxwell squares weighted by scalars—associated respectively with the stationary Killing vector and topological magnetic flux—and confirms genuine De Smet type 22 via irreducible quadratic factorization of the Weyl polynomial.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the exact Weyl double copy beyond known algebraically special (type-D/N) solutions to a more generic, non-type-D/N black hole—the 5D spinning black ring—with mixed Weyl types (G and I_i) in its exterior.

Method: Explicit construction of two source-free Maxwell fields on the black-ring background: one aligned with the stationary Killing vector, the other carrying magnetic flux through a sphere linking the ring; derivation of their scalar partners satisfying a shared sourced wave equation; analysis of Weyl polynomial factorization to determine De Smet type.

Result: The full nonlinear Weyl curvature is exactly expressed as a sum of two scalar-weighted Maxwell squares; both Maxwell fields are source-free and live on the black-ring spacetime; the Weyl polynomial factorizes into two distinct irreducible quadratics, confirming genuine De Smet type 22 and excluding reduction to a single Maxwell square.

Conclusion: The 5D spinning black ring admits an exact Weyl double copy involving two physically distinct Maxwell fields and their scalar partners, establishing the first exact double copy for a black object with mixed Weyl types (G and I_i) and confirming genuine De Smet type 22—thus broadening the scope of exact double copy beyond algebraically special spacetimes.

Abstract: We construct an exact Weyl double copy of the singly spinning five-dimensional Emparan--Reall black ring. Its complete nonlinear Weyl curvature is the sum of two scalar-weighted Maxwell squares. One field is generated by the stationary Killing vector; the other carries topological magnetic flux through a sphere linking the ring. Both are source-free Maxwell fields on the black-ring spacetime, with corresponding scalar partners obeying a common sourced wave equation. This extends exact five-dimensional Weyl doubling beyond the type-$\mathrm D$ and type-$\mathrm N$ examples to a black hole whose exterior contains both type-$\mathrm G$ and type-$\mathrm{I}_i$ regions. The Weyl polynomial factorizes into two distinct irreducible quadratics, establishing genuine De Smet type $22$, thereby ruling out a reduction to a single Maxwell square.

</details>


### [7] [Twisted \texorpdfstring{$A_{2N}$}{A(2N)} Argyres--Douglas theories: vertex algebras, Higgs branches, and double covers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25212)
*Christopher Beem,Harshal Kulkarni*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores the relationship between two families of generalized Argyres–Douglas SCFTs (twisted A_{2N} and D_{N+1} series), proposing that the A-series arise via nilpotent Higgsing of the D-series; it identifies their associated VOAs as finite extensions of affine Kac–Moody algebras at non-boundary admissible levels, with Higgs branches often being double covers of nilpotent orbit closures; Z₂-gauged A-series yield unitary SCFTs whose VOAs are exactly those affine Kac–Moody algebras, equipped with a novel R-filtration compatible with graded unitarity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the structural relationship between twisted A_{2N} and D_{N+1} generalized Argyres–Douglas theories, particularly how their associated vertex operator algebras (VOAs) and Higgs branches are related, and to resolve the apparent mismatch between expected affine Kac–Moody VOAs and observed non-trivial extensions in the A-series.

Method: The authors use representation-theoretic and geometric methods from 4d N=2 SCFTs: analyzing Higgs branch geometry via nilpotent orbit closures, leveraging the correspondence between SCFTs and VOAs (via the 4d/2d correspondence), computing VOA structures (e.g., extensions, filtrations), and examining Z₂ gauging as a tool to isolate pure affine Kac–Moody VOAs; evidence includes consistency of central charges, Schur index matches, and module structure comparisons.

Result: The A-series VOAs are finite extensions of affine Kac–Moody VOAs at non-boundary admissible levels (not the algebras themselves); their Higgs branches are typically double covers of nilpotent orbit closures; Z₂ gauging recovers unitary SCFTs whose VOAs are precisely those affine Kac–Moody algebras; these VOAs admit a non-standard R-filtration consistent with graded unitarity.

Conclusion: The twisted A_{2N} and D_{N+1} Argyres–Douglas theories are hierarchically related via nilpotent Higgsing; this explains the VOA extension structure and Higgs branch geometry, and provides new unitary SCFT realizations of affine Kac–Moody VOAs at non-boundary admissible levels—equipped with physically motivated R-filtrations.

Abstract: We investigate the associated vertex operator algebras and Higgs branches of two closely related families of generalised Argyres--Douglas theories, arising from the twisted $A_{2N}$ and twisted $D_{N+1}$ series, respectively. We propose that the $A$-series theories are realised as nilpotent Higgsings of the $D$-series, and we assemble a variety of pieces of evidence for this proposal. A consequence is that the associated vertex operator algebras of the $A$-series are finite extensions of affine Kac--Moody vertex algebras at (non-boundary) admissible levels, rather than those Kac--Moody algebras themselves, and that their Higgs branches are in many cases double covers of nilpotent orbit closures. The $\mathbb{Z}_2$ gaugings of the $A$-series theories then furnish examples of unitary SCFTs whose associated vertex operator algebras are precisely affine Kac--Moody algebras at non-boundary admissible levels. We propose that these vertex algebras are equipped with a non-standard $\mathfrak{R}$-filtration, and comment on compatibility with graded unitarity.

</details>


### [8] [Classification of order-two T-duality orbifolds at the SO(12) free fermionic point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25213)
*Alon E. Faraggi,Stefan Groot Nibbelink,Benjamin Percival*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper classifies order-two asymmetric orbifold T-folds on the SO(12) lattice in type II string theory, parametrizes their spectra via generalized GSO projections, identifies minimal Hodge numbers (1,1) for six cases, conjectures asymmetric mirror symmetry, determines orientifoldable configurations, and analyzes supersymmetry constraints—showing that spin-3/2 states imply vanishing vacuum energy due to supersymmetric spectral reorganization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and classify non-geometric T-fold backgrounds—specifically order-two asymmetric orbifolds—as concrete string-theoretic realizations beyond geometric compactifications, and to explore their supersymmetry properties, Hodge structure, duality symmetries, and phenomenological implications such as spin-3/2 states and vacuum energy.

Method: Systematic classification of all Z2 point groups acting asymmetrically on the six-torus; explicit construction of T-fold configurations on the SO(12) fermionic lattice; parametric derivation of massless spectra using generalized GSO projections; computation of effective Hodge numbers from cohomology of twisted sectors; identification of orientifoldable models via worldsheet parity; analysis of supersymmetry preservation and breaking via spin-structure consistency and Rarita-Schwinger sector content.

Result: Full classification of order-two asymmetric T-folds on SO(12); spectra expressed in terms of generalized GSO phases; six models with minimal Hodge numbers (h_{11}, h_{12}) = (1,1); identification of orientifoldable configurations; discovery that pure asymmetric twisted sectors can host Rarita-Schwinger multiplets; determination of all Z2 point groups admitting non-supersymmetric T-folds; demonstration that presence of spin-3/2 states enforces supersymmetric spectral organization and zero vacuum energy.

Conclusion: Asymmetric orbifold T-folds provide controlled, computable frameworks for non-geometric string vacua; their spectral and topological data (e.g., minimal Hodge numbers) reflect intrinsic non-geometry; spin-3/2 emergence is a robust indicator of residual supersymmetry—even when conventional SUSY-breaking projections are applied—implying vacuum stability through spectral reorganization rather than explicit SUSY preservation.

Abstract: Asymmetric orbifolds provide concrete examples of non-geometric constructions dubbed T-folds. All Z2 point groups of six dimensional asymmetric order-two orbifolds are identified. The order-two T-fold configurations on the SO(12) lattice are classified at the fermionic point of type II string theories for all Z2 point groups. The spectra of the models on these configurations are presented parametrically, in terms of certain generalised GSO phases. The minimal effective Hodge numbers were found to be (h_{11},h_{12})=(1,1) for six T-fold configurations. Asymmetric generalisations of the mirror symmetry map are conjectured. The orientifoldable configurations using the basic worldsheet parity were identified within the classification. Twisted sectors corresponding to pure asymmetric twists may contain Rarita-Schwinger multiplets with spin-3/2 states. Throughout the paper, generalised GSO projections are chosen to preserve the maximal amount of supersymmetry possible. Relaxing this, the order-two point groups were identified for which non--supersymmetric T-folds can be constructed. Since some of them may enhance to N=1 or, even, N=2 supergravities, we argue that appearance of spin-3/2 states necessarily implies that the spectrum has reorder itself in a supersymmetric fashion and hence that the vacuum energy vanishes.

</details>


### [9] [Bulk OPE Coefficients of the $E$-Series Virasoro Minimal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25215)
*Amaury Lhoste,Jiaxin Qiao,Masahito Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes bulk-primary OPE coefficients for Virasoro minimal models with E6, E7, or E8 modular invariants, reducing the conformal bootstrap to a finite algebraic problem via quantum-group 6j symbols; existence/uniqueness is conditionally proven from finite 'seed' data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve the conformal bootstrap for exceptional modular invariants in Virasoro minimal models by overcoming infinite-dimensional complexity via structural simplifications rooted in quantum-group theory and modular constraints.

Method: Algebraic reduction using chiral generalized-minimal-model OPE coefficient normalization, root-of-unity quantum-group 6j symbols, computer-assisted finite seed computation (s=1), and rigorous implication of full Kac-label solutions from seed data under genus-zero bulk locality and crossing symmetry.

Result: Complete tables of reduced bulk-primary OPE coefficients for all E6/E7/E8 minimal models (unitary and nonunitary); finite representative counts (18/32/107); high-precision numerical evidence for existence/uniqueness of seeds; conditional proof that exact seed solutions imply global uniqueness modulo field sign ambiguities.

Conclusion: The conformal bootstrap for exceptional modular invariant Virasoro minimal models is algebraically finite and structurally controlled by quantum-group 6j symbols; full analytic solution hinges on certifying a small finite set of seed coefficients.

Abstract: We present bulk-primary operator-product coefficients for every Virasoro minimal model with an $E_6$, $E_7$, or $E_8$ modular invariant. Dividing by a universal product of chiral generalized-minimal-model OPE coefficients reduces the conformal bootstrap problem to a finite algebraic problem governed by root-of-unity quantum-group $6j$ symbols. The exceptional factors of the OPE coefficients can be chosen independent of the second Kac indices $s$, and their dependence on the central charge reduces to finitely many reference cases and explicit phases. A finite computer-assisted calculation yields candidate algebraic $s=1$ seed data and high-precision numerical evidence for existence and uniqueness modulo primary-field signs. We prove that exact existence and uniqueness for these seeds imply the corresponding result for all Kac labels within the genus-zero bulk locality and crossing equations; the finite seed step remains uncertified. The three families require $18$, $32$, and $107$ representative reduced OPE coefficients for $E_6$, $E_7$, and $E_8$, respectively. We provide reconstruction conventions and coefficient tables for both unitary and nonunitary models. Our calculation assumes only Virasoro symmetry, bulk locality (permutation symmetry) and OPE associativity (crossing equations).

</details>


### [10] [Functional Dimensional Regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25238)
*Piero Beretta,Alessandro Codello*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces Functional Dimensional Regularization (FDR), a non-perturbative functional RG framework unifying dimensional regularization with functional methods; recovers ε-expansion, computes 3D Ising exponents with high accuracy, and enables systematic derivation from other one-loop-exact RG schemes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between the technical simplicity of dimensional regularization (DR) and the non-perturbative versatility of functional RG, overcoming limitations of standard DR (restricted to perturbation theory) and conventional functional RG (lacking scheme independence and direct link to critical dimensions).

Method: Defines FDR beta functions as a continuous-dimension sum over scheme-independent DR beta functions at all critical dimensions, incorporating threshold functions; applies local potential approximation (LPA) and second-order derivative expansion; introduces a 'distillation' procedure to derive FDR flow from arbitrary one-loop-exact RG schemes.

Result: Recovers all multi-critical ε-expansions in LPA; establishes correspondence to functional scaling solutions, spike plots, and eigen-perturbation spectra; computes 3D Ising exponents (η, ν, ω) with rapid convergence and agreement with state-of-the-art non-perturbative results; demonstrates structural links and distinctions vs. proper-time and other functional RGs.

Conclusion: FDR is a self-contained, competitive functional RG framework that inherits DR’s algebraic simplicity while enabling fully non-perturbative, dimension-continuous analysis—unifying critical phenomena tools across perturbative and non-perturbative regimes.

Abstract: We introduce and develop Functional Dimensional Regularization (FDR), a novel functional renormalization group (RG) framework that extends the principles of dimensional regularization beyond the perturbative $\varepsilon$-expansion. The key insight is to define the FDR beta functions, valid in any continuous dimension $d$ and characterized by threshold functions, as a sum over all critical dimensions of the corresponding scheme-independent beta functions computed in DR. We demonstrate that this formalism exhibits all the hallmarks of a fully-fledged functional RG. Within the local potential approximation, we perform a general fixed point analysis, recovering the $\varepsilon$-expansion for all multi-critical models and establishing a direct connection to functional scaling solutions, spike plots, and eigen-perturbation spectra. Extending the framework to the second order of the derivative expansion, we compute the critical exponents for the Ising universality class in $d=3$, providing estimates for the anomalous dimension $η$ and RG spectrum that converge rapidly and show favorable agreement with state-of-the-art results. We present a detailed comparison with non-perturbative and proper-time RG approaches, highlighting structural similarities and key differences. In particular, we introduce a ``distillation'' procedure that allows one to systematically derive the FDR flow from any other one-loop-exact RG scheme. Our results establish FDR as a self-contained and competitive RG framework that combines the technical simplicity of dimensional regularization with the versatility of the functional approach.

</details>


### [11] [Anyon Crystallization by Statistics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25249)
*Zohar Komargodski,Xuzixiang Lou,Ivri Nagar,Domenico Orlando,Susanne Reffert,Amit Sever*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the anyon gas in the near-bosonic limit (large N, fixed N/k), finding a crystalline ground state with full screening of statistical interactions; it identifies a quantum phase transition to a superfluid phase below a critical k_c ≈ 2–5, challenging the assumption that purely statistical interactions always yield superfluidity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing question of whether an anyon gas with only statistical interactions must be superfluid, by examining the solvable near-bosonic regime where conventional many-body techniques fail.

Method: Analytical and renormalization-group analysis of the anyon gas in the large-N, fixed-N/k limit; characterization of the crystalline ground state via unit-cell size, energy, acoustic modes, angular momentum crossings, and finite-size scaling.

Result: Discovery of a stable anyon crystal phase with complete screening of statistical interactions; identification of a quantum phase transition at k_c ≈ 2–5 between crystal and superfluid; mapping of RG flow revealing new fixed points and finite-size effects.

Conclusion: Purely statistical interactions do not guarantee superfluidity; the anyon gas can form a conventional, non-superfluid crystal, overturning common lore and establishing a richer phase diagram governed by k = N/(statistical flux quanta).

Abstract: We revisit the long-standing problem of the anyon gas. We study the solvable near-bosonic limit of a large number of anyons $N$ at fixed $N/k$, where $1/k$ characterizes the statistical interaction. In this limit, the ground state is a crystal with order $N/k$ lattice sites. The statistical interactions, mediated by a long-range gauge field, are completely screened within the crystalline phase. The resulting state is a conventional crystal without superfluidity. We characterize this anyon crystal by determining its unit-cell size, ground-state energy, acoustic excitations, angular momentum crossings, and other properties.
  Our results indicate a quantum phase transition between the crystal at $k>k_c$ and a superfluid at $k<k_c$, with a critical value $k_c\simeq2\text{--}5$. These results imply that an anyon gas with only statistical interactions is not necessarily a superfluid, contrary to common lore.
  We also present a detailed analysis of the renormalization-group evolution of anyon--anyon interactions, identifying many new potential fixed points and elucidating finite-size effects.

</details>


### [12] [Primary Decompositions in Lorentz-Covariant Rings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25253)
*Giuseppe De Laurentis,David Tai*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper advances computational algebraic geometry methods for simplifying integral coefficients in scattering amplitudes by improving primary decomposition techniques—specifically addressing Lorentz covariance preservation, numerical point sampling near varieties, primality testing of unmixed ideals, and delivering new codimension-two decompositions for five-point amplitudes in dimensional regularization and one-mass kinematics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct compact, singularity-manifest representations of integral coefficients in scattering amplitudes—essential for efficient analytic and numeric evaluation—by leveraging algebraic geometry, particularly fraction fields of polynomial quotient rings and primary decomposition of ideals defining pole loci.

Method: Extends ideal saturation-based primary decomposition; introduces Lorentz-covariant ideal generator construction via fitting ansätze in special kinematic limits; improves semi-numerical algorithms for computing independent sets, variety dimensions, numerical point generation near varieties, and primality testing of unmixed ideals.

Result: New codimension-two primary decompositions for: (i) five-point massless amplitudes at higher orders in the dimensional regulator ε; (ii) finite remainders in five-point one-mass kinematics, revealing a previously unknown spurious singular locus.

Conclusion: Algebraic methods grounded in ideal theory and enhanced with covariance-aware ansatz fitting and robust semi-numerical geometry tools significantly advance the practical computation of scattering amplitude structures, enabling previously intractable decompositions and exposing new physical features like spurious singularities.

Abstract: An effective strategy for constructing compact representations of integral coefficients in scattering amplitudes is to make their singularity structure manifest. This is naturally achieved by treating them as elements of fraction fields of polynomial quotient rings, where poles correspond to algebraic vanishing loci. Simplification via partial fraction decomposition relies on exploiting the structure of the numerators and is governed, at least in a first approximation, by their behaviour on irreducible codimension-two varieties. These are identifiable through primary decompositions of the associated ideals. Despite the availability of general algorithms, primary decompositions remain computationally challenging and often require tailored strategies. In this work, we extend an approach based on ideal saturation, identify an issue related to the breaking of Lorentz covariance in the choice of ideal generators, and present a solution based on fitting ansätze in special kinematic limits. We also provide substantial improvements to an algorithm for the generation of numerical points in proximity of given varieties and to one for testing primality of unmixed ideals, both of which rely on a semi-numerical procedure for computing independent sets and variety dimensions. Finally, we present new codimension-two primary decompositions for five-point massless amplitudes at higher powers in the dimensional regulator, and for finite remainders with five-point one-mass kinematics, where a new spurious singular locus appears.

</details>


### [13] [Dual symmetry breaking and magnetic charge screening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25324)
*Saulo Carneiro*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends dual electrodynamics via Lagrangian formulation with electric and magnetic Higgs scalars coupled nonlocally to Mandelstam potentials; in a magnetic-Higgs vacuum, Maxwell equations stay local, electric potential remains massless (yielding long-range Coulomb interaction), magnetic sector gains mass (screening magnetic charge per Swieca theorem), Dirac quantisation persists, and screened monopoles are proposed as dark sector candidates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a consistent dual electrodynamics framework where both electric and magnetic charges can be incorporated dynamically, while addressing confinement/screening mechanisms and potential connections to dark matter via topological excitations.

Method: Lagrangian formulation with spontaneously broken dual symmetry; introduction of electric and magnetic Higgs scalar fields coupled to Mandelstam's nonlocal potentials; analysis of vacuum structure (magnetic Higgs condensate); derivation and interpretation of generalised Maxwell equations; application of Swieca theorem for magnetic charge screening; enforcement of Dirac quantisation condition from symmetric phase.

Result: Local generalised Maxwell equations despite nonlocal scalar couplings; massless electric photon and massive magnetic photon in magnetic-Higgs vacuum; Coulombic electric interaction at large distances; exponentially suppressed pole-pole and charge-pole interactions; magnetic charge screening à la Swieca; preservation of Dirac quantisation; identification of screened monopoles as viable dark sector degrees of freedom.

Conclusion: Dual electrodynamics with magnetic Higgs mechanism provides a theoretically consistent setting for electric-magnetic asymmetry, naturally realising charge screening dichotomy and offering a field-theoretic basis for monopole-based dark matter models.

Abstract: We extend a Lagrangian formulation of dual electrodynamics by introducing electric and magnetic Higgs scalar fields. The scalars couple to Mandelstam nonlocal potentials which enter the electric and magnetic sectors. We consider the vacuum in which only the magnetic scalar has a nonzero expectation value. The generalised Maxwell equations remain local even though the scalar couplings are nonlocal. In this phase the local electric potential keeps a massless pole while the magnetic sector acquires a mass. The electric interaction is therefore Coulombian at large distances while the pole-pole and charge-pole interactions become short ranged. The magnetic charge is then screened in the sense of the Swieca theorem while the electric charge is not. The Dirac condition is inherited from the symmetric phase as a quantisation rule for the microscopic electric and magnetic couplings. We also comment on screened monopoles as possible dark sector degrees of freedom.

</details>


### [14] [Hidden symmetries and the universality of Hawking radiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25475)
*Ivan Pérez-Castro,Abdel Pérez-Lorenzana*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives the Hawking radiation spectrum for no-hair black holes without near-horizon metric approximations, using gauge invariance and separability of the Hamilton–Jacobi equation from hidden spacetime symmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the limitation of standard complex-path (Hamilton–Jacobi) methods that rely on approximating the metric near the horizon, especially for no-hair black holes; to provide a more rigorous, symmetry-based, and broadly applicable derivation of Hawking radiation.

Method: Leverages gauge invariance in quantum field theory and the separability of the Hamilton–Jacobi equation—enabled by hidden symmetries (e.g., Killing tensors) of stationary, axisymmetric black hole spacetimes—to compute tunneling amplitudes exactly, without horizon-approximation.

Result: Exact derivation of the thermal Hawking spectrum for scalar particles; elucidation of the effective (1+1)-dimensional blackbody behavior at the horizon; extension conditions for higher-spin fields; and a framework compatible with back-reaction and quantum-gravity corrections.

Conclusion: The Hawking spectrum arises fundamentally from spacetime symmetries and gauge structure—not from ad hoc near-horizon approximations—enabling a more robust, generalizable, and physically transparent semiclassical description of black hole radiation.

Abstract: Quantum tunneling of particles across a black hole (BH) horizon provides an intuitive interpretation of Hawking radiation (HR), yielding a conceptually simple derivation of the thermal spectrum within a semiclassical framework. However, for BHs obeying the no-hair theorem, usual implementations of the complex-path (Hamilton-Jacobi) method typically rely on approximating the metric near the BH horizon. In this work, we demonstrate that the Hawking spectrum can be derived without such approximations by exploiting gauge invariance in quantum field theory and the separability of the Hamilton-Jacobi (HJ) equation arising from hidden symmetries of these spacetimes. However, for BHs obeying the no-hair theorem, usual implementations of the complex-path (Hamilton--Jacobi) method typically rely on approximating the metric near the horizon. This perspective shows explicitly why the BH behaves as an effective $(1+1)$-dimensional blackbody at the event horizon, provides the conditions needed to extend the HR to higher-spin particles, and offers a framework that can naturally incorporate back-reaction and quantum-gravity effects.

</details>


### [15] [Holographic description of RN-like black hole in bumblebee gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25530)
*Jian-Peng Zhang,Bin Chen,Jiaju Zhang,Yu Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the holographic dual CFT description of an RN-like black hole in bumblebee gravity—a vector-tensor theory with spontaneous Lorentz violation—and finds that the dual 2D CFT has equal right- and left-moving central charges (c_R = c_L), a consequence of the spontaneous (rather than explicit) nature of Lorentz breaking; all thermodynamic and CFT quantities reduce smoothly to standard RN black hole results in the Lorentz-invariant limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a consistent holographic (AdS/CFT-like) description of black holes in bumblebee gravity—a theoretically motivated framework for spontaneous Lorentz violation—and to understand how Lorentz violation affects the structure of the dual CFT, particularly chirality and central charges.

Method: 1) Thermodynamic analysis using the first laws of both outer and inner horizons to derive CFT parameters (temperatures, chemical potentials, entropies); 2) Extraction of central charges from the mass-independent horizon entropy product; 3) Verification via Cardy formula reconstruction of horizon entropies; 4) Independent dynamical confirmation using hidden conformal symmetry of a charged scalar probe in the black hole background.

Result: The dual 2D CFT has c_R = c_L, equal temperatures and chemical potentials for left/right sectors, and all CFT data depend smoothly on the Lorentz-violating parameter; the result reduces to standard RN/CFT correspondence when Lorentz violation vanishes; crucially, no chiral asymmetry arises—unlike in explicitly Lorentz-breaking theories such as topologically massive gravity.

Conclusion: Spontaneous Lorentz violation, as realized in bumblebee gravity, preserves parity symmetry in the holographic dual, leading to a symmetric (non-chiral) 2D CFT with c_R = c_L; this distinguishes it fundamentally from theories with explicit Lorentz violation and supports the robustness of the entropy-product–based holographic dictionary under controlled symmetry breaking.

Abstract: We study the holographic description of the RN-like black hole in bumblebee gravity, which is a vector-tensor theory with spontaneous Lorentz violation. By combining the first laws of the outer and inner horizons, the thermodynamics method yields the right- and left-moving entropies, temperatures, and chemical potentials of the dual two-dimensional conformal field theory (CFT), determines the central charges from the mass-independent entropy product, and reproduces the outer and inner horizon entropies through the Cardy formula. An independent dynamical test based on the hidden conformal symmetry of a charged scalar probe confirms these results. A central outcome is the equality of the right- and left-moving central charges, $c_R=c_L$, which we trace to the spontaneous character of Lorentz breaking. Because the action of bumblebee gravity preserves diffeomorphism and local Lorentz invariance, and the symmetry is broken only by a vector vacuum expectation value, no chirally asymmetric structure arises in the dual theory, in sharp contrast to topologically massive gravity in $(2+1)$ dimensions. All CFT data depend smoothly on the Lorentz-violating parameter and reduce to the known RN results when Lorentz violation is switched off.

</details>


### [16] [Semi-Universality of $O(N)$ model on $S^1\times S^2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25622)
*Vinayak Mishra*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the thermal partition function of the critical large-N O(N) model on rotating S¹×S² using a Weyl-covariant hydrostatic expansion, identifies latitude-dependent saddle behavior induced by rotation, and matches high-temperature results with the near-light-speed (pp-wave) limit; low-temperature analysis uncovers interaction-dependent corrections from thermal-source-induced saddle response.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how rotation affects thermal properties of critical large-N conformal field theories, particularly the interplay between geometry (rotation), temperature, and quantum fluctuations in a controlled semi-universal regime.

Method: Weyl-covariant hydrostatic gradient expansion up to four derivatives; analysis of saddle-point latitude dependence governed by local temperature; matching between rotating sphere geometry and pp-wave limit near light-speed; direct computation of theory-dependent residue on the limiting geometry at both high and low temperatures.

Result: Partition function determined to four-derivative order in high-temperature and semi-universal limits; high-temperature result on S¹×S² matches the near-light-speed pp-wave limit; low-temperature expansion yields novel interaction-dependent corrections tied to saddle-point response to thermal sources.

Conclusion: Rotation induces nontrivial latitude-dependent saddle structure, and the thermal partition function exhibits distinct high- vs. low-temperature behaviors: universality dominates at high T, while interactions imprint measurable corrections at low T via saddle deformation.

Abstract: We study the thermal partition function of the critical large-$N$ $O(N)$ model on rotating $S^1\times S^2$. Rotation makes the saddle latitude dependent, with its leading profile governed by the local temperature. Using a Weyl-covariant hydrostatic expansion, we determine the partition function through four-derivative order in high-temperature and semi-universal limits. In the near-light-speed limit, the geometry around the equator reduces to a pp-wave and captures the expected semi-universal behavior of the partition function. We compute the corresponding theory-dependent residue directly on this limiting geometry, both at high and low temperatures. The high-temperature result agrees with the near-light-speed limit of the sphere calculation, while the low-temperature expansion reveals interaction-dependent corrections arising from the response of the saddle to the thermal source.

</details>


### [17] [Negative Rank Relations for Giant Graviton Correlators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25846)
*Qi Chen,Zhongjie Huang,Xinan Zhou*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes an exact duality between correlation functions of giant and dual giant gravitons in N=4 SYM, mediated by Young diagram transposition and a formal rank reflection N → −N, valid for free correlators and perturbative integrands at all loops.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the gauge/gravity correspondence for giant and dual giant gravitons—distinct D3-brane configurations in AdS5×S5 that map to Schur polynomial operators labeled by transposed Young diagrams—and uncover a hidden symmetry relating their correlation functions.

Method: Analyzes correlation functions of Schur polynomial operators using representation theory of U(N)/SU(N), exploits properties of characters and Weingarten calculus, applies Lagrangian insertion to extend results from free to weakly coupled regimes, and examines transposition symmetry across arbitrary numbers of external Schur operators.

Result: Proves an exact identity: correlation functions of Schur operators labeled by Young diagrams λ equal those labeled by transposed diagrams λ^T under formal replacement N → −N; holds exactly at finite N for free theory, extends to separated-point weak-coupling integrands at all loop orders, and generalizes to arbitrary numbers of Schur insertions with simultaneous transposition.

Conclusion: Transposition of Young diagrams encoding giant/dual-giant operators induces a precise, nontrivial duality in correlation functions governed by N → −N, revealing a deep algebraic structure unifying these seemingly distinct gravitational objects in N=4 SYM.

Abstract: We study correlation functions of giant and dual giant gravitons in $\mathcal N=4$ super Yang--Mills theory. Although the two objects correspond to rather different D3 brane configurations in $AdS_5\times S^5$, their gauge theory operators are Schur polynomials associated with transposed Young diagrams. We show that this transposition is accompanied by a formal rank reflection $N\to -N$ at the level of correlation functions. For both $U(N)$ and $SU(N)$, the relation is exact for free correlators at finite $N$, and, using Lagrangian insertion, extends to separated point weak coupling integrands at every loop order. More generally, it applies to any number of Schur polynomial external operators, provided all Young diagrams are transposed. We illustrate the relation in several examples and also discuss evidence for its extension beyond perturbative integrands.

</details>


### [18] [Numerical Sasaki--Einstein metrics and harmonic forms on del Pezzo links](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25857)
*Nakwoo Kim,Sejin Kim,Hoseob Shin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper numerically constructs the Sasaki–Einstein metric on the link of the cone over dP₂ and computes two primitive harmonic basic (1,1)-forms at its irregular volume-minimizing Reeb vector, using both polynomial and neural-network approximations to the Guillemin potential; achieves unprecedented Monge–Ampère residual (~10⁻¹³), validates against known Y^{p,q} and dP₃ metrics, and provides data for holographic IIB backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct explicit Sasaki–Einstein metrics on non-regular, irregular toric Sasaki manifolds—specifically the link of the dP₂ cone—where no closed-form solution exists, enabling applications in AdS/CFT correspondence (e.g., warped IIB supergravity backgrounds analogous to Klebanov–Tseytlin).

Method: Exploits toric symmetry: encodes the Sasaki–Einstein metric via a convex symplectic potential on a Delzant polygon; computes correction to the canonical Guillemin potential using (i) high-degree polynomial expansion and (ii) neural networks; enforces volume minimization (irregular Reeb vector) via extremization of the volume functional; validates via curvature invariants (against Y^{p,q}) and Laplacian eigenvalue spectra (against Doran et al.’s dP₃ result).

Result: Achieves mean-squared Monge–Ampère residual < 10⁻¹³ for the volume-minimizing irregular Reeb vector—orders of magnitude better than the ~10⁻² residual for non-minimizing (regular) case; produces high-accuracy numerical metric and two primitive harmonic basic (1,1)-forms; cross-validation confirms consistency with known analytic and numerical benchmarks.

Conclusion: High-precision numerical construction of irregular Sasaki–Einstein metrics is feasible using symplectic toric methods with polynomial ansätze; the resulting data enables concrete studies of dual non-conformal holographic field theories and warped supergravity solutions beyond the conifold.

Abstract: We numerically construct the Sasaki--Einstein metric on the link of the cone over the second del Pezzo surface $\mathrm{dP}_2$ and two primitive harmonic basic $(1,1)$-forms at its irregular volume-minimizing Reeb vector. Being toric, the metric in symplectic coordinates is encoded in a single convex function on a polygon. We approximate the correction to the canonical Guillemin potential in two ways: polynomial expansion and neural networks. The polynomial fit achieves a held-out mean-squared Monge--Ampère residual below $10^{-13}$, in contrast to the $10^{-2}$ plateau for the non-volume-minimizing regular Reeb vector. We validate our method against closed-form metrics of $Y^{p,q}$ using curvature invariants, and also against the numerical result of Doran et al. (2007) for the Kähler--Einstein metric of $\mathrm{dP}_3$ using the Laplacian spectrum of low torus-invariant modes. Our data for the metric and harmonic forms can be used to study warped non-conformal holographic IIB backgrounds, the analogues of the Klebanov--Tseytlin solution on the conifold.

</details>


### [19] [Quantum Love Numbers are Non-Zero](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25875)
*Asaad Elkhidir,Godwin Martin,Julio Parra-Martinez,M. V. S. Saketh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Quantum corrections generate non-zero static Love numbers for 4D Schwarzschild black holes, suppressed by ℓ_pl²/r_h², indicating classical vanishing stems from an accidental symmetry broken by quantum loops.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether the classical vanishing of static Love numbers for Schwarzschild black holes persists in quantum gravity, and to identify the physical origin of this vanishing.

Method: Worldline effective field theory applied to compute leading quantum corrections to Compton amplitudes for massless scalars and photons scattering off a Schwarzschild black hole at first post-Minkowskian order; renormalization of logarithmic divergences yields running Love numbers.

Result: Logarithmic UV divergences appear in the Compton amplitude, requiring renormalization of static Love numbers; RG running generates finite, Planck-suppressed Love numbers ∝ ℓ_pl²/r_h²; quadrupolar gravitational Love number estimated as λ ∼ M r_h² ℓ_pl².

Conclusion: Classical vanishing of static Love numbers is not fundamental but results from an accidental symmetry of static GR, broken by quantum loops; quantum gravity generically induces non-zero, calculable Love numbers.

Abstract: The static Love numbers of four-dimensional Schwarzschild black holes vanish classically. We show that this is not true in the quantum theory. Working in the worldline effective field theory, we compute the leading quantum correction to the Compton amplitude for massless scalars and photons scattering off a Schwarzschild black hole at first post-Minkowskian order, and find logarithmic divergences associated with the renormalization of the static Love numbers. We conclude that renormalization-group running necessarily generates Love numbers suppressed by the square of the Planck length in units of the horizon radius, $\ell_{\rm pl}^{2}/r_{\rm h}^{2}$. This is consistent with the classical vanishing being a consequence of an accidental symmetry of static general relativity, broken by quantum loops. Extrapolated to gravitational perturbations, our results suggest that the quadrupolar static Love numbers of Schwarzschild are of size $λ\sim M r_{\rm h}^{2} \ell_{\rm pl}^{2}$.

</details>


### [20] [BRST quantization of the induced gauge theory for the Higgs-branch CFT${}_2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26571)
*Bogdan Stefański*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a BRST-quantized framework for Witten's induced gauge theory arising from the D1-D5 Higgs-branch 2D CFT, deriving induced propagators and verifying consistency conditions (Slavnov-Taylor identity, gauge-parameter independence) essential for perturbative computation of observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a consistent, perturbatively well-defined quantization scheme for Witten's induced gauge theory—where gauge fields emerge dynamically from integrating out hypermultiplets in the D1-D5 CFT₂—enabling reliable computation of physical observables.

Method: BRST quantization of the induced gauge theory; derivation of induced propagators; verification of Slavnov-Taylor identity for the induced Aφ² vertex; analysis of ghost contributions and gauge-parameter dependence of correlators; use of a specified regularization prescription.

Result: Ghost contributions enter only at subleading order; the induced Aφ² vertex satisfies the Slavnov-Taylor identity; gauge-invariant correlators are independent of the gauge parameter; a consistent perturbative framework for observables is established.

Conclusion: The BRST-quantized formulation with the chosen regularization provides a robust, gauge-covariant perturbative foundation for computing physical quantities in Witten's induced gauge theory from the D1-D5 Higgs-branch CFT₂.

Abstract: We study Witten's induced gauge theory associated with the D1-D5 Higgs-branch CFT${}_2$, in which the non-dynamical vector multiplet becomes reanimated by integrating out the fundamental hypermultiplets. We propose a BRST quantisation of this theory and derive its induced propagators. We show that the ghost sector first contributes at subleading order, that the induced $Aφ^2$ vertex satisfies the Slavnov-Taylor identity and that gauge-invariant correlators are independent of the gauge parameter. Together with our chosen regularization prescription, this gives a perturbative framework for computing observables in the induced theory.

</details>


### [21] [Density-driven reversal of flavour-entanglement anisotropy in the D3-D7 holographic conductor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26641)
*Fuminori Okabayashi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates how a constant DC electric field affects spatial entanglement entropy in a strongly coupled 3+1D conductor (holographically modeled by the D3-D7 system), focusing on probe-flavour contributions. It identifies a density-driven sign reversal in the anisotropic entropy response—linked to radial competition in the Dirac-Born-Infeld stress tensor—and confirms it via mutual information; the reversal is smooth, not a phase transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how external electric fields influence quantum entanglement structure in strongly coupled, finite-density conductors—beyond equilibrium thermodynamics—using holography as a non-perturbative tool.

Method: Holographic computation using the D3-D7 brane system at finite temperature and charge density; evaluation of equal-time spatial entanglement entropy for slab-shaped subregions aligned parallel vs. transverse to a constant DC electric field; analysis of probe-flavour (quark) contribution via DBI action; extraction of anisotropic response via entropy difference and mutual information.

Result: A sign reversal in the field-induced anisotropic entanglement entropy correction, controlled by charge density; crossover density increases with electric field strength and decreases with strip width; mutual information confirms same reversal; origin traced to competing radial components in the DBI stress tensor anisotropy.

Conclusion: The sign reversal reflects a smooth, homogeneous, spatially traceless quantum response to the electric field—distinct from thermodynamic phase transitions—and reveals intrinsic anisotropy in entanglement structure driven by charge density and field strength.

Abstract: We study the leading probe-flavour contribution to equal-time spatial entanglement entropy in a homogeneous 3+1-dimensional strongly coupled conductor at finite temperature and charge density, realised holographically by the D3-D7 system and driven by a constant DC electric field. We compare slab-shaped subregions that are extended in two spatial directions and have finite width parallel or transverse to the field. The difference between the corresponding entropy corrections isolates a homogeneous, spatially traceless response and exhibits a density-driven sign reversal. Over the investigated parameter range, the crossover density increases with the electric-field magnitude and decreases with the strip width. Mutual information shows the same qualitative reversal and provides an ultraviolet-finite check. We trace the effect to a radial sign competition in the full Dirac-Born-Infeld stress anisotropy. The reversal is a smooth response crossover rather than a thermodynamic phase transition.

</details>


### [22] [Quasinormal modes and quantum black hole interiors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26724)
*Chiara Coviello,Ansh Gupta,Robie A. Hennigar,Kai Shi,Andrew Svesko*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes asymptotic quasinormal modes (QNMs) of (2+1)-dimensional quantum black holes using complex analysis to probe interior singularity structure; it derives a universal linear-in-n QNM spectrum, links the offset to the singularity’s scaling exponent, extracts metric scaling near the singularity from subleading QNM corrections, and identifies oscillatory QNMs in the crossover region of charged black holes as a possible inner-horizon signature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To use asymptotic QNMs—highly sensitive to black hole interior geometry—as probes of quantum singularity structure in semi-classical black hole solutions, particularly to distinguish between spacelike/timelike singularities and detect non-perturbative quantum effects near singularities.

Method: Analytic continuation of the radial coordinate into the complex plane; application of complex WKB/stokes line analysis to compute leading-order and subleading asymptotic QNMs; reconstruction of singularity scaling exponents from the offset and metric function behavior from subleading corrections; numerical confirmation of spectral form ω = offset + n × gap.

Result: 1) Asymptotic QNMs universally obey ω = offset + n × gap; 2) The offset encodes the singularity scaling exponent under a general Stokes topology assumption; 3) Subleading QNM corrections robustly determine the metric function’s near-singularity scaling; 4) Oscillatory QNM behavior emerges in the charge–mass crossover regime of charged quantum black holes, suggesting inner-horizon imprint.

Conclusion: Asymptotic QNMs serve as precise, non-perturbative probes of quantum black hole interiors: they encode singularity type, Kasner transition physics, and inner-horizon structure—demonstrating that gravitational wave spectroscopy can access Planck-scale geometry.

Abstract: Quasinormal modes (QNMs) of black hole perturbations in the large overtone limit, namely, asymptotic QNMs, are highly sensitive to a black hole's interior geometry. We probe the singularity structure of a class of exact (2+1)-dimensional quantum black hole solutions to semi-classical gravity by computing their asymptotic QNM spectra. We do this using methods of complex analysis in which the radial coordinate is analytically continued to the complex plane. The leading order QNM frequencies all fit the generic form $ω=(\text{offset})+n(\text{gap})$, for overtone number $n$, which we also confirm numerically. Under a general assumption about the global Stokes topology of the complexified radial coordinate, we show how to reconstruct the scaling exponent of more general (spacelike or timelike) black hole singularities from the offset. We then compute subleading corrections to the asymptotic QNMs, from which we provide a robust method for extracting the scaling of the metric function near the singularity. Our findings exemplify the transition between "Kasner eons", successive regimes encountered on approach to a spacelike singularity, as non-perturbative quantum effects become dominant. For charged quantum black holes, the asymptotic QNMs exhibit a crossover between two regimes in which the neutral and charge contributions to the metric function dominate, respectively. Within the crossover region, the QNMs develop an oscillatory behavior that may be a signature of the inner horizon.

</details>

### [23] [System-size dependence of bottomonium suppression from Pb-Pb to light-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25182)
*Nora Brambilla,Tom Magorsch,Antonio Vairo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper applies an open-quantum-system transport framework to study bottomonium suppression across Pb-Pb, O-O, and Ne-Ne collisions, finding consistent temperature-dependent transport coefficients across system sizes—supporting a common QGP-origin for suppression in deconfined matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether bottomonium suppression exhibits system-size dependence and whether it arises from a common microscopic mechanism (i.e., QGP-induced decoherence) across collision systems of vastly different sizes, minimizing cold nuclear matter effects.

Method: Uses QTraj to solve the next-to-leading-order pNRQCD Lindblad equation in anisotropic hydrodynamic backgrounds; retains microscopic transport coefficients from prior Pb-Pb fits; computes integrated Υ double ratios for Pb-Pb, O-O, and Ne-Ne collisions.

Result: Reproduces CMS/LHCb O-O suppression data—including sequential hierarchy and magnitude; quantifies sensitivity to transport coefficients and termination temperature; Ne-Ne shows marginally stronger suppression than predicted, within large experimental uncertainties.

Conclusion: Bottomonium suppression across Pb-Pb, O-O, and Ne-Ne is consistently described by the same temperature-dependent transport coefficients, indicating a universal QGP-driven mechanism largely insensitive to system size—provided cold nuclear matter effects are subdominant.

Abstract: We test the system-size dependence of bottomonium suppression using the same open-quantum-system transport framework for Pb-Pb, O-O, and Ne-Ne collisions. The microscopic transport coefficients are retained from previous Pb-Pb analyses. Using QTraj, we solve the next-to-leading-order pNRQCD Lindblad equation in anisotropic hydrodynamic backgrounds and calculate integrated $Υ$ double ratios. The QGP-induced evolution reproduces the observed sequential hierarchy and overall magnitude of the O-O suppression measured by CMS and LHCb. We quantify the sensitivity to both transport coefficients and to the termination temperature of the in-medium evolution. The Ne-Ne measurement favors somewhat stronger suppression than predicted, although the present experimental uncertainty is large. The extension from Pb-Pb to light ions supports, at least for observables less sensitive to cold nuclear matter effects, a common microscopic origin of bottomonium suppression in deconfined matter, characterized by the same temperature-dependent transport coefficients despite the large change in collision-system size.

</details>


### [24] [Elastic toroidal vector dark matter through a dark photon in the LUX-ZEPLIN high recoil window](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25114)
*Imtiaz Khan,Salvatore Capozziello,G. Mustafa,Farkhod Botirov,Ahmadjon Abdujabbarov,Farruh Atamurotov,Phongpichit Channuie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a complex vector dark matter model with a toroidal electromagnetic moment to explain a single nuclear recoil event observed by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment at ~248 keV, showing that elastic scattering via dimension-six operators can produce a spectral shape consistent with the data, especially through a correlated nonrelativistic reduction of operators O₁₇ and O₂₀; it constructs a renormalizable SU(2)_X × U(1)_D model with Z₃ stabilization and CP-violating messengers, predicts distinctive recoil spectrum ratios, isotope dependence (xenon vs. argon), absence of tensor contribution in spin-zero targets, and annual modulation—enabling multiple correlated experimental tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the single 248 keV nuclear recoil candidate reported by LZ (2026 search) within a theoretically consistent and testable dark matter framework beyond standard WIMP paradigms, specifically addressing spectral shape, isotope sensitivity, and operator structure.

Method: Nonrelativistic effective field theory (NREFT) reduction of dimension-six toroidal electromagnetic moment interactions for complex vector DM; construction of a renormalizable UV-complete model based on SU(2)_X × U(1)_D gauge symmetry with Z₃ residual stabilization and vectorlike CP-violating messengers; computation of differential nuclear recoil spectra for natural Xe and Ar using form factors and response functions; analysis of spectral ratios (e.g., R(248)/R(50), high/low energy interval ratio), isotope fraction dependence, spin-tensor operator suppression in spin-zero nuclei, and annual modulation amplitude.

Result: The model predicts a broad recoil maximum near 205 keV in xenon, retaining 80–85% of its rate at 248 keV; benchmark parameter choice (m_X = 0.998 TeV, m_A′ = 0.450 GeV) yields R(248)/R(50) = 1.43 (GCN) and 1.21 (JJ55), high-to-low energy response ratio ≈ 0.46, >99.9% odd-A Xe fraction dominance, vanishing tensor contribution for spin-zero argon, and few-percent annual modulation; all features are interlinked and falsifiable.

Conclusion: The observed LZ recoil is compatible with elastic scattering of complex vector dark matter carrying a toroidal electromagnetic moment, realized in a renormalizable gauge model; the correlated spectral, isotopic, target-dependent, temporal, and mediator-mass signatures provide a rich set of predictions for cross-experiment validation—distinguishing this tensor-mediated scenario from conventional spin-independent or spin-dependent WIMPs.

Abstract: The 2026 LUX-ZEPLIN search reports one nuclear recoil candidate at $248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}~\mathrm{keV}$ in an extended window reaching about $270~\mathrm{keV}$. We consider elastic scattering of neutral complex vector dark matter through its dimension-six toroidal electromagnetic moment and find appreciable spectral weight in this recoil region. The nonrelativistic reduction correlates $\mathcal O_{17}$ and $\mathcal O_{20}$, relating a coherent charge branch to a transverse spin tensor branch. For $m_X=1~\mathrm{TeV}$, natural xenon exhibits a broad high recoil maximum at $204.8$ to $206.6~\mathrm{keV}$ and retains $80.4$ to $85.4\%$ of this maximum at $248~\mathrm{keV}$. Resolving the interaction through a dark photon relates the high-to-low recoil ratio to the mediator mass. A renormalizable $SU(2)_X\times U(1)_D$ realization stabilizes the complex vector by a residual $Z_3$ gauge symmetry and generates the toroidal current through vectorlike messengers carrying a physical CP phase. For a benchmark with $m_X=0.998~\mathrm{TeV}$ and $m_{A'}=0.450~\mathrm{GeV}$, $R(248)/R(50)=1.43$ for GCN and $1.21$ for JJ55, while the odd $A$ xenon fraction remains above $99.9\%$. The response level ratio between the $350$ to $590~\mathrm{keV}$ and $100$ to $270~\mathrm{keV}$ intervals is about $0.46$ at this benchmark. The tensor branch is absent for spin zero argon, whereas annual modulation remains at the few percent level. The resulting spectral, isotope, target, timing, mediator, and high energy dependences give correlated tests of an elastic tensor interpretation of the LZ recoil.

</details>


### [25] [Cosmic Birefringence and Axiogenesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25202)
*Raymond T. Co,Lawrence J. Hall,Keisuke Harigaya,Taegyu Lee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that cosmic birefringence (a parity-violating CMB polarization rotation) and the baryon asymmetry can both originate from the rotation of an axion-like field, with testable predictions including MeV-scale reheating, gravitational-wave signatures, and enhanced angular momentum via an extended Affleck-Dine mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To jointly explain two outstanding anomalies—the 2–4σ CMB birefringence hint (β ≈ 0.3°) and the observed baryon asymmetry—within a single, minimal, falsifiable physics framework beyond the Standard Model.

Method: Theoretical modeling combining axion-like field dynamics, Affleck-Dine baryogenesis, and cosmological evolution; includes analysis of field rotation, equation-of-state transitions (matter/kination domination), reheating temperature constraints, and associated gravitational-wave production.

Result: A minimal axion-like field rotation model simultaneously yields positive β ≈ 0.3° (matching CMB data) and correct baryon asymmetry; predicts matter/kination domination ending at T ~ 3–50 MeV, enabling astrophysical tests and distinctive stochastic gravitational-wave backgrounds.

Conclusion: Cosmic birefringence and baryogenesis can share a common origin in axion-like field rotation; the model is predictive, falsifiable, and connects CMB anomalies to early-universe particle physics and GW observables.

Abstract: Recent analyses of Planck and ACT CMB data report a $2\text{-}4σ$ hint of isotropic cosmic birefringence ($β\approx 0.3^\circ$), corresponding to a parity-violating rotation of polarization planes that points to potential new physics. We show that both the hint of cosmic birefringence and the observed baryon asymmetry can be simultaneously explained by the rotation of an axion-like field. The minimal setup predicts a positive birefringence angle, consistent with the CMB hint, and matter and kination domination ending around a temperature of $\mathcal O(3\text{-}50)$ MeV, making the model falsifiable by astrophysical probes and potentially giving rise to characteristic gravitational-wave signals. We also introduce a mechanism that can exponentially enhance the angular momentum of the rotation generated by the Affleck-Dine mechanism.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve precision in extracting non-perturbative QCD parameters governing proton transverse structure from high-precision LHC boson production data, particularly where low-pT kinematics dominate and systematic correlations are significant.

Result: Correlated uncertainties in low-pT bins significantly bias non-perturbative parameter extractions; forward-region measurements (LHCb) exhibit stronger sensitivity to large-x PDFs and photon-induced backgrounds than central ones (CMS); mismatches arise between data-driven extractions and underlying-event tunes in parton-shower MCs.

Conclusion: Robust determination of non-perturbative QCD inputs requires explicit treatment of inter-bin correlations and region-specific theoretical systematics—especially for forward LHC measurements—and highlights tensions with current Monte Carlo underlying-event modeling.

Abstract: We investigate the determination of non-perturbative QCD contributions, associated with proton's transverse structure, from high-precision experimental data for electroweak boson production at the LHC. We demonstrate that these determinations are strongly affected by correlated uncertainties across transverse-momentum bins in the region of low boson's transverse momenta. We illustrate our results through a detailed analysis of LHCb measurements of $Z$-boson spectra at forward rapidities, and a comparison with CMS measurements at central rapidities. We further examine the influence on the extraction of the non-perturbative parameters from final-state electromagnetic radiation, photon-induced background, large-$x$ contributions to parton distributions in the forward region, matching to perturbative next-to-leading order. We comment on the comparison of our results with those from the underlying-event tuning of parton-shower Monte Carlo event generators.

</details>


### [27] [Limitations of quantum tomography in Higgs-boson decays to four leptons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25217)
*Morgan Del Gratta,Fabio Maltoni,Davide Pagani,Giovanni Pelliccioli*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a fundamental limitation in two-qutrit quantum tomography applied to Higgs-boson decays to four charged leptons: standard reconstruction fails to yield a positive semidefinite density operator at next-to-leading order due to interference between photon- and Z-mediated off-shell dilepton contributions, invalidating the assumption of universal decay analysers. However, the rank-two angular sector remains stable and usable to obtain a robust lower bound on squared concurrence—enabling reliable entanglement detection beyond leading order.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the reliability of quantum tomography for entanglement detection in Higgs → 4ℓ decays beyond leading order, where radiative corrections and interference effects may compromise positivity of the reconstructed density operator.

Method: Analytical quantum information analysis combined with perturbative QFT (specifically NLO electroweak corrections), examining coherence and factorization properties of photon- vs. Z-mediated off-shell dilepton amplitudes in the Higgs decay chain; identifies validity conditions for spin-analysing operators and isolates the perturbatively stable angular subspace.

Result: Standard two-qutrit tomography yields non-positive-semidefinite operators at NLO due to coherent γ/Z interference breaking universality of decay analysers; however, the rank-two angular sector remains intact and yields a stable, model-independent lower bound on squared concurrence.

Conclusion: Entanglement verification in H → 4ℓ is possible beyond leading order, but requires restricting tomography to the rank-two angular subspace—bypassing positivity failure while preserving sensitivity to entanglement via concurrence.

Abstract: We investigate the limitations of the two-qutrit quantum tomography in Higgs-boson decays to four charged leptons. At next-to-leading order, the standard reconstruction can yield an operator that is not positive semidefinite, even when radiatively improved spin-analysing powers are used. We trace this failure to the coherent combination of photon- and Z-mediated contributions to the off-shell dilepton system, whose distinct production and decay structures invalidate the assumed universal decay analysers. Nevertheless, the rank-two angular sector remains accessible and perturbatively stable, enabling a robust lower bound on the squared concurrence, and hence a reliable assessment of entanglement beyond leading order.

</details>


### [28] [A Unitary Fixed Point Away from Threshold: Momentum-Surface EFT for Strong Mixing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25219)
*Lorenzo De Ros,Javier Reig Navarro*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a non-perturbative regime in radiative capture for non-relativistic systems due to strong scattering-bound state mixing near a finite momentum k₀, leading to unitarity violation; it constructs a novel EFT around |k| = k₀ with linear dispersion to systematically resum contributions and restore unitarity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain how radiative capture can become non-perturbative despite weak underlying interactions—specifically, due to strong channel mixing near a finite momentum scale k₀ causing perturbative unitarity violation.
