Result: Demonstrates emergence of a unitary fixed point under RG flow; establishes a new EFT framework that restores unitarity via resummation and yields a systematic expansion for both elastic scattering and radiative capture cross sections.

Conclusion: Radiative capture exhibits intrinsic non-perturbative behavior tied to finite-momentum dynamics, necessitating an EFT built on a k₀-centered surface—not the usual zero-momentum limit—enabling consistent, unitary, and predictive description of fermionic systems at unitarity with linear dispersion.

Abstract: Radiative capture in a non-relativistic system can become non-perturbative even when the underlying transition interaction is weak. We demonstrate this mechanism in a simple quantum-mechanical two-channel model with a systematic power counting, where strong mixing between scattering and bound states develops in a narrow region around a finite momentum $k_0$, leading to a perturbative violation of unitarity. In this regime, the enhanced contributions factorize, reducing the dynamics to a free scattering state interacting through a strong local coupling. Under RG evolution in the factorization scale, this local interaction approaches a unitary fixed point, analogous to that of systems with an anomalously large scattering length. This motivates an EFT organized around the finite-momentum surface $|\mathbf{k}|=k_0$, describing fermions at unitarity with a linear dispersion relation. The resummation resulting from the EFT restores unitarity and provides a systematic expansion for both elastic scattering and radiative capture.

</details>


### [29] [Fast Surrogate for the Earth Matter Effect on Solar Neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25229)
*Saeed Ansarifard*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a deep-learning surrogate model—a residual convolutional neural network—to approximate the Earth matter effect on solar neutrinos, achieving ~2% pointwise relative accuracy and ~60× speed-up over numerical integration on a laptop CPU; it is modular, retrainable, and publicly released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accelerate computationally expensive numerical calculations of Earth matter effects in solar neutrino oscillations—critical for precision analyses—while retaining flexibility for new-physics scenarios and alternative simulation setups.

Method: A residual convolutional neural network conditioned on solar neutrino oscillation parameters, trained on high-fidelity numerical solutions computed over a dense grid of energy, zenith angle, and oscillation parameters; the model learns only the Earth-crossing propagation component.

Result: The surrogate achieves ~2% pointwise relative accuracy on the electron-neutrino survival probability and ~60× inference speed-up on a laptop CPU; it generalizes to new physics that preserve Earth propagation and supports retraining with alternate solvers or extended parameter spaces.

Conclusion: The surrogate provides a fast, accurate, modular, and open-source tool for solar neutrino analyses, enabling efficient exploration of parameter space and new-physics models without sacrificing fidelity in the Earth matter component.

Abstract: We present a deep-learning surrogate for the Earth matter effect on solar neutrinos. The model uses a residual convolutional network conditioned on the solar neutrino oscillation parameters and is trained on numerical solutions computed over a grid of parameter values. It predicts the Earth-induced transition probabilities across neutrino energy and zenith angle, providing a fast approximation to the direct numerical calculation. For the reference electron-neutrino survival probability, the surrogate achieves a pointwise relative accuracy of approximately $2\%$ and a speed-up of about a factor of 60 in a laptop-CPU test. Since the network learns only the Earth-crossing propagation, it can be applied without retraining to new-physics scenarios that leave this propagation unchanged. The surrogate can also be retrained using alternative numerical implementations or extended parameter sets, providing a flexible and computationally efficient approach for solar-neutrino analyses. The code and simulation data are publicly available at \href{https://github.com/AI-Driven-HEP/NuMatterSurrogate}{\faGithub}.

</details>


### [30] [Cosmological stasis and the coupled dark sector of the Dark Dimension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25230)
*Alexander Stewart*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes cosmological stasis in coupled dark matter–dark energy models—particularly the Dark Dimension—showing that exact stasis requires exponential evolution of couplings, while running (non-constant) slopes yield either exact stasis or quasi-stasis depending on integrability; it derives stationary solutions as finite-lifetime stasis epochs, proves stasis cannot drive acceleration or yield phantom total equation of state, and uses observational anchors and fifth-force bounds to exclude future stasis capture in geometric Dark Dimension realizations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether and how cosmological stasis—long periods of near-constant cosmic expansion rate and matter abundances—can arise naturally in theoretically motivated coupled dark sector models, especially the Dark Dimension, and to constrain such models using dynamics, observations (matter-radiation equality, present-day abundances), and experimental bounds (dark fifth forces).

Method: Treats dark matter, dark energy (scalar field), baryons, and radiation as an autonomous dynamical system; classifies stasis via slope conditions (exponential vs. running) on coupling trajectories; identifies constant-abundance solutions as stationary points of coupled quintessence; computes lifetimes and exits analytically; imposes observational anchors and fifth-force constraints to test viability and exclusion.

Result: Exact stasis requires exponential coupling slopes; running slopes yield exact or quasi-stasis depending on integrability; constant-abundance solutions are finite-lifetime stationary epochs; stasis regions are non-accelerating and never yield phantom total w; for the Dark Dimension, geometric potentials exclude future stasis capture under fifth-force bounds, but SM-brane dark matter production enables an additional stasis unreachable in standard scenarios; analytic parameter exclusions are derived, independent of initial conditions.

Conclusion: Cosmological stasis in coupled dark sector models is tightly constrained: it is dynamically transient rather than eternal, observationally bounded, and subject to robust analytic exclusions; the Dark Dimension admits novel stasis features only when incorporating SM-brane physics, highlighting the interplay between geometry, dynamics, and phenomenology in dark sector model building.

Abstract: We study cosmological stasis in coupled dark matter and dark energy models in which a scalar field drives the dark energy and sets the dark matter mass, with the Dark Dimension as the motivating realization. Treating dark matter, dark energy, baryons, and radiation as an autonomous dynamical system, we show that exact stasis requires exponential slopes along the trajectory, and that running slopes give exact stasis or a drifting quasi-stasis depending on whether the running is integrable. The constant abundance solutions are the stationary solutions of coupled quintessence, which we read as stasis epochs with computable lifetimes and exits. Anchoring the cosmology to the measured abundances at matter-radiation equality and today, we show that the stasis region cannot accelerate for any initial conditions, and that the total equation of state is never phantom although the effective dark energy of a $Λ$CDM fit can appear so. For the Dark Dimension, capture into stasis in the future is excluded for geometric sources of the potential once dark fifth-force bounds are imposed, and dark matter production from the Standard Model brane supports a further stasis which the standard scenario does not reach. The stasis solutions provide analytic exclusions of the parameter space which do not depend on initial conditions.

</details>


### [31] [Restoring minimal modular neutrino masses via Kähler $R$-parity violation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25592)
*Anjan S. Joshipura,Ketan M. Patel*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper revisits Feruglio's A₄ modular model for neutrino masses, showing that in local supersymmetry, the Kähler potential introduces R-parity-violating bilinear terms (εᵢLᵢHᵤ) and flavon–neutrino mixing, enabling successful fits to both normal and inverted neutrino mass orderings—unachievable in the original model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain why Feruglio's minimal A₄ modular model fails to reproduce observed neutrino mixing angles and mass ratios, and to explore whether local supersymmetry (i.e., supergravity) effects—specifically Kähler potential contributions—can resolve this shortcoming.

Method: Systematic analysis of the Kähler potential in the modular A₄ supergravity framework; derivation of R-parity-violating bilinear terms εᵢLᵢHᵤ from modular forms Y(τ) and their derivatives; inclusion of flavon fermion–neutrino mixing; computation of neutrino mass matrices via Weinberg operator and φ-induced seesaw; numerical fitting to oscillation and cosmological data.

Result: R-parity violation from the Kähler potential enables accurate fits to all measured neutrino parameters (θ₁₂, θ₂₃, θ₁₃, Δm²₂₁, |Δm²₃₁|) for both normal and inverted mass orderings; Weinberg-operator scenario yields ∑m_ν ≈ 0.06–0.12 eV (near cosmological bound), while seesaw scenario gives ∑m_ν ≪ 0.06 eV.

Conclusion: Local supersymmetry effects—previously neglected—are essential for phenomenological viability of minimal modular A₄ neutrino models; Kähler-driven R-parity violation and flavon–neutrino mixing provide a robust, predictive mechanism reconciling modular symmetry with full neutrino data.

Abstract: The possibility of describing neutrino masses through a single multiplet of modular forms in a modular symmetric framework is reanalysed. The minimal model achieving this is Feruglio's model based on modular $A_4$ symmetry, which contains a chiral flavon triplet $φ$, with all neutrino masses and mixing determined by a superfield triplet of modular forms $Y(τ)$. We point out that, in local supersymmetry, these superfields by themselves allow an additional $R$-parity-violating contribution to neutrino masses originating from the Kähler potential of the model. The $R$ violation manifests through a bilinear term $ε_i L_i H_u$ in the effective superpotential, with parameters $ε_i$ determined in terms of $Y(τ)$ and its derivatives. The Kähler potential also mixes neutrinos with the fermionic component of $φ$, leading to seesaw neutrino masses when these fermions are massive. Two separate cases are analysed, with neutrinos obtaining mass through the Weinberg operator or through the $φ$-induced seesaw. Unlike the original model, adding the $R$-violating contribution allows accurate reproduction of neutrino mixing angles and mass ratios for both the normal and inverted orderings in either case, with the Weinberg operator predicting a sum of neutrino masses close to the cosmological limit and the seesaw mechanism predicting ones comfortably below it.

</details>


### [32] [Electroweak phase transition triggered by a long-lived scalar and LHC searches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25601)
*Lei Wang,Xiaoting Zhaoa,Xiao-Fang Han,Hongxin Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a long-lived scalar particle S in the singlet scalar extension of the Standard Model that enables a strongly first-order electroweak phase transition (SFOEWPT); it identifies viable parameter space via finite-temperature effective potential analysis, predicts detectable gravitational-wave signals for LISA, and assesses LHC sensitivity—especially using far detectors like ANUBIS and MATHUSLA—for probing S in the mass range up to 250 GeV and mixing angles 10⁻¹⁰–10⁻⁵, while noting insensitivity below sinθ ≈ 10⁻¹¹.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize a strongly first-order electroweak phase transition (SFOEWPT)—a key ingredient for electroweak baryogenesis—and simultaneously accommodate a long-lived scalar S that evades prompt detection at colliders, leveraging small Higgs–singlet mixing as a natural mechanism for longevity.

Method: Finite-temperature effective potential analysis to determine SFOEWPT-allowed parameter space; computation of associated stochastic gravitational-wave spectra; collider phenomenology study including Higgs-mediated off-shell production (gg → h* → SS) at LHC; sensitivity projections for ATLAS main detector and multiple far detectors (ANUBIS, CODEX-b, FACET, FASER2, MoEDAL-MAPP1/2, MATHUSLA).

Result: Part of the SFOEWPT parameter space yields gravitational-wave signals detectable by LISA; combined LHC searches with ATLAS + far detectors (especially ANUBIS and MATHUSLA) cover m_S ≤ 250 GeV and 10⁻¹⁰ ≤ sinθ ≤ 10⁻⁵; no sensitivity remains for sinθ ≤ 10⁻¹¹.

Conclusion: A long-lived singlet scalar S can simultaneously enable SFOEWPT and yield observable signatures across gravitational-wave and collider experiments, but only within a narrow, experimentally accessible window of mixing angle and mass; weaker mixing renders S cosmologically stable and undetectable at current facilities.

Abstract: We investigate a long-lived scalar $S$ that triggers a strongly first-order electroweak phase transition (SFOEWPT) in the singlet scalar extension of standard model (SM). A sufficient small mixing angle between $S$ and the SM-like Higgs boson $h$ naturally leads to a long lifetime for $S$. We focus on the mass range $m_S>m_h/2$, where the dominant production channel at the LHC is the Higgs-mediated process $gg\to h^*\to SS$ with an off-shell $h$. By analyzing the finite-temperature effective potential, we identify the parameter space realizing an SFOEWPT and calculate the associated gravitational-wave spectra, finding that part of the parameter space can be probed by LISA. We further study the LHC sensitivity to the long-lived $S$ using the ATLAS detector and proposed far detectors, including ANUBIS, CODEX-b, FACET, FASER2, MoEDAL-MAPP1 and MAPP2, and MATHUSLA. The combined sensitivity of the main detector and far detectors, particularly ANUBIS and MATHUSLA, allows a large fraction of the SFOEWPT parameter space to be probed for $10^{-10}\leq \sinθ\leq 10^{-5}$, with $m_S$ up to $250~\mathrm{GeV}$. For $\sinθ\leq 10^{-11}$, however, the SFOEWPT parameter space becomes inaccessible to both the main detector and the far-detector searches.

</details>


### [33] [Dark Diffraction at LZ from a Screened Neutral Composite Baryon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25723)
*Hyunjoo Jung,Seong Chan Park*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes composite dark matter with elastic form factors to explain the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) 248 keV nuclear recoil candidate, showing that a four-constituent neutral baryon in an SU(4)_D × U(1)_D gauge theory can reproduce the signal via momentum-dependent form factor enhancement — not inelastic scattering or boosting — and predicts ~1.5 additional recoils beyond LZ’s current ROI.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous 248 keV nuclear-recoil-like event observed by LZ without resorting to fine-tuned mechanisms like inelastic dark matter or boosted populations, by leveraging the momentum-dependent elastic form factor of composite dark matter.

Method: Analyzes elastic scattering of composite dark matter (neutral baryons built from N dark quarks) off nuclei, modeling the charge form factor as a sum of Gaussians per charged constituent; derives recoil spectra, constrains constituent number via spectral shape and event rate in LZ’s signal window, and constructs a concrete confining SU(4)_D × U(1)_D gauge model yielding a neutral bosonic baryon of four dark quarks.

Result: Three-constituent form factors underproduce the LZ signal; four constituents suffice to match the observed event and yield a hard recoil spectrum; predicts ~1.5 true nuclear recoils above LZ’s current region of interest for a 200 GeV benchmark; form factor nodes produce target-dependent diffraction dips scaling as 1/m_A.

Conclusion: Composite dark matter with a four-constituent elastic form factor provides a viable, theoretically grounded explanation for the LZ candidate event, distinct from standard WIMP paradigms, and yields testable predictions for future direct detection experiments.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment recently reported a nuclear-recoil-like candidate at $E_R=248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}$ keV. We show that such an energetic recoil can arise from the elastic form factor of composite dark matter, without invoking an inelastic mass splitting or a boosted population. A neutral baryon of $N$ dark quarks can have a charge form factor that is suppressed at low momentum transfer but recovers at higher momentum transfer, providing a momentum-space band-pass filter. Modeling the form factor with one Gaussian per charged constituent species, we prove that two terms cannot produce a hard recoil spectrum and find that three yield too few events in the LZ signal window, while four suffice. A confining $SU(4)_D\times U(1)_D$ gauge theory provides a concrete realization through a neutral bosonic baryon made of four dark quarks. For a 200 GeV benchmark normalized to one accepted event, we predict approximately 1.5 true recoils beyond the current LZ region of interest. The form factor also produces diffraction nodes at fixed momentum transfers, placing them at recoil energies that scale as $1/m_A$ across target nuclei.

</details>


### [34] [Impact parameter and transverse charge densities of the pion and kaon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25780)
*Fernando Chandra,Parada T. P. Hutauruk,Terry Mart*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the spatial structure of pions and kaons using unpolarized vector GPDs at nonzero skewness within the covariant Nambu–Jona-Lasinio model, revealing how increasing skewness shifts valence-quark distributions to smaller x, suppresses central density, and broadens transverse profiles—offering tests of nonperturbative QCD and guidance for JLab and EIC experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe hadron spatial structure and the interplay between dynamical chiral symmetry breaking and SU(3)-flavor symmetry breaking via impact parameter and transverse charge densities.

Method: Calculation of unpolarized vector generalized parton distributions (GPDs) at nonzero skewness ξ for pion and kaon within the covariant Nambu–Jona-Lasinio model; ultraviolet divergences regulated using the Schwinger proper-time scheme.

Result: Increasing skewness ξ shifts valence-quark distributions toward smaller longitudinal momentum fraction x, reduces their magnitude at transverse impact parameter b_⊥ = 0, and broadens their transverse spatial profiles.

Conclusion: The results reveal key features of pion and kaon spatial structure, provide stringent benchmarks for nonperturbative QCD models, and are directly relevant to upcoming experimental measurements at Jefferson Lab and the Electron-Ion Collider.

Abstract: The impact parameter and transverse charge densities provide spatially resolved information on hadron structure and probe the interplay between dynamical chiral symmetry breaking and SU(3)-flavor symmetry breaking. We investigate the valence-quark distributions of the pion and kaon using their unpolarized vector GPDs at nonzero skewness $ξ$ within the covariant Nambu--Jona-Lasinio model, with ultraviolet divergences regulated by the Schwinger proper-time scheme. Increasing $ξ$ shifts the distributions toward smaller $x$, reduces their central magnitude at $b_\perp=0$, and broadens their transverse profiles. These results provide insight into the spatial structure of the pion and kaon and offer stringent tests of nonperturbative QCD dynamics, with relevance to forthcoming measurements at Jefferson Lab and the Electron-Ion Collider.

</details>


### [35] [Quasi-Gaussian Missing Higher Order Uncertainties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25824)

Main category: hep-ph

Result: Achievement of smooth, quasi-Gaussian posterior probability distributions—unlike the typically irregular, non-Gaussian shapes—enabling better compatibility with conventional uncertainty propagation methods in physics.

Conclusion: Careful choice of likelihood and priors in Bayesian models for missing higher-order uncertainties can produce well-behaved, quasi-Gaussian posteriors, improving practical utility without sacrificing statistical rigor.

Abstract: We review Bayesian models for estimating the uncertainty induced by missing higher orders in perturbative predictions. These models return probability distributions, which have peculiar shapes typically very different from the Gaussian distribution often assumed in physics analysis. In this work, we are able to obtain smooth probability distributions with quasi-Gaussians shapes by suitably modifying the likelihood and the priors of the models.

</details>


### [36] [Double integral representation in the invariant masses of a composite system and nonperturbative calculation of the asymptotics of light-meson electromagnetic form factors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25840)
*A. F. Krutov,E. V. Razueva,V. E. Troitsky*

Abstract: We obtain nonperturbative asymptotic expansions of the electromagnetic form factors of the pi and rho mesons at large spacelike momentum transfer, Q^2->\infty. The calculation is based on a double integral representation in the invariant masses of the composite system. The model parameters are kept at the values fixed in the authors' earlier description of meson electroweak properties. We show that, in the limit of vanishing constituent-quark mass, with the constituent quarks taken to be pointlike, the asymptotic ratio of the longitudinally polarized rho-meson to pion form factors in the helicity basis is consistent with the prediction of perturbative QCD (pQCD) within the adopted parameter ranges.

</details>


### [37] [Chiral anomaly and nucleon spin decomposition in the chiral quark soliton model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25867)
*Josuke Minamiguchi,Tomoya Uji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how the chiral anomaly affects nucleon spin decomposition in the chiral quark soliton model by introducing an anomaly-generated flavor-singlet pseudoscalar field; it finds that the anomaly enhances quark helicity contribution, but increasing its mass shifts weight toward orbital angular momentum—without qualitatively changing the overall spin decomposition.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Conclusion: The chiral anomaly introduces quantitative, but not qualitative, modifications to nucleon spin decomposition within the extended chiral quark soliton model.

Abstract: We investigate how the chiral anomaly influences the decomposition of nucleon spin between quark spin and orbital motion. We extend the chiral quark soliton model by including a flavor-singlet pseudoscalar field whose mass is generated by the chiral anomaly. Within this framework, we calculate the quark and antiquark helicity and orbital angular momentum distributions, accounting for the singlet field induced by the rotation of the soliton. We vary the anomaly-induced mass while keeping the remaining model parameters fixed to examine how the anomaly affects the spin decomposition. The induced field enhances the quark helicity contribution relative to the conventional pion-only model. Increasing the mass suppresses this enhancement and increases the orbital contribution, while the two contributions continue to account for the nucleon spin. This redistribution remains modest over the parameter values considered and does not qualitatively alter the spin decomposition of the conventional model.

</details>


### [38] [Big Bang Nucleosynthesis Confronts Domain Walls and First-Order Phase Transitions as the Pulsar Timing Array Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25950)
*Fatemeh Elahi,Diego Fortini,Yann Gouttenoire,Nicholas Leister,Pedro Schwaller*

TL;DR: This paper explores how baryon isocurvature fluctuations (S_B) generated by annihilating domain walls or first-order phase transitions—potentially linked to the nanohertz gravitational-wave background detected by pulsar timing arrays—affect deuterium abundance and can alleviate the observed deuterium tension in Big Bang Nucleosynthesis.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Abstract: Pulsar timing arrays (PTAs) have detected a nanohertz gravitational-wave background, often attributed to annihilating domain walls (DWs) or first-order phase transitions (FOPTs). These so-called baryon isocurvature fluctuations $S_B$ survive until Big Bang Nucleosynthesis, whose yields depend nonlinearly on it. Modest $S_B$ fluctuations can therefore alleviate the current deuterium tension in parts of the PTA preferred regions and therefore strengthen their interpretation. Overabundant DWs and too strong FOPTs overproduce $D/H$ and are ruled out.

Abstract: We investigate the effects of super-Gaussian pulse shapes on dynamically assisted pair production in spatially inhomogeneous electric fields with frequency chirping within the framework of the (1+1)-dimensional Dirac-Heisenberg-Wigner formalism. Our results are presented in terms of the reduced momentum distribution and the total particle yield, revealing that the interplay between pulse shaping, spatial inhomogeneity, frequency chirping, and dynamical assistance can significantly modify the pair-production dynamics. In particular, chirping substantially enhances the absolute pair-production yield and reshapes the momentum spectrum, with chirping of the weak component being particularly effective, while simultaneous chirping of both components produces the largest yields. The super-Gaussian order has a relatively modest influence on strong-field production, while increasing the flat-top character of the pulse enhances spectral structures and momentum redistribution in the weak and dynamically assisted regimes, particularly under chirping. Finally, we show that the relative phase between the two field components provides an additional control parameter, with its influence becoming more pronounced for larger spatial scales leading to significant changes in the momentum-space structure. Successful leptogenesis is realized for lightest pseudo-Dirac neutrino masses at the multi-TeV scale. The predicted cLFV branching ratios lie well below the current experimental upper limits while remaining within the sensitivity reach of next-generation experiments. These results establish the model as a viable and testable framework that links low-energy neutrino observables to TeV-scale leptogenesis and cLFV phenomenology.

These decays correspond to four different quark-level transitions: \(c\to d\), \(b\to c\), \(c\to s\), and \(b\to u\), allowing us to explore a broad range of flavor dynamics.\\ Using the Hard-Wall AdS/QCD model, we compute the relevant transition form factors as functions of the momentum transfer \(q^{2}\). We examine the electroweak phase transition of this mirror singlet extended world and we show that the phase transition is a strong first order phase transition, the bubble collision of which can be detectable by LISA, the BBO and the DECIGO gravitational wave experiments. Inelastic (endothermic) dark matter is a natural candidate: its kinematic threshold suppresses low-energy recoils and shifts the signal toward higher recoil energies. The two ratios vary in opposite directions with $δ$ and are simultaneously optimized at a unique splitting $δ^\star$. For $m_χ=1\,{\rm TeV}$, $δ^\star=339\,{\rm keV}$ with a combined Poisson tension of $\approx1.9σ$; over $500\,{\rm GeV}$--$3\,{\rm TeV}$, the tension at $δ^\star$ rises from $\lesssim1.3σ$ to $\approx2.5σ$. Additional suppression arises from the Helm form factor: $|F_{\rm Xe}^{\rm Helm}|^2\simeq3\times10^{-4}$ at $248\,{\rm keV}$, near its second diffraction zero.   <summary>Details</summary>

Abstract: A lowering of the post-inflation reheating temperature $T_{\rm RH}$ has attracted much attention as a means to facilitate obtaining the correct Dark Matter (DM) relic abundance. We take a relook at the paradigm under the assumption that the inflaton decay is essentially instantaneous, with the thermal bath coming into existence only at a relatively low temperature. We demonstrate that, contrary to the popular misconception, the relevant scale is not necessarily the DM mass itself, but the characteristic temperature at which the dominant DM production processes are thermally efficient. With $T_{\rm RH}$ setting the maximum temperature available for thermal production, and thereby determining the accessible stages of the production history, lowering $T_{\rm RH}$ tends to reduce the relic abundance. While this effect could, in principle, be offset by altering the couplings defining the theory, there exists a minimum $T_{\rm RH}$---determined by the mass spectrum---below which the relic abundance falls to minuscule levels, whether the mechanism be freeze-in or freeze-out.

</details>

TL;DR: This paper computes universal scaling exponents and functions for massless scalar field theories far from equilibrium using a large-N expansion to next-to-leading order, revealing distinct infrared scaling behaviors for energy vs. particle-number transport, with power-law tails f_S(p) ∼ 1/p^κ where κ = d+1 (energy) or κ = d (particle number) in d=3, demonstrating that relativistic and nonrelativistic theories belong to different universality classes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand universal far-from-equilibrium scaling behavior in massless scalar field theories, particularly how energy and effective particle number are transported to the infrared (low ω, low p), and to determine whether relativistic and nonrelativistic systems with linear dispersion share the same universality class.

Result: For d = 3 spatial dimensions: (a) energy transport yields κ = d + 1 = 4, i.e., f_S(p) ∼ 1/p⁴; (b) effective particle-number transport yields κ = d = 3, i.e., f_S(p) ∼ 1/p³; this difference proves relativistic and nonrelativistic linear-dispersion theories belong to distinct universality classes.

Abstract: We compute universal scaling exponents and scaling functions for massless scalar field theories far from equilibrium. Using a large-$N$ expansion to next-to-leading order, we investigate the nonperturbative infrared behaviour associated with the transport of a) energy or b) effective particle number towards low frequencies $ω$ and momenta $\bar p$. For massless dispersion $ω= \bar{p}$, we determine the scaling form of the distribution function whose universal power-law tail $f_S(\bar p) \sim 1/\bar{p}^κ$ is found to be a) $κ= d+1$ and b) $κ= d$ in $d=3$ spatial dimensions. Our results establish that relativistic and nonrelativistic theories do not belong to the same universality class in this case, which opens up new applications to far-from-equilibrium phenomena in high-energy and (quantum) many-body physics with linear dispersions.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain mild excesses in ATLAS/CMS soft two- and three-lepton searches (the 'golden channel') with compressed neutralino-chargino spectra, while satisfying all major phenomenological constraints including relic density and direct detection.

Abstract: The search for electroweak supersymmetric particles remains an important part of the LHC program since neutralinos and charginos with masses around a few hundred GeV are still compatible with the present constraints in compressed spectra. We study this configuration in the Minimal Supersymmetric Standard Model (MSSM) with the Generalized minimal Supergravity (GmSUGRA) boundary conditions, imposing the Higgs, flavor, collider, relic-density and direct-detection constraints relevant to the spectrum. For both signs of $μ$, the solutions compatible with the Planck experiment contain an almost pure bino $\widetildeχ_1^0$ with mass about $260$--$266$~GeV and a nearly degenerate wino-like $\widetildeχ_2^0/\widetildeχ_1^\pm$ pair near $280$~GeV. The resulting splitting, $Δm_{21}\simeq18.5$--$19.1$~GeV, lies in the mass-difference region probed by the ATLAS and CMS soft-lepton searches and places the $Z^{(*)}/W^{(*)}$ decays below the on-shell gauge-boson thresholds. The same compressed hierarchy keeps the wino states thermally populated during freeze-out, so bino-wino coannihilation yields a neutralino relic abundance consistent with the Planck experiment while the surviving solutions remain compatible with the direct-detection experimental bounds. Thus, the GmSUGRA boundary conditions provide a high-scale realization of the compressed bino-wino spectrum relevant to the LHC soft-lepton channel.

</details>

The selected parameter region lies near the $A_1$ resonance, with $m_{A_1}>2m_{\tildeχ_1^0}$, allowing both resonantly enhanced neutralino annihilation and invisible $A_1$ decays. After imposing theoretical and experimental constraints, we find surviving samples with cascade signal cross sections of approximately 0.16 fb at $\sqrt{s}=14~\mathrm{TeV}$ even when their relic-density-rescaled spin-independent scattering cross sections lie below the neutrino floor. A Monte Carlo analysis of a representative benchmark point yields an expected statistical significance of approximately $6.1σ$ at the high-luminosity LHC with an integrated luminosity of $3000~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$, neglecting systematic uncertainties. These results highlight the potential of the diphoton plus missing transverse momentum channel as a complementary probe of neutralino dark matter.

TL;DR: Proposes a pseudo-Dirac fermion dark matter model (20–100 GeV) with inelastic couplings via a baryon-number gauge boson V; explains both the Galactic Center Gamma-Ray Excess and LZ events via co-annihilation and exothermic downscattering.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously explain the Galactic Center Gamma-Ray Excess (GCE) and anomalous low-energy events in the LZ experiment using a unified dark matter framework.

Method: Constructs a pseudo-Dirac fermion DM model with two nearly degenerate mass eigenstates (χ₁, χ₂) coupled to a hadrophilic spin-1 mediator V (baryon-number gauge boson); computes thermal freeze-out abundances, co-annihilation (χᵢχᵢ → VV), stability of χ₂ via suppressed Z-mixing and sub-MeV mass splitting, and exothermic scattering χ₂ → χ₁ on xenon nuclei.

Result: The model reproduces the GCE spectrum and morphology for thermal-relic cross sections; for δ ∼ 1000 keV mass splitting, it also accounts for the LZ excess via exothermic nuclear scattering without violating cosmological or direct detection constraints.

Conclusion: A pseudo-Dirac DM scenario with baryonic mediation and controlled inelasticity provides a viable, testable unification of indirect (GCE) and direct (LZ) detection anomalies.

Abstract: We propose that the dark matter (DM) is a pseudo-Dirac fermion with a mass of approximately $20-100$ GeV that couples inelastically to the Standard Model through a hadrophilic spin-1 mediator, $V$, the gauge boson of spontaneously broken baryon number. The two DM eigenstates, $χ_{1}$ and $χ_2$, acquire approximately equal abundances after thermal freeze-out and are depleted through the processes $χ_1 χ_1 \to VV$ and $χ_2 χ_2 \to VV$. Although the heavier state is unstable, for a mass splitting below the electron-positron threshold and sufficiently small mass mixing with the $Z$ boson, its lifetime can exceed the age of the universe, allowing both states to remain approximately equally abundant in the Galactic halo. For a thermal-relic annihilation cross section, this model can produce a signal that is consistent with the observed features of the longstanding Galactic Center Gamma-Ray Excess (GCE). If $δ\sim 1000$ keV, the same parameter space that explains the GCE can also explain the LZ event through exothermic $χ_2 \to χ_1$ downscattering off xenon nuclei.

</details>


### [51] [Tachyonic particle production in strongly supercooled phase transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26767)
*Mateusz Kulejewski,Bogumila Swiezewska,Jorinde van de Vis*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates particle production during strongly supercooled cosmological phase transitions, where the scalar field rolls far below the potential minimum; tachyonic instabilities lead to efficient particle creation, altering gravitational-wave spectra, bubble dynamics, and potentially enabling primordial black hole formation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how post-transition scalar field evolution—particularly tachyonic particle production—affects energy budget partitioning (vs. gradient energy), gravitational-wave signatures, bubble expansion, and primordial black hole formation in strongly supercooled phase transitions.

Method: Theoretical analysis of scalar field dynamics in strongly supercooled cosmological phase transitions, focusing on mode functions with temporarily tachyonic effective masses; computation of particle production via Bogoliubov transformation or WKB-instanton methods; assessment of energy transfer to particles versus gradient energy; derivation of implications for gravitational-wave spectrum (bubble wall collisions, sound waves, turbulence), equation of state, bubble velocity, and PBH formation conditions.

Result: A significant fraction of phase-transition energy is converted to particles rather than gradient energy due to tachyonic amplification; this suppresses low-frequency GW signals from sound waves/turbulence, sources a new high-frequency GW component, reduces bubble wall velocity, softens the effective equation of state, and enhances conditions for primordial black hole formation.

Conclusion: Tachyonic particle production is a critical, previously underappreciated channel in supercooled phase transitions that qualitatively reshapes gravitational-wave phenomenology, hydrodynamics, and early-universe structure formation.

Abstract: In strongly supercooled cosmological phase transitions, which generically lead to strong, observable gravitational-wave backgrounds, the scalar field typically transitions to a value well below the minimum of its potential. We study particle production caused by the subsequent evolution of the field. Because some modes feature temporarily tachyonic masses, they can be efficiently produced, and a significant fraction of the energy density released in the phase transition can get converted to particles rather than gradient energy. This has consequences for the shape and amplitude of the gravitational-wave spectrum caused by the interactions in bubbles and sources a new, high-frequency signal. Moreover, it affects the bubble expansion velocity and equation of state, which can facilitate the formation of primordial black holes.

</details>


### [52] [Resonant neutrino flavor conversion within dark matter spikes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26773)
*P. S. Bhupal Dev,Elisa Gaido,Alejandro Ibarra,Yago Porto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how neutrino–dark matter interactions in dark matter spikes around supermassive black holes alter high-energy astrophysical neutrino flavor composition, showing measurable deviations from standard vacuum oscillations—especially at ~100 TeV—and proposes IceCube flavor measurements as a novel probe of such interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test for non-standard neutrino interactions with dark matter in extreme astrophysical environments—specifically, DM spikes near AGN central black holes—using high-energy neutrino flavor composition as a sensitive observable.

Method: Computes coherent forward scattering-induced flavor-dependent matter potentials for neutrinos traversing DM spikes; solves flavor evolution equations for pion-decay and muon-damped (pγ) sources; compares predicted Earth-frame flavor ratios with IceCube's flavor triangle (MESE) constraints.

Result: At ~100 TeV, DM-induced potentials compete with vacuum oscillations when |ε_α(n_χ − n_{arχ})| ∼ 10¹⁸ cm⁻³; muon-damped pγ predictions violate the 95% MESE contour if the DM potential dominates vacuum terms at production.

Conclusion: High-energy neutrino flavor measurements—particularly from IceCube—can constrain or detect neutrino–DM interactions in AGN environments, offering a new astrophysical probe beyond terrestrial experiments.

Abstract: We investigate how neutrino--dark matter (DM) interactions modify the flavor composition of high-energy neutrinos from active galactic nuclei (AGNs). Coherent forward scattering in a DM spike around the central supermassive black hole can generate a flavor-dependent potential, inducing flavor conversion as the neutrinos escape. We calculate the flavor composition at Earth for pion-decay and muon-damped sources and find substantial departures from vacuum-oscillation expectations. At neutrino energies around $100\,\mathrm{TeV}$, the matter potential begins to compete with vacuum oscillations when the coupling-weighted net DM number density reaches $|ε_α(n_χ-n_{\barχ})|\sim10^{18}\,\mathrm{cm}^{-3}$. We compare our predictions with IceCube flavor triangle measurements under the illustrative assumption that the diffuse flux originates from AGNs with common neutrino-production and DM-spike properties. Under this assumption, when the DM-induced potential acts on the muon flavor and dominates the vacuum terms at production, the $pγ$ muon-damped predictions lie outside the 95\% MESE contour. High-energy neutrino flavor measurements can therefore provide a novel probe of neutrino-DM interactions in astrophysical environments.

</details>

### [53] [Gradient Flow for the Jet Transport Coefficient: A Quenched Lattice Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25089)
*Hai-Tao Shu,Cheng Zhang*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the jet transport coefficient $\hat q$ in quenched lattice QCD using gradient flow, finding $\hat q/T^3 \simeq 1.1$ above $T_c$, with strong suppression below $T_c$, validating gradient flow for nonperturbative $\hat q$ extraction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain a nonperturbative, renormalization-scheme-independent determination of the jet transport coefficient $\hat q$ in thermal QCD plasma, especially given challenges in operator renormalization and mixing in full QCD.

Method: Applies the gradient-flow framework to compute the leading-twist contribution to $\hat q$ in quenched lattice QCD across $0.76T_c \leq T \leq 2.25T_c$; uses gradient-flow-defined energy-momentum tensor to extract entropy density $s/T^3$, and combines it with one-loop perturbative short-distance coefficient from OPE of $\hat q/T^3$.

Result: $\hat q/T^3$ is strongly suppressed below $T_c$, rises sharply across deconfinement, and plateaus at $\simeq 1.1$ above $T_c$; results are statistically consistent with prior quenched lattice studies using different renormalization, confirming gradient flow's reliability.

Conclusion: Gradient flow provides a robust, systematic framework for renormalizing and matching operators relevant to $\hat q$, essential for future extension to full QCD where quark-gluon operator mixing must be controlled.

Abstract: We apply the gradient-flow framework to determine the nonperturbative medium contribution to the jet transport coefficient $\hat q$ using quenched lattice QCD in the temperature range $0.76T_c \leq T \leq 2.25T_c$. The calculation focuses on the leading-twist contribution at leading order for an asymptotically energetic quark propagating through a thermal pure-gluon plasma. In the infinite-energy limit, the operator-product expansion expresses $\hat q/T^3$ in pure gauge theory as the product of a perturbative short-distance coefficient and the entropy density $s/T^3$. The latter is determined from the gradient-flow energy-momentum tensor at three lattice spacings for each temperature and the former is estimated perturbatively at one-loop order. We find that $\hat q/T^3$ is strongly suppressed below $T_c$, rises rapidly across the deconfinement transition, and remains approximately constant at $\hat q/T^3\simeq1.1$ throughout the deconfined temperature range studied. This is statistically consistent with the previous quenched lattice determination based on a different renormalization procedure, providing a nontrivial validation of the gradient-flow framework for the jet transport coefficient. The systematic treatment of operator renormalization and matching provided by gradient flow becomes particularly important in full QCD, because the need to renormalize quark operators and account for their mixing with the gluonic sector can likewise be addressed within the same framework.

</details>


### [54] [Experiment $\leftrightarrow$ lattice QCD: understanding high-temperature QCD matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25191)
*Bedangadas Mohanty*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper reviews the interplay between relativistic heavy-ion collision experiments and lattice QCD in probing QCD thermodynamics and phase structure, emphasizing challenges in connecting equilibrium lattice calculations to dynamical, finite-size collision systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge experimental observations from heavy-ion collisions with first-principles lattice QCD predictions for strongly interacting matter under extreme conditions, especially where non-perturbative effects dominate.

Method: Critical review and synthesis of recent theoretical (lattice QCD) and experimental (RHIC, LHC, FAIR, NICA, EIC) developments, focusing on equation of state, transport coefficients, conserved-charge fluctuations, and critical point search.

Result: Quantitative tests of QCD thermodynamics are now feasible across broader T and μ_B ranges; key observables like cumulants and hydrodynamic flow constrain lattice inputs; systematic limitations—finite volume, real-time dynamics, and baryon chemical potential reach—are identified.

Conclusion: A synergistic, multi-scale approach combining lattice QCD, phenomenological modeling, and diverse collider experiments is essential to map the QCD phase diagram and validate fundamental properties of quark-gluon plasma.

Abstract: Relativistic heavy-ion collisions provide a unique experimental opportunity to study strongly interacting matter at extreme temperature and density, while lattice quantum chromodynamics (QCD) offers a first-principles approach to the equilibrium properties of such matter in the non-perturbative regime. The interplay between experiment and lattice QCD has therefore become central to establishing the properties and phase structure of QCD matter. Selected areas where this connection is particularly informative are discussed, including the QCD equation of state and its role in hydrodynamic descriptions of heavy-ion collisions, transport properties of the quark-gluon plasma, conserved-charge fluctuations and their relation to experimental cumulants, and the ongoing search for a critical point in the QCD phase diagram. Particular attention is given to the limitations involved in confronting equilibrium lattice calculations with the finite, dynamical and experimentally constrained systems produced in heavy-ion collisions. Recent developments increasingly allow quantitative tests of QCD thermodynamics over an extended range of temperature and baryon chemical potential. The continuing experimental programmes at RHIC and the LHC, together with future measurements at FAIR, NICA and the Electron-Ion Collider, provide important opportunities for an increasingly close interplay between lattice QCD, phenomenology and experiment.

</details>

### [55] [Search for boosted vector boson scattering $W^{\pm}W^{\pm}H$ production in final states with same-sign leptons and $b$-jets in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}=$13 TeV with the ATLAS detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25153)
*ATLAS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a search for electroweak $W^\pm W^\pm Hjj$ production using the full ATLAS Run 2 dataset (140 fb⁻¹ at √s = 13 TeV), targeting same-sign dilepton + MET + boosted $H→bb$ final states to probe the $WWHH$ quartic coupling via $κ_{WW}$. No significant deviation from the SM is found; 95% CL limits exclude $κ_{WW} \notin (-2.1, 4.1)$ observed / $(-1.9, 3.9)$ expected.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To directly probe the electroweak quartic gauge-Higgs coupling $WWHH$, a key prediction of the Standard Model's electroweak symmetry breaking mechanism and highly sensitive to BSM physics; this process is inaccessible via triple-gauge or Higgs-strahlung channels.

Method: Analysis of full ATLAS Run 2 pp collision data (√s = 13 TeV, 140 fb⁻¹); event selection requires two same-sign leptons, large missing transverse momentum, and a boosted Higgs boson reconstructed as a large-radius jet with $b\bar{b}$ substructure; background estimation uses data-driven and simulation-based techniques; statistical interpretation employs profile likelihood ratio test with $κ_{WW}$ as the signal strength parameter.

Result: No significant excess over SM expectation observed; 95% CL upper limits on $σ(W^\pm W^\pm Hjj)$ set as function of $κ_{WW}$; observed (expected) exclusion: $κ_{WW} < -2.1$ or $κ_{WW} > 4.1$ ($-1.9$ or $3.9$).

Conclusion: The $W^\pm W^\pm Hjj$ process remains unobserved; current data constrain deviations in the $WWHH$ quartic coupling at the O(1) level, consistent with SM; sets strongest direct limit on $κ_{WW}$ to date.

Abstract: A search for the electroweak production of a Higgs boson in association with a same-sign $W$ boson pair and two forward jets ($W^{\pm}W^{\pm}Hjj$) is presented. The full Run 2 proton-proton collision dataset recorded at a center-of-mass energy of $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV by the ATLAS detector at the Large Hadron Collider is used, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 140 fb$^{-1}$. The search targets events where both $W$ bosons decay leptonically, yielding a final state with two same-sign charged leptons, missing transverse momentum, and a Higgs boson candidate. The high-momentum Higgs-boson candidate decaying into a $b\bar{b}$ pair is reconstructed as a single large-radius jet. This process provides direct sensitivity to the quartic $WWHH$ interaction. Possible deviations from the Standard Model prediction are parameterized through the coupling modifier $κ_{WW}$, where $κ_{WW}=1$ corresponds to the Standard Model. No significant excess over the Standard Model prediction is observed. Upper limits at the 95% confidence level are set on the $W^{\pm}W^{\pm}Hjj$ production cross-section as a function of $κ_{WW}$. Observed (expected) values of $κ_{WW}$ outside the interval $-2.1 < κ_{WW} < 4.1$ ($-1.9 < κ_{WW} < 3.9$) are excluded.

</details>

Zhu,K. Zhu,K. J. Zhu,K. S. Zhu,L. X. Zhu,Lin Zhu,S. H. Zhu,T. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,J. H. Zou*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Analysis of ΛΛ̄ system in ψ(3686) radiative decays reveals threshold enhancement dominated by ^1S_0 and ^3P_0 partial waves, and discovers a new pseudoscalar resonance η(2600), the heaviest light meson observed so far.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate baryon-antibaryon threshold dynamics and explore pseudoscalar light hadron spectroscopy via ψ(3686) radiative decays.

Method: Model-independent partial wave analysis of ΛΛ̄ system using 2712.4 ± 14.3 million ψ(3686) events collected with the BESIII detector.

Result: Observation of a significant threshold enhancement in ΛΛ̄ system dominated by ^1S_0 (J^{PC} = 0^{-+}) and ^3P_0 (J^{PC} = 0^{++}) partial waves; discovery of a new pseudoscalar resonance η(2600) in ^1S_0 wave, mass consistent with X(2600).

Conclusion: The results provide new insights into baryon-antibaryon threshold behavior and extend the spectrum of light pseudoscalar mesons, establishing η(2600) as the heaviest light meson observed to date.

Abstract: Using $2712.4 \pm 14.3$ million $ψ(3686)$ events collected with the BESIII detector, the $Λ\barΛ$ system produced in $ψ(3686)$ radiative decays is studied. A model-independent partial wave analysis reveals a significant threshold enhancement structure dominated by the $^1S_0$ and $^3P_0$ partial waves, corresponding to $J^{PC} = 0^{-+}$ and $0^{++}$, respectively. In addition, a new pseudoscalar resonance, designated as $η(2600)$, is observed in the $^1S_0$ partial wave with a mass value consistent with the previously reported $X(2600)$ state, which represents the heaviest light meson observed to date. These results enhance our understanding of baryon-antibaryon threshold dynamics and the pseudoscalar light hadron spectroscopy.

</details>


### [57] [Discovery of an unexpectedly light and narrow beauty-strange state](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25178)

TL;DR: LHCb reports the first observation of a nonconventional single-beauty hadron—a narrow resonance in the beauty-strange sector with >7σ significance—challenging the conventional quark model and offering new insights into chiral dynamics and heavy-quark spin symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the universality of exotic hadron formation mechanisms beyond the charm sector, particularly in the theoretically cleaner open-beauty system where chiral dynamics and heavy-quark symmetries can be more rigorously probed.

Method: Analysis of proton-proton collision data from the LHCb experiment at CERN, employing invariant mass spectroscopy and statistical significance assessment to identify and characterize a new resonance in the beauty-strange sector.

Result: Discovery of a narrow-width resonance with mass significantly below conventional quark-model predictions and global significance >7σ, unambiguously establishing it as a nonconventional (exotic) single-beauty hadron.

Conclusion: This observation confirms the existence of exotic hadrons in the bottom-quark sector, demonstrating that multi-quark or molecular configurations are not limited to charm physics and providing direct evidence for the role of chiral dynamics and heavy-quark spin symmetry in exotic hadron formation.

Abstract: As the essential building blocks of visible matter, hadrons have traditionally been classified by the quark model as mesons composed of quark-antiquark pairs and baryons built from three valence quarks. Yet, this classical picture does not account for the intricate chiral dynamics of the strong force and the emergence of exotic multi-quark hadrons. While experimental evidence for such unconventional dynamics has surfaced in the charm sector, the open-beauty system remains the long-sought frontier for testing the universality of these mechanisms. Here the observation of a new resonance in the beauty-strange sector with a global significance exceeding seven standard deviations is reported using proton-proton collision data recorded by the Large Hadron Collider beauty (LHCb) experiment at the European Organization for Nuclear Research (CERN). It exhibits a narrow natural width and a substantial mass deficit compared to the conventional quark model predictions. This result marks the first observation of a nonconventional single-beauty hadron, providing crucial insights into the chiral dynamics of the strong interaction and heavy-quark spin symmetry in the exotic domain.

</details>


### [58] [Fast Timing at Colliders: From Scintillator Hodoscopes to MicroPattern Arrays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25358)
*Sebastian N. White*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews charged particle timing techniques at colliders, emphasizing dual-threshold timing as a key solution for amplitude walk correction in high-radiation, high-luminosity environments like the HL-LHC; it demonstrates the technique using the TOFHIR2C ASIC in CMS.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the growing challenges of precise time measurement under high-luminosity and high-radiation conditions—particularly amplitude walk correction and dynamic calibration needs—in collider experiments.

Method: Analysis and demonstration of dual-threshold timing using the TOFHIR2C ASIC, configured to capture signals at two thresholds to mitigate amplitude-dependent time walk.

Result: Dual-threshold timing significantly simplifies amplitude walk correction—reducing its time span by an order of magnitude relative to target resolution—and enables robust timing performance despite varying radiation-induced operating conditions.

Conclusion: Dual-threshold timing is a critical enabler for precision timing in the HL-LHC era; its implementation in ASICs like TOFHIR2C validates its suitability for next-generation collider detectors requiring adaptive, radiation-tolerant calibration.

Abstract: We give a select overview of charged particle timing at colliders. Roughly speaking, three eras capture the evolution of timing arrays. While luminosity and multiplicity dictate the detector technologies, there is also a need to review the various approaches to signal capture and processing, and their role in changing conditions for calibration.
  For the current era--the High-Luminosity LHC upgrades--the technique of dual-threshold timing can significantly simplify the crucial correction for amplitude walk.This correction often spans a time range an order of magnitude larger than the target resolution. Furthermore, because operating conditions vary in the high radiation environment, calibration can no longer be thought of as a one-time procedure.
  The TOFHIR2C ASIC designed for a CMS upgrade can be configured to provide dual threshold timing. In this report, we use this capability to demonstrate the power of this technique suitable for our new era.}

</details>

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a high-precision measurement of the lifetime ratio r_τ = τ(Ω_b⁻)/τ(Ξ_b⁻) using LHCb Run 2 data, yielding r_τ = 1.109 ± 0.055 ± 0.010 and τ(Ω_b⁻) = 1.751 ± 0.089 ± 0.022 ps — doubling the precision of the previous world average and agreeing with heavy quark expansion predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the precision of the Ω_b⁻ baryon lifetime, which is crucial for testing theoretical models (e.g., heavy quark expansion) and probing QCD dynamics in b-baryon decays, where prior measurements suffered from large uncertainties.

Method: Lifetime ratio r_τ is measured using two decay channels: (Ω_b⁻, Ξ_b⁻) → (Ω_c⁰π⁻, Ξ_c⁰π⁻) and (Ω_b⁻, Ξ_b⁻) → (J/ψΩ⁻, J/ψΞ⁻), with full reconstruction of intermediate states (e.g., Ω_c⁰ → pK⁻K⁻π⁺, Ω⁻ → Λ⁰K⁻, Λ⁰ → pπ⁻, J/ψ → μ⁺μ⁻). Data correspond to 6 fb⁻¹ of pp collisions at √s = 13 TeV (LHCb Run 2); results are combined with Run 1 measurements using the same modes.

Result: r_τ = 1.109 ± 0.055 (stat) ± 0.010 (syst); τ(Ω_b⁻) = 1.751 ± 0.089 (stat) ± 0.022 (syst) ps — the most precise Ω_b⁻ lifetime to date, with ~2× improvement over previous world average.

Conclusion: The result confirms consistency with heavy quark expansion predictions and significantly constrains non-perturbative QCD contributions in b-baryon lifetimes, validating the theoretical framework and enabling more sensitive searches for new physics in b-hadron decays.

Abstract: The lifetime ratio ${r_τ\equivτ_{Ω_b^-}/τ_{Ξ_b^-}}$ between the ${Ω_b^-}$ and ${Ξ_b^-}$ baryons is measured using a sample of $pp$ collision data corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 6 fb$^{-1}$ and collected by the LHCb experiment during LHC Run 2 (2015$-$2018). The ratio $r_τ$ is measured in two sets of decays modes, ${ {(Ω_b^-,Ξ_b^-)\to(Ω_c^0π^-,Ξ_c^0π^-)}}$ and ${(Ω_b^-,Ξ_b^-)\to(J/ψΩ^-, J/ψΞ^-)}$, with ${(Ω_c^0,Ξ_c^0)\to pK^-K^-π^+}$, ${(Ω^-,Ξ^-)\to(Λ^0 K^-,Λ^0π^-)}$, ${Λ^0\to pπ^-}$ and $J/ψ\toμ^+μ^-$. The measured $r_τ$ values are averaged and combined with Run 1 (2011$-$2012) measurements in the same decay modes to obtain ${r_τ = 1.109\pm0.055\pm0.010}$. Multiplying by the known ${Ξ_b^-}$ lifetime results in the ${Ω_b^-}$ lifetime ${τ_{Ω_b^-} = 1.751\pm0.089\pm0.022~{\rm ps}}$, where the uncertainties are statistical and systematic. This measurement improves on the precision of the $Ω_b^-$ lifetime by about a factor of two over the previous world average. The value of $r_τ$ is in agreement with the most recent theoretical predictions from the heavy quark expansion framework.

</details>


### [60] [Spin-spin correlations in $Λ\barΛ$ pair production at the SPD experiment at NICA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25383)
*D. Gubachev,A. Guskov*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper assesses the feasibility of measuring ΛΛ̄ spin-spin correlations at the SPD experiment (NICA) for pp/dd collisions at √s_NN ≤ 27 GeV, using SU(6) and Burkardt–Jaffe models to estimate P_ΛΛ̄; it evaluates detector performance via full simulation and highlights prospects for probing non-perturbative vs. perturbative s̄s production and initial-state polarization effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit ΛΛ̄ spin correlations as a probe of s̄s spin structure and spin transfer in hadronization—motivated by recent STAR and CMS results—and to explore spin dynamics in the parton-to-hadron transition at NICA energies.

Method: Feasibility study combining theoretical modeling (SU(6) and Burkardt–Jaffe models for spin-triplet s̄s pairs) with full GEANT4-based detector simulation of the SPD experiment; analysis includes kinematic acceptance, reconstruction efficiency, and sensitivity to s̄s production mechanisms.

Result: Quantitative estimates of the expected ΛΛ̄ spin correlation P_ΛΛ̄ are provided; simulation confirms SPD’s capability to measure these correlations in pp and dd collisions; the setup is shown to be suitable for disentangling non-perturbative and perturbative s̄s production and, with polarized beams, for probing initial-state spin effects.

Conclusion: The SPD experiment at NICA offers a unique opportunity to advance understanding of spin-dependent QCD dynamics in hadronization, particularly through precision measurements of ΛΛ̄ spin correlations—complementing existing high-energy results and enabling systematic studies of spin transfer across collision systems and energies.

Abstract: Recent measurements by the STAR collaboration have demonstrated that spin correlations of $Λ\barΛ$ pairs provide sensitivity to the spin structure of $s\bar{s}$ pairs and to spin transfer during hadronization.These results, together with recent CMS measurements at higher energies, open a new avenue for studying spin dynamics in the transition from partons to hadrons.
  A feasibility study of spin-spin correlations in $Λ\barΛ$ pairs at the SPD experiment at NICA is presented for $pp$ and $dd$ collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}}\leq27$~GeV. The expected correlation $P_{Λ\barΛ}$ is estimated within the SU(6) and Burkardt--Jaffe models for a spin-triplet $s\bar{s}$ pair. The kinematic properties of the process are analysed, and the suitability of the detector for such measurements is evaluated using a full simulation. The SPD experiment provides an opportunity to investigate the relative contributions of non-perturbative and perturbative $s\bar{s}$-production mechanisms and, with polarized beams, to study the role of initial-state polarization in spin-dependent partonic dynamics.

</details>
