Wilson,S. Tairafune,A. Eguchi,E. Watanabe,J. Xia,K. Terada,R. Asaka,M. Shinoki,T. Yoshida,V. Gousy-Leblanc,M. Nicholson,S. Amanai,R. Shibayama,R. Shimamura*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a new partial lifetime limit of 4.2 × 10³² years for the trinucleon decay mode ¹⁶O(ppp) → ¹³C π⁺ π⁺ e⁺, using Super-Kamiokande data (0.401 megaton-years), improving prior limits by six orders of magnitude; it introduces the first CNN with attention mechanism for nucleon decay classification in SK, leveraging high-statistics exposure to enhance sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve experimental constraints on rare trinucleon decay modes—specifically ¹⁶O(ppp) → ¹³C π⁺ π⁺ e⁺—which probe beyond-Standard-Model baryon-number violation and grand unification scenarios, where existing limits were insufficiently stringent.

Method: A convolutional neural network with an attention mechanism was trained on simulated and real Cherenkov ring patterns in Super-Kamiokande to classify candidate events; analysis combined data from five operational periods (SK-I to SK-V, total exposure 0.401 megaton-years); background estimation and signal efficiency were evaluated via Monte Carlo simulation and data-driven techniques.

Result: A new lower limit on the partial lifetime of 4.2 × 10³² years (90% CL) for ¹⁶O(ppp) → ¹³C π⁺ π⁺ e⁺, representing a six-order-of-magnitude improvement over previous experimental bounds.

Conclusion: The application of attention-based CNNs significantly enhances pattern recognition in large-volume water Cherenkov detectors for rare-event searches; this result sets the most stringent constraint to date on this trinucleon decay channel and demonstrates the power of modern ML techniques in neutrino and nucleon decay physics.

Abstract: We report a new partial lifetime limit of $4.2 \times 10^{32}$ years for the trinucleon decay mode $^{16}\text{O}(ppp) \rightarrow ^{13}\text{C} π^+ π^+ e^+$, obtained from a search conducted using the Super-Kamiokande detector with 0.401 megaton-years of exposure across five operational periods (SK-I: 1996--2001, SK-II: 2002--2005, SK-III: 2006--2008, SK-IV: 2008--2018, SK-V: 2019--2020). This represents an improvement of six orders of magnitude over previous experimental constraints. The analysis utilizes a convolutional neural network (CNN) incorporating an attention mechanism---a computational technique that enables the model to focus on the most relevant regions of Cherenkov ring patterns---to enhance event classification, thereby improving the sensitivity of the search. This is the first application of a CNN to a nucleon decay search in Super-Kamiokande. Furthermore, the large dataset available in Super-Kamiokande (hereafter "SK") strengthens the statistical power of the study, enabling a more stringent constraint than those set by prior experiments.

</details>


### [62] [Feasibility Study of $e^+e^-\to η_cJ/ψ$ Production and Fully-Charmed Tetraquark Searches at STCF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25870)
*Zhuojuan Dong,Xiaorong Zhou,Xiaorui Lv,Zhengyun You,Jin Zhang,Cong Geng*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the discovery potential of the high-luminosity Super Tau-Charm Facility (STCF) for the fully-charmed vector tetraquark candidate $T_{4c}$ via $e^+e^- \to \eta_c J/\psi$ near threshold (6.71–6.79 GeV), using Monte Carlo simulations with 100 fb⁻¹ per energy point; significance depends critically on the uncertain dielectron width $\Gamma_{ee}$, yielding expected ST significances of 5.1σ, 10.6σ, and 20.5σ for $\Gamma_{ee} = 0.25$, 0.5, and 1 eV, respectively.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the feasibility of discovering the fully-charmed vector tetraquark $T_{4c}$ at the upcoming STCF, leveraging its clean threshold environment and high luminosity, given theoretical interest in exotic hadrons and experimental ambiguity in $T_{4c}$'s dielectron width $\Gamma_{ee}$.

Method: Monte Carlo simulation of an energy scan from $\sqrt{s} = 6.71$ to $6.79~\mathrm{GeV}$ with $100~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ per point; primary analysis uses high-efficiency single-tag (ST) reconstruction of $J/\psi \to \ell^+\ell^-$ ($\ell = e, \mu$); double-tag (DT) reconstruction $\eta_c \to K^+K^-\pi^0$ serves as cross-check; three benchmark values of $\Gamma_{ee}$ (0.25, 0.5, 1 eV) are tested to bracket theoretical uncertainty.

Result: Expected ST significances of $5.1\,\sigma$, $10.6\,\sigma$, and $20.5\,\sigma$ for $\Gamma_{ee} = 0.25$, 0.5, and 1 eV, respectively; DT channel provides consistent but lower-sensitivity validation; statistical sensitivity is sufficient for discovery (≥5σ) across all benchmarks.

Conclusion: The STCF has robust discovery potential for the $T_{4c}$ tetraquark near charm-threshold energies, even under conservative assumptions about $\Gamma_{ee}$; this establishes STCF as a key facility for exploring fully-charmed exotics.

Abstract: The proposed high-luminosity Super Tau-Charm Facility (STCF) offers a clean experimental environment for threshold studies of exotic hadrons. In this Letter, we evaluate the expected significance for the fully-charmed vector tetraquark candidate $T_{4c}$ in the $e^+e^- \to η_c J/ψ$ channel at the STCF. Using Monte Carlo simulations of an energy scan from $\sqrt{s}=6.71$ to $6.79~\mathrm{GeV}$ with $100~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ per point, we adopt a high-efficiency single-tag (ST) reconstruction of $J/ψ$ leptonic decays as the primary strategy, with a double-tag (DT) reconstruction of $η_c \to K^+K^-π^0$ retained as an independent cross-check. Because the signal cross section is governed by the still-uncertain dielectron width $Γ_{ee}$, we consider three benchmark hypotheses, $Γ_{ee}=0.25, 0.5, 1~\mathrm{eV}$. The corresponding expected ST significances are $5.1\,σ$, $10.6\,σ$, and $20.5\,σ$, respectively. These results indicate that the STCF can provide meaningful sensitivity to fully-charmed tetraquark states near threshold.

</details>


### [63] [Future Muon Physics Experiments: Muon Beams and Experimental Apparatus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25943)
*Yi Yuan,Leyun Gao,Jian Tang,Qiang Li*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews global muon-beam facilities and experimental programs across three categories—charged-lepton flavor violation (CLFV), precision muon and bound-state measurements, and muon-scattering experiments—highlighting beam requirements and detector/technique advances needed for next-generation sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess current capabilities and roadmap future upgrades of muon-beam facilities and experiments that probe physics beyond the Standard Model, test QED in bound states, determine fundamental constants, and explore dark sectors.

Method: Systematic review and synthesis of existing and planned muon experiments—including CLFV searches (e.g., μ→eγ, μ-e conversion), precision measurements (g−2, EDM, muonium/muonic-atom spectroscopy), and scattering programs (MUonE, NA64μ, PKMu)—with emphasis on beam parameters (intensity, timing, purity, phase-space control) and experimental design (beamline optics, detector systems, background suppression).

Result: Comprehensive overview identifying key technical challenges and synergies across muon programs; clarifies required beam performance metrics and experimental innovations to achieve order-of-magnitude improvements in sensitivity for CLFV, (g−2), EDM, hadronic vacuum polarization, and dark-sector searches.

Conclusion: Muon-based experiments constitute a uniquely powerful and complementary suite of probes at the intensity and precision frontiers; coordinated advancement of beam infrastructure, detector technology, and analysis techniques is essential to fully exploit their discovery potential in fundamental physics.

Abstract: Muons are powerful probes of fundamental physics at the precision and intensity frontiers. We review the status and planned upgrades of muon-beam facilities worldwide, together with the experimental apparatus required across three experimental categories. Charged-lepton flavor violation (CLFV) searches test charged-lepton flavor conservation through rare muon decays, muon-to-electron conversion in nuclei, and muonium--antimuonium conversion. Precision measurements of the muon magnetic and electric dipole moments, muonium spectroscopy, and muonic-atom spectroscopy test the Standard Model and bound-state QED and determine fundamental constants and nuclear charge and magnetization distributions. Muon-scattering programs include the MUonE determination of hadronic vacuum polarization, NA64$μ$ missing-momentum searches for invisible dark sectors, and the phased PKMu program, which investigates muonphilic dark matter, light mediators, LFV muon--electron processes, and atomic effects in resonant annihilation. We emphasize the beam intensity, timing, purity, and phase-space control, as well as beamline and detector design, background suppression, and experimental techniques needed to achieve next-generation sensitivity and precision.

</details>


### [64] [Improving the CMS High Level Trigger tracking at the HL-LHC with novel and evolved heterogeneous algorithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26376)
*Mario Masciovecchio*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents the state-of-the-art CMS High Level Trigger (HLT) track reconstruction for the HL-LHC, combining the parallelized mkFit algorithm with the hardware-agnostic Patatrack (pixel) and Line Segment Tracking (LST) (outer tracker) algorithms, enhanced by ML techniques (DNNs, multi-objective PSO) to suppress duplicates/misreconstructions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The computational load of charged particle track reconstruction at HL-LHC exceeds projected CPU resources; heterogeneous computing (CPUs/GPUs) and novel, parallelizable, hardware-agnostic algorithms are required to maintain reconstruction efficiency under extreme pile-up and data rates.

Method: Integration of mkFit (parallel/vectorized CPU pattern recognition) with Patatrack (established pixel tracking, Run 3 HLT) and LST (novel outer tracker algorithm); both Patatrack and LST generate seeds for mkFit. ML techniques—deep neural networks and multi-objective particle swarm optimization—are applied to duplicate suppression and misreconstruction mitigation.

Result: State-of-the-art HLT track reconstruction performance for HL-LHC CMS is achieved using the mkFit+Patatrack+LST pipeline with ML-based post-processing, demonstrating scalability and efficiency on heterogeneous architectures.

Conclusion: The combined mkFit–Patatrack–LST framework, augmented with ML, constitutes a viable, high-performance solution for HL-LHC CMS HLT track reconstruction; further ML integration holds promise for continued improvements in accuracy, speed, and robustness.

Abstract: Charged particle track reconstruction is one of the heaviest computational tasks in the event reconstruction chain at LHC experiments. Furthermore, projections for the High Luminosity LHC (HL-LHC) show that the required computing resources for single-threaded CPU algorithms will exceed those that are expected to be available. It follows that experiments at the HL-LHC will need to employ novel and evolved track reconstruction algorithms, within heterogeneous computing systems that include many-core CPUs as well as GPUs, in the attempt to maximize the computational performance while retaining the best possible reconstruction efficiency. In the context of the CMS High Level Trigger (HLT) at the HL-LHC, the mkFit algorithm, already in use for the CMS track reconstruction during the LHC Run 3, will exploit its parallelized and vectorized nature on CPUs to perform pattern recognition using seed tracks produced with algorithms that are designed to be fully parallelizable and hardware agnostic, thus suitable for heterogeneous systems: the Patatrack and the Line Segment Tracking (LST) algorithms. Patatrack is an established algorithm, already used for the CMS pixel track reconstruction at HLT during the Run 3 of the LHC, while LST is a novel algorithm, recently integrated in the CMS software, targeting the reconstruction of tracks in the outer tracker of the HL-LHC CMS detector. The state-of-the-art performance for the CMS HLT track reconstruction at the HL-LHC is presented, obtained using the combination of the mkFit, Patatrack, and LST algorithms, that in turn use machine-learning (ML) techniques such as deep neural networks and multi-objective particle swarm optimization to suppress duplicate and misreconstructed tracks. Prospects of further improvements are also presented, with a focus on the usage of ML techniques for track reconstruction at CMS.

</details>


### [65] [An enhanced track reconstruction for the CMS experiment at the HL-LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26383)
*Mario Masciovecchio*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: CMS proposes a new phase-2 baseline tracking strategy integrating GPU-optimized (Patatrack, LST) and vectorized CPU (mkFit) algorithms with ML techniques to achieve high-efficiency, high-resolution tracking under HL-LHC pileup, while reducing fake rates, computational cost, and enabling displaced/long-lived particle reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address extreme pileup conditions at the High-Luminosity LHC (HL-LHC), where conventional tracking suffers from degraded efficiency, resolution, increased fake rates, and excessive computational load—especially critical for online reconstruction and rare physics signatures like long-lived particles.

Method: A heterogeneous, unified tracking strategy combining GPU-accelerated algorithms (Patatrack, LST) and vectorized CPU-based fitting (mkFit), augmented with machine learning techniques; incorporates global displaced track reconstruction and leverages modern hardware for throughput optimization.

Result: Improved tracking efficiency and resolution, reduced fake track rate, lower computational resource requirements, enhanced throughput, and extended sensitivity to displaced and long-lived particle signatures.

Conclusion: The proposed phase-2 CMS baseline tracking strategy—by unifying diverse algorithmic paradigms and integrating ML and hardware-aware optimizations—enables robust, scalable, and physics-rich tracking for both online and offline reconstruction at the HL-LHC.

Abstract: This contribution presents the new phase-2 CMS baseline tracking strategy currently proposed for online event reconstruction, which can be similarly useful in offline reconstruction under the extreme pileup conditions of the High-Luminosity LHC (HL-LHC). By unifying diverse algorithmic paradigms and integrating machine learning (ML) techniques into a coherent sequence, CMS can maintain high-efficiency and high-resolution tracking while improving the fake track rate and the computational performance, a crucial aspect especially for the HL-LHC. The approach combines GPU-optimized algorithms (Patatrack, LST) with vectorized CPU methods (mkFit), exploiting modern hardware to maximize throughput. This heterogeneous strategy reduces the resource requirements and enhances the physics reach by incorporating a global displaced track reconstruction and extending sensitivity to rare, interesting signatures, such as long-lived particles.

</details>


### [66] [Complementary Time- and Distance-Based Methods for Cosmogenic $^{9}$Li/$^{8}$He Background Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26538)
*Cailian Jiang,Qishan Liu,Liangjian Wen,Gaosong Li,Zeyuan Yu,Wanlei Guo,Lei Zhang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces the DCJ-MuCAT method—a distance-constrained, muon-categorized joint time fit—to improve statistical precision in estimating residual cosmogenic $^{9}$Li/$^{8}$He backgrounds in low-energy neutrino experiments, achieving >40% reduction in uncertainty over prior methods while maintaining accuracy across varying muon rates and isotope fractions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional time-based fits for estimating cosmogenic $^{9}$Li and $^{8}$He backgrounds become unreliable at high muon rates and suffer from low statistics due to muon vetoes; a more robust, statistically efficient estimation framework is needed.

Method: The authors propose (1) J-MuCAT: an event-level, muon-categorized joint time fit using time-to-most-recent-muon binned by muon energy-loss category; (2) TraDiTS: a track-distance-based estimator using candidate-to-muon-track distance distribution, with far-distance region used to isolate muon-uncorrelated background; (3) DCJ-MuCAT: a constrained version of J-MuCAT where the TraDiTS-derived muon-uncorrelated component fixes part of the background model.

Result: In detector-level simulations with realistic cosmogenic vetoes, DCJ-MuCAT reduces statistical uncertainty by >40% vs. J-MuCAT and >10% vs. TraDiTS; fitted background yields remain consistent with truth; performance is stable across wide ranges of muon rate and $^{9}$Li/$^{8}$He fraction.

Conclusion: DCJ-MuCAT provides a practical, precise, and robust framework for residual $^{9}$Li/$^{8}$He background estimation in large-scale low-energy neutrino detectors—especially under high-rate, high-veto conditions where conventional methods fail.

Abstract: Cosmogenic $^{9}$Li and $^{8}$He isotopes constitute an important correlated background in low-energy neutrino experiments because their $β$-delayed neutron decay signatures can mimic inverse beta decay signals. Conventional estimates based on the time since the last muon become challenging at high muon rates, while muon-related vetoes further reduce the residual isotope statistics. We extend the conventional time fit to an event-level muon-categorized joint time (J-MuCAT) fit, which uses the time to the most recent preceding muon in each energy-loss category. A complementary estimate is obtained from the candidate-to-muon-track distance distribution (TraDiTS). The muon-uncorrelated component is determined from the far-distance region and subtracted. This estimate is then used to constrain the J-MuCAT fit, defining the distance-constrained J-MuCAT (DCJ-MuCAT) fit. In detector-level simulation with successive cosmogenic-background vetoes, DCJ-MuCAT reduces the statistical uncertainty by more than $40\%$ relative to J-MuCAT and by more than $10\%$ relative to the TraDiTS. The fitted results remain consistent with the simulation truth. Applicability studies further show good performance over a broad range of muon rates and isotope fractions. The proposed framework provides a practical approach for estimating residual $^{9}$Li/$^{8}$He backgrounds in large neutrino detectors.

</details>


### [67] [Extending the reconstruction of displaced tracks at CMS during the HL-LHC with Line Segment Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26695)
*Jade Chismar*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new Line Segment Tracking (LST) object—the quadruplet (T4)—to enhance displaced track reconstruction at the HL-LHC, extending radial acceptance from 40 cm to 60 cm compared to standard LST.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The HL-LHC's increased pileup demands more efficient, GPU-accelerated tracking algorithms with higher acceptance for displaced tracks—especially beyond the ~8 cm limit of conventional CMS tracking.

Method: The authors extend the existing LST algorithm by introducing the 'quadruplet' (T4), defined as a linked pair of triplets sharing a common line segment, optimized for reconstructing highly displaced tracks using only outer tracker hits; performance is evaluated across multiple physics scenarios.

Result: Inclusion of T4s extends radial displacement acceptance for displaced tracks from 40 cm (with standard LST) to 60 cm, representing a significant gain in geometric coverage and reconstruction efficiency.

Conclusion: The T4-enhanced LST algorithm substantially improves displaced-track reconstruction capability for HL-LHC conditions, enabling access to longer-lived particle signatures previously outside detector acceptance.

Abstract: The upgrade of the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) to the High-Luminosity LHC (HL-LHC) will greatly increase the average number of simultaneous proton-proton interactions per bunch-crossing (pileup), and will place significant demands on computing resources for the reconstruction of charged-particle tracks. Line Segment Tracking (LST) is a novel, highly parallelizable algorithm that can run efficiently on GPUs and has been integrated into the CMS software to enable the reconstruction of displaced tracks in the high pileup scenarios of the HL-LHC. The LST algorithm is designed to build track candidates using only outer tracker hits, naturally allowing for high acceptance of displaced track signatures. In the HL-LHC tracking reconstruction sequence without LST, displaced tracking ends at a radial displacement of the track origin of approximately 8 cm. The usage of LST in track reconstruction enhanced efficiency for displaced tracks, extending the acceptance in radial displacement from the track origin by more than a factor of 4. In this work, we introduce a new LST object, the quadruplet (T4), defined as a linked pair of triplets with a common line segment, targeting displaced track signatures. We present the performance of this improved implementation of the LST algorithm in several physics scenarios, extending the displaced-track acceptance from 40 cm to 60 cm in radial displacement compared to LST without T4s.

</details>

### [68] [Testing Van der Waals black holes using black hole photon rings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25097)
*Chengzhen Li,Meirong Tang,Zhaoyi Xu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the Van der Waals black hole in AdS spacetime using photon ring observables—derived from null geodesics and effective potential analysis—to constrain its molecular volume parameter $b$ and pressure $P$, finding observational consistency with EHT data for M87* and Sgr A*, compatibility with energy conditions, and distinguishability from Schwarzschild BHs via SBH-LBH phase-transition–induced sensitivity to $b$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To observationally test the physical viability of thermodynamic parameters (e.g., molecular volume $b$, pressure $P$) in the Van der Waals black hole model—a non-Kerr AdS BH exhibiting phase transitions—and to probe BH microstructure via strong-field photon ring signatures.

Method: Derived null geodesic equations and conserved quantities from the Van der Waals BH metric; analyzed effective potential to determine photon sphere radii and stability; classified photon ring structures and radiative properties; constrained $b$ and $P$ using EHT measurements of M87* and Sgr A*; verified energy condition satisfaction and phase stability.

Result: The molecular volume $b$ strongly governs photon sphere radius, photon ring morphology, and luminosity; a specific $b$-interval yields excellent agreement with EHT observations while satisfying weak/strong/dominant energy conditions and lying within the stable Van der Waals-type phase transition region; photon rings clearly distinguish Van der Waals BHs from Schwarzschild BHs due to $b$-sensitive SBH-LBH transitions and microstructural discontinuities.

Conclusion: Photon rings serve as a robust observational probe of BH microstructure in thermodynamic black hole models; this work establishes a direct link between microscopic parameters ($b$, $P$), spacetime geometry, and observable features, enabling empirical derivation of BH microphysics.

Abstract: The Van der Waals black hole (BH) is a non-Kerr model in Anti-de Sitter (AdS) spacetime that exhibits characteristic thermodynamic phase transitions. Whether the physical validity of parameters such as the BH molecular volume $b$ and pressure $P$ is feasible still requires observational verification. As a sensitive probe of strong gravitational fields, the BH photon ring directly carries spacetime geometric information, making it an ideal object for model testing. In accordance with the spacetime metric associated with this BH, this study obtains the null geodesic equations for photons and conserved quantities, determines the photon sphere conditions and radii via effective potential analysis, investigates the photon ring classification and radiative properties, and employs measurements from the Event Horizon Telescope (EHT) to place constraints on parameters and simulate the optical appearance. The results show that the molecular volume parameter $b$ significantly regulates the radius of the photon sphere radius, photon ring structure, and the luminosity. Within a specific interval, the model exhibits a high degree of agreement with the EHT measurements for M87* and Sgr A*. This interval not only satisfies the energy conditions (weak, strong, and dominant) but also corresponds to a stable Van der Waals-type phase transition region. Furthermore, the photon ring can distinguish between the Van der Waals BH and the Schwarzschild BH: due to the small-black-hole-large-black-hole (SBH-LBH) phase transition and microstructural discontinuities, the photon ring of the Van der Waals BH is particularly sensitive to the parameter $b$, whereas the Schwarzschild BH lacks these features. This work uses the photon ring to probe BH molecular models, linking its microstructure, spacetime geometry and observational signatures, and provides a new way to derive BH microphysics from observations.

</details>


### [69] [A Closed-Form Master Solution for Gravitational Decoupling of a Unified Family of Isotropic Stellar Seeds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25110)
*Anirudh Pradhan,Safiqul Islam,Ajit Kumar,Muhammad Aamir*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a unified framework for generating anisotropic compact star models via Minimal Geometric Deformation (MGD) using a two-parameter seed metric family. A single closed-form solution for the decoupling sector — expressed via the Gauss hypergeometric function _2F_1 — yields anisotropic extensions for arbitrary parameters (n,m). Physical viability, matching to Schwarzschild exterior, and tidal deformability (Love number) are systematically analyzed, revealing that MGD enhances deformability and that causal limits constrain some models more strictly than others.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and extend known isotropic compact star solutions (e.g., Tolman IV, Durgapal IV–V) into physically viable anisotropic counterparts using Minimal Geometric Deformation, while enabling analytic control over matter content, matching conditions, and relativistic tidal response.

Method: Minimal Geometric Deformation (MGD) applied to a two-parameter isotropic seed metric ν(r) = Ln[C(1 + a r^{2n})^m], with λ(r) fixed by isotropy. The θ-sector is closed analytically via one source function g(r), solving the decoupler equation in terms of _2F_1. Physical acceptability (energy conditions, causality, regularity, matching) and tidal Love numbers are derived exactly.

Result: A master analytic formula for anisotropic extension valid for arbitrary (n,m); explicit mapping of physically admissible parameter space for n=1; demonstration that Tolman IV and Korkina–Orlyanskii allow arbitrarily high compactness under MGD, whereas Heintzmann IIa and Durgapal IV–V exhibit compactness ceilings; systematic enhancement of tidal deformability with decoupling strength, especially near causal limits.

Conclusion: MGD provides a powerful, unified, and analytically tractable scheme for constructing physically acceptable anisotropic compact star models from isotropic seeds, with direct implications for gravitational-wave asteroseismology via enhanced and parameter-sensitive tidal deformability.

Abstract: We present a unification scheme for anisotropic compact stars generated via minimal geometric deformation (MGD), built on a two-parameter seed family $ν(r)=\text{Ln}[C(1+ar^{2n})^{m}]$ whose radial metric potential $μ(r)=e^{-λ(r)}$ is fixed in closed form by the isotropy condition. At $n=1$ the family reproduces Tolman IV, Korkina--Orlyanskii, Heintzmann IIa, and Durgapal IV--V as the particular cases $m=1,2,3,4,5$. The key result is a single closure of the MGD $θ$-sector, through one source function $g(r)$, that integrates the decoupler equation in closed form, in terms of the Gauss hypergeometric function ${}_2F_1$, for \emph{arbitrary} $(n,m)$: the anisotropic extension of the $n=1$ family is thus obtained from one master formula. We derive the effective anisotropic matter content, the Darmois--Israel matching to the exterior Schwarzschild vacuum, and the full set of physical acceptability criteria constraining the parameter space, and map the admissible region in compactness and decoupling strength explicitly for the case $n=1$: Tolman IV and Korkina--Orlyanskii admit physically consistent anisotropic deformations at arbitrarily high compactness, while Heintzmann IIa and Durgapal IV--V are bounded by a compactness ceiling beyond which no value of the decoupling parameter restores causality. Using the same closed-form matter content, we compute the quadrupolar tidal Love number and dimensionless tidal deformability across the admissible region, finding that the decoupling systematically enhances both relative to the isotropic seed at fixed compactness, with a sharp rise near the causal ceiling in the Heintzmann and Durgapal branches.

</details>


### [70] [A Primer on Spacetime Singularities II: Spatially Homogeneous Cosmologies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25112)
*Jean-Pierre Luminet*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper, Primer II, explores concrete realizations of spacetime singularities in spatially homogeneous cosmological models within classical General Relativity, building on the mathematical foundations from Primer I. It analyzes singularity types—including matter, non-scalar (whimper), and kinematic singularities—using Bianchi classification, Raychaudhuri equation, and recent rigorous results, revealing a rich hierarchy of singular boundaries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically investigate how spacetime singularities manifest physically in spatially homogeneous cosmologies, extending the abstract framework of Primer I to concrete model-based analysis and clarifying distinctions among singularity types beyond simple curvature blow-up.

Method: Geometric analysis of spatial homogeneity and Bianchi classification; derivation and analysis of Einstein field equations for orthogonal and tilted perfect fluid cosmologies; application of Raychaudhuri's equation to establish finite-time boundary formation; classification of singularities by curvature, density, fluid tilt, and Cauchy horizon structure; reinterpretation of Ellis-King analysis in light of modern results (e.g., curvature-blow-up in Bianchi VIII/IX, kinematic singularities, dipole cosmology).

Result: Demonstrates that spatially homogeneous cosmologies host a rich hierarchy of singular boundaries: standard matter singularities (divergent density/Ricci), non-scalar 'whimper' singularities (finite curvature but causal pathologies at Cauchy horizons), and kinematic singularities (geodesically complete yet singular due to extreme fluid tilt); confirms and extends curvature-blow-up results for Bianchi VIII and IX; identifies reappearance of whimper behavior in dipole cosmology.

Conclusion: Even under high symmetry (spatial homogeneity), general relativistic cosmologies exhibit diverse and nuanced singularity structures — challenging simplistic notions of singularities as mere curvature infinities — thereby necessitating refined classification schemes and setting the stage for dynamical analysis in Primer III.

Abstract: This article is the second part of a review devoted to spacetime singularities in classical General Relativity. While Primer I developed the mathematical framework for defining, classifying, and proving the existence of singularities, the present work examines their concrete realization in spatially homogeneous cosmological models. After reviewing the geometrical foundations of homogeneity and the Bianchi classification, we derive the field equations for orthogonal and tilted perfect fluid cosmologies and use the Kasner and Bianchi I solutions as elementary prototypes of anisotropic singular behaviour. Raychaudhuri focusing provides general conditions under which the homogeneous Cauchy development reaches a finite proper time boundary. We then distinguish matter singularities, characterized by divergent density and Ricci curvature, from more subtle non-scalar or whimper singularities associated with Cauchy horizons and extreme fluid tilt. The classical Ellis-King analysis is reconsidered in the light of later developments, including rigorous curvature-blow-up results for Bianchi VIII and IX models, kinematic singularities in geodesically complete tilted spacetimes, and the recent reappearance of whimper behaviour in dipole cosmology. The resulting picture shows that even spatially homogeneous cosmologies exhibit a remarkably rich hierarchy of singular boundaries. The detailed dynamical approach to these boundaries - Hamiltonian cosmology, Kasner transitions, Mixmaster dynamics and the BKL conjecture - is reserved for Primer III.

</details>


### [71] [Generalized Regular Black Holes with Tunable Cores and Geodesic Properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25113)
*Hassan Hassanabadi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a family of regular, spherically symmetric black-hole geometries parameterized by a discrete core index n, regularization scale a, and deformation parameter η; it systematically analyzes their horizon structure, thermodynamics (temperature, entropy, first law), null/timelike geodesics (photon sphere, shadow radius, ISCO, MBO), and weak lensing—unifying Bardeen and Reissner–Nordström-like features in a tunable framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a flexible, physically motivated class of regular black-hole spacetimes that interpolate between known solutions (e.g., Bardeen, Schwarzschild, Reissner–Nordström) while allowing independent control over core regularity (via n and a) and asymptotic charge-like corrections (via η), enabling systematic study of how core structure and exterior deformation jointly influence observables.

Method: Constructs static, spherically symmetric metrics from a tunable, positive, normalized effective energy density profile; derives exact and perturbative solutions for horizons, thermodynamic quantities (using radial Einstein equation), null geodesics (photon sphere, shadow radius, Lyapunov exponent), timelike geodesics (ISCO, MBO, orbital frequencies), and weak gravitational lensing via asymptotic metric expansion.

Result: Obtains analytic and implicit expressions for horizon radii, extremality condition, Hawking temperature, entropy, horizon thermodynamic identity; computes photon sphere radius, shadow radius (with exact limits and weak-η approximations), orbital/instability timescale ratio via angular frequency and Lyapunov exponent; derives ISCO and MBO conditions; provides weak-lensing deflection and magnification in sector-isolated asymptotic expansion.

Conclusion: The proposed family provides a unified, parametric framework to disentangle the distinct imprints of central regularity (governed by n and a) and asymptotic RN-like deformation (governed by η) on black-hole thermodynamics and geodesic observables—enabling quantitative comparison with astrophysical constraints (e.g., EHT shadow measurements, QPOs, lensing).

Abstract: We introduce a family of static and spherically symmetric regular black-hole geometries characterized by a discrete core index $n$, a regularization scale $a$, and a dimensionless deformation parameter $η$. The construction starts from a positive, normalized effective density profile with a tunable central behavior. The lowest member contains the Bardeen geometry as a limiting case, whereas a nonzero deformation parameter generates a Reissner--Nordström-like asymptotic correction. The parameter $n$ controls the order of central depletion: the lowest member possesses a de Sitter-type core, whereas higher members approach a Minkowski-like center with vanishing density and curvature. We determine the horizon structure, extremality condition, Hawking temperature, entropy, and the horizon thermodynamic identity implied by the radial Einstein equation. Null geodesics are analyzed to obtain the photon sphere, critical impact parameter, and shadow radius, including analytic weak-deformation approximations, the exact Schwarzschild and Bardeen limits, and comparison with the leading Reissner--Nordström-like behavior. The photon-orbit angular frequency and Lyapunov exponent are then used to separate orbital and instability timescales; their ratio provides information not already contained in the shadow radius. Weak gravitational lensing and magnification are discussed through a controlled sector-isolated expansion of the asymptotic metric. We further study timelike geodesics, circular motion, the marginally bound orbit, and the innermost stable circular orbit, deriving exact implicit relations and perturbative expressions. The model provides a unified framework for investigating how a tunable regular core and an RN-like exterior deformation affect the thermodynamic and geodesic properties of regular black holes.

</details>


### [72] [Event Detection and Finite-Grid Effects at an Imposed Absorbing Boundary in Newtonian Black Hole Orbit Integrations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25137)
*Gregory M Davis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines how numerical implementation of absorbing boundaries affects black-hole orbit classification, showing that discrete boundary evaluation—especially at t=0—significantly influences inferred critical periapsis, and that apparent thresholds may reflect numerical artifacts rather than true dynamical behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether numerically observed critical periapsis values in black-hole orbit classification stem from genuine physical/dynamical thresholds or are artifacts of how absorbing boundaries are discretely implemented in simulations.

Method: Numerical integration of massless particle trajectories around a Newtonian point mass, with an absorbing boundary at rsink = 10⁴RS; systematic variation of time-step resolution and inclusion/exclusion of boundary evaluation at initial time t = 0; comparison against analytically known Keplerian periapsis q as ground truth for classification.

Result: Omitting boundary evaluation at t = 0 causes misclassification of particles already inside rsink; including t = 0 evaluation recovers exact analytic classification across all grids; higher grid resolution drives inferred critical periapsis asymptotically toward rsink, revealing numerical dependence.

Conclusion: Apparent critical periapsis is not intrinsic to dynamics alone but depends on both the imposed sink radius and its numerical treatment—highlighting the necessity of distinguishing numerical artifacts from real dynamical thresholds in orbit classification studies.

Abstract: Numerical classification of black-hole orbits can depend on how prescribed inner capture or absorbing boundaries are implemented. This study investigated how discrete evaluation of such a boundary affects orbit classification. Massless particles were integrated about an isolated Newtonian point mass and initialized at periapsis with an absorbing boundary at rsink = 104RS. Because the Keplerian periapsis q is constant, the expected classification can be analytically determined. A classifier omitting evaluation at initialization can miss particles that already satisfy the removal criterion, but evaluation at t = 0 reproduced the analytic classification on every sampled grid. As the grid resolution increased, the inferred critical periapsis approached the imposed sink radius. These results demonstrate that an apparent critical periapsis can depend on both the imposed boundary and its numerical evaluation, emphasizing the need to distinguish numerical effects from genuine dynamical thresholds.

</details>


### [73] [The BTZ black hole, the Third Law and gravitational collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25218)
*Aidan M. McSharry,Harvey S. Reall,Jorge E. Santos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper proves a Third Law of black hole mechanics for the BTZ black hole in 3D gravity with matter satisfying the dominant energy condition, showing that extremal BTZ cannot be reached in finite time from trapped-surface initial data—unless the collapse occurs in the Neveu-Schwarz sector, where extremal BTZ formation *is* possible; this apparent contradiction is resolved by sector-dependent supersymmetry (Ramond vs. Neveu-Schwarz), and infinite-time asymptotic approaches to extremality are also analyzed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish whether the extremal BTZ black hole can be physically attained in finite time via gravitational collapse, and to reconcile seemingly contradictory results regarding the Third Law in 3D gravity.

Method: Defines quasilocal energy and angular momentum; employs spinorial techniques to derive a BPS inequality saturated by extremal BTZ; analyzes gravitational collapse of a massless scalar field; distinguishes boundary conditions and superselection sectors (Neveu-Schwarz vs. Ramond) in AdS₃.

Result: A rigorous Third Law is proven: no finite-time evolution from trapped-surface initial data can yield an event horizon identical to extremal BTZ under generic boundary conditions; however, extremal BTZ *can* form in finite time from massless scalar collapse in the Neveu-Schwarz sector—highlighting sector-dependent supersymmetry as the resolution.

Conclusion: The Third Law holds robustly for generic evolutions, but its apparent violation in scalar collapse stems from sector mismatch: extremal BTZ is supersymmetric only in the Ramond sector, while collapse dynamics reside in the Neveu-Schwarz sector; thus, the law constrains horizon geometry attainment, not just asymptotic charges.

Abstract: We prove that a Third Law of black hole mechanics holds for the BTZ black hole in 3d gravity coupled to matter obeying the dominant energy condition. This law states that initial data containing a trapped surface cannot evolve in finite time to a black hole that coincides with extremal BTZ on the event horizon, for any choice of boundary conditions at infinity. The result is proved by defining a quasilocal energy and angular momentum and using spinorial methods to establish a BPS inequality that is saturated by the extremal BTZ solution. Nevertheless, we show that gravitational collapse of a massless scalar field can result in the formation of an extremal BTZ black hole in finite time. The difference between these results arises because gravitational collapse occurs in the Neveu-Schwarz sector, whereas extremal BTZ is supersymmetric in the Ramond sector. Solutions that settle down to extremal BTZ in infinite time along the event horizon are also discussed.

</details>


### [74] [Gravity-Informed Neural Networks for Post-Newtonian Binary Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25222)
*Gabriele Barbagallo,Javier Matulich*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Introduces GravINNs, a physics-informed neural network framework that embeds post-Newtonian equations of motion to model conservative and dissipative binary orbital dynamics—achieving high accuracy without requiring reference trajectories for training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop accurate, flexible, and computationally efficient surrogates for post-Newtonian (PN) binary orbital dynamics—both conservative and dissipative—without reliance on expensive numerical integrations or precomputed trajectory data.

Method: Constructs gravity-informed neural networks (GravINNs) that directly encode PN equations of motion into the loss function; implements three tiers: (i) single-orbit conservative surrogates, (ii) parametric 4D surrogate (initial separation, radial/tangential momenta, symmetric mass ratio), and (iii) dissipative 2.5PN radiation-reaction modeling without dissipative training data.

Result: GravINNs achieve high accuracy across diverse orbital configurations; the parametric model generalizes over orbit families; the 2.5PN extension correctly captures orbital decay and secular energy loss without dissipative reference trajectories.

Conclusion: Physics-informed neural networks—specifically GravINNs—are capable of robustly modeling both conservative and dissipative post-Newtonian dynamics, offering a promising alternative to traditional numerical integration with potential applications in gravitational-wave modeling and parameter estimation.

Abstract: We introduce a gravity-informed neural-network (GravINNs) framework for learning post-Newtonian binary orbital dynamics. The method incorporates the equations of motion directly into the training phase and is developed at three complementary levels. First, we construct single-orbit surrogates for conservative post-Newtonian dynamics and assess their accuracy across different orbital configurations. We then extend the approach to a parametric model spanning a four-dimensional parameter space defined by the initial separation, radial and tangential momenta, and symmetric mass ratio, allowing a single trained network to represent entire families of orbits and replace repeated numerical integrations. Finally, we consider dissipative dynamics by including the leading radiation-reaction contribution at $2.5$PN order. In this regime, the network accurately reproduces both the orbital evolution and the associated secular energy loss without using dissipative reference trajectories in the training. These results show that physics-informed neural-networks (PINNs) can provide accurate and flexible representations of both conservative and dissipative post-Newtonian dynamics. The complete implementation and the models used in this work are publicly available through the \texttt{GravINNs} repository.

</details>


### [75] [Gravitational wave signatures of subsolar neutron star formation in collapsar disks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25225)
*Vishal Baibhav,Valerio De Luca,Loris Del Grosso,Emanuele Berti,Brian D. Metzger,Lam Hui*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates gravitational-wave signatures of subsolar-mass neutron star binaries formed in collapsar accretion disks, identifying distinctive environmental imprints—orbital Doppler modulation, gas-driven migration, and tidal interactions—that distinguish them from other subsolar merger channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the collapsar formation channel for subsolar-mass neutron stars by identifying unique gravitational-wave signatures arising from their birth environment—a neutrino-cooled accretion disk around a central black hole.

Method: Waveform modeling incorporating relativistic orbital motion (Doppler/phase modulations), gas-driven migration dynamics, and tidal effects during inspiral; analysis of detectability and parameter estimation with current and next-generation gravitational-wave detectors.

Result: Subsolar-mass binary systems formed in collapsar disks produce uniquely identifiable waveforms due to combined low masses and environmental imprints; these features enable discrimination from alternative formation channels (e.g., primordial or isolated binary evolution).

Conclusion: Gravitational-wave observations of subsolar-mass mergers can probe collapsar physics—if detected, such events would provide direct evidence for neutron star formation in active accretion disks and constrain the viability of the collapsar pathway.

Abstract: Standard models for stellar core collapse predict the birth of neutron stars with masses exceeding a solar mass. In the collapsar model, a rapidly rotating massive star collapses to a black hole surrounded by a dense, neutrino-cooled accretion disk, whose outer regions may in some cases fragment into neutron-rich clumps that collapse to form subsolar-mass neutron stars. These disk-formed objects can then merge either with one another or, following possibly hierarchical evolution, with the central black hole. In this work, we characterize the gravitational-wave signatures of these binaries, focusing on distinctive features of the collapsar environment: Doppler and other phase modulations from orbital motion about the central black hole, gas-driven migration in the disk, and tidal interactions during inspiral. We show that the combination of anomalously low component masses with these environmental imprints produces unique waveform signatures that can distinguish the collapsar channel from other subsolar merger scenarios. Current and next-generation detectors have good prospects for identifying the intrinsic properties of such objects and, if subsolar mergers are discovered, for testing and constraining the collapsar formation pathway.

</details>


### [76] [Accurate Black Hole Quasinormal Modes, Regge Poles, and Greybody Factors from Light Ring Ladder Symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25228)
*Rajes Ghosh,David Pereñiguez,Jaime Redondo-Yuste,Emanuele Berti*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reveals a hidden ladder symmetry in black hole perturbation equations, mapping quasinormal modes, Regge poles, and greybody factors to an anharmonic oscillator problem, enabling highly accurate analytical computations (up to 18th order in inverse angular momentum) for Schwarzschild black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a systematic, efficient, and convergent analytical framework for computing black hole response functions—quasinormal modes, Regge poles, and greybody factors—beyond existing approximation schemes.

Method: Exploits the high-frequency correspondence between black hole oscillations and null geodesics near the light ring; identifies a hidden ladder symmetry in the radial Teukolsky (or Regge–Wheeler/Zerilli) equation; reformulates the problem as quantum-mechanical anharmonic oscillator using WKB and large-angular-momentum expansion; implements resummation techniques (e.g., Padé, Borel) to enhance convergence.

Result: Analytical expressions for quasinormal modes, Regge poles, and greybody factors of Schwarzschild black holes up to 18th order in 1/ℓ; demonstrably improved accuracy and convergence over standard WKB or continued-fraction methods; successful resummation boosts greybody factor accuracy significantly.

Conclusion: The ladder-symmetry-based anharmonic-oscillator framework provides a unified, high-precision, and extensible method for black hole spectroscopy, applicable to rotating black holes and modified gravity theories.

Abstract: The characteristic oscillations of a black hole can be understood in terms of null rays trapped near its unstable light ring, with this correspondence becoming exact in the high-frequency limit. In this regime, we uncover a hidden ladder symmetry in the perturbation equations, which maps the calculation of quasinormal modes, Regge poles, and greybody factors for a spherically symmetric black hole onto the quantum-mechanical problem of an anharmonic oscillator. This provides a systematic, efficient framework for computing the black hole response, with improved convergence properties compared with existing approximation schemes. As an explicit application, we analytically compute all three quantities for a Schwarzschild black hole through eighteenth order in inverse angular momentum, obtaining highly accurate results. We explore different resummation schemes and find that they can further improve the accuracy of the Regge poles and, particularly, the greybody factors. Our framework can be readily extended to rotating black holes and to modified theories of gravity.

</details>


### [77] [Higher derivative Hořava-Lifshitz fermions: from superconductivity to atomic spectroscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25236)
*A. A. Araújo Filho,K. E. L. de Farias,N. Heidari,M. Paganelly,A. F. Santos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies an isotropic Hořava-Lifshitz fermion theory with a dimension-six spatial derivative term (z=2 Lifshitz scaling), analyzing its consequences for superconductivity (BCS/NJL pairing, gap equation, Meissner effect), scattering (exponentially damped oscillatory potential, finite cross sections), and atomic spectroscopy (hydrogenic level shifts in electronic/muonic atoms), yielding direct constraints on HL-scale couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore physical implications of Hořava-Lifshitz (HL) fermions—specifically, how the z=2 anisotropic scaling modifies low-energy phenomena such as superconductivity, scattering, and atomic energy levels—while preserving first-order time evolution and staying within the derivative expansion regime.

Method: Three complementary analytical approaches: (1) BCS/NJL mean-field theory to compute quasiparticle spectra, finite-temperature gap equation, and gauge-invariant stability criterion for superconductivity; (2) scalar pole exchange kernel to derive a massive, exponentially damped oscillatory potential and compute Born-scattering amplitudes and cross sections; (3) perturbative quantum electrodynamic calculation of hydrogenic level shifts (1S, 2S, 2P) induced by the HL-modified interaction, retaining full range dependence.

Result: The z=2 dispersion alters the density of states and electromagnetic vertices, affecting superconducting gap formation and Meissner screening; yields a finite-range Yukawa-like potential with quartic momentum dependence leading to finite forward/total cross sections and non-Rutherford transfer behavior; induces calculable, range-dependent shifts in hydrogenic energy levels—especially enhanced in muonic atoms—providing spectroscopic bounds on the product of nuclear and leptonic HL couplings.

Conclusion: The z=2 Hořava-Lifshitz fermion sector generates observable, testable deviations from standard QED and BCS physics across condensed matter, scattering, and atomic spectroscopy domains; muonic atom transitions offer the most sensitive probe of the short-distance HL scale due to their enhanced wavefunction overlap with the nucleus.

Abstract: We investigate an isotropic Hořava-Lifshitz fermion theory supplemented by a dimension-six spatial derivative operator, preserving first order time evolution while modifying the ultraviolet dispersion relation. Within the regime of validity of the derivative expansion, we develop three complementary consequences of the $z=2$ sector. First, a Bardeen-Cooper-Schrieffer/Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (BCS/NJL) mean field treatment of fermion pairing yields the particle and antiparticle quasiparticle spectra, the finite temperature gap equation, and the gauge invariant criterion for a stable superconducting phase. The modified dispersion alters both the density of states governing the pairing scale and the electromagnetic vertices entering the Meissner response. Second, using the scalar pole denominator as a minimal spin independent exchange kernel, we derive an exponentially damped and oscillatory massive potential and obtain the corresponding Born scattering amplitudes. The quartic momentum dependence renders both the forward and total cross sections finite and produces a large transfer behavior distinct from Rutherford scattering. Finally, we evaluate the induced shifts of the hydrogenic $1S$, $2S$, and $2P$ levels in closed form, retaining the full dependence on the interaction range. In other words, these latter results gives direct spectroscopic constraints on the product of nuclear and leptonic couplings, with muonic atoms, showing the respective sensitivity to the short distance Hořava-Lifshitz sector.

</details>


### [78] [Frozen-In Gravitational Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25240)
*Felipe A. Asenjo,Maricarmen A. Winkler,Luca Comisso*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces gravitational field connections—two-surfaces and field lines with frozen-in connectivity—enabled by an ideal Ohm-type condition in a nonlinear electrodynamics–inspired formulation of general relativity; it derives conserved gravitational magnetic flux and gravitational helicity, revealing topological constraints on spacetime evolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand persistent geometric and topological structures arising from the nonlinear evolution of spacetime governed by the Einstein field equations.

Method: Reformulates the Einstein equations analogously to nonlinear electrodynamics in continuous media; identifies gravitational field connections via an ideal Ohm-type condition; derives conservation laws using differential-geometric and topological analysis.

Result: Demonstrates existence of gravitational field connections with frozen-in topology; proves conservation of gravitational magnetic flux and gravitational helicity; provides topological interpretation of field-line linking and twisting.

Conclusion: General relativity admits intrinsic topological invariants—gravitational flux and helicity—that constrain admissible spacetime evolutions, offering a new organizing principle for nonlinear gravitational dynamics.

Abstract: Spacetime can undergo complex nonlinear evolution, as governed by the Einstein field equations, and a central challenge is to understand the geometric structures that arise, persist, and interact throughout its evolution. Using a formulation of Einstein equations that parallels nonlinear electrodynamics of continuous media, we show that general relativity admits gravitational field connections---two-surfaces and associated field lines whose connectivity is maintained by the spacetime dynamics. This gravitational frozen-in behavior is enabled by an ideal Ohm-type condition for the gravitational field. We further show that the same framework naturally leads to a conserved ``gravitational magnetic'' flux. A conserved gravitational helicity also emerges, with a clear topological interpretation in terms of gravitational field-line structures. These results identify well-defined topological constraints on admissible spacetime evolution and provide an organizing principle underlying the nonlinear dynamics of spacetime.

</details>


### [79] [Reconnection of Gravitational Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25241)
*Luca Comisso,Felipe A. Asenjo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a theory of gravitational reconnection—analogue to magnetic reconnection—in tetrad-based general relativity, quantifying how breakdown of ideal (frozen-in) gravitational field evolution alters flux conservation, energy-momentum exchange, and gravitational helicity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand non-ideal gravitational dynamics analogous to plasma resistivity in magnetohydrodynamics, specifically how deviations from frozen-in behavior of the gravitational field affect spacetime structure and conservation laws.

Method: Uses tetrad formulation of GR; introduces an ideal Ohm-type condition for gravity; defines gravitational reconnection as topological change in gravitational field sheets; derives reconnection rates via breaking of the frozen-in constraint; analyzes energy-momentum transfer and gravitational helicity evolution using geometric and field-theoretic tools.

Result: Quantitative expressions for gravitational reconnection rate; demonstration that reconnection mediates energy-momentum exchange between gravity and matter; derivation of modified helicity evolution equation incorporating reconnection effects.

Conclusion: Gravitational reconnection provides a rigorous, geometrically grounded framework for describing topological changes in dynamical spacetimes, with implications for strong-field gravity, gravitational wave generation, and quantum gravity phenomenology.

Abstract: In a tetrad formulation of general relativity, an ideal Ohm-type condition for the gravitational field implies the conservation of gravitational fluxes and the preservation of the connectivity of gravitational field sheets. We investigate the consequences of departures from ideal gravitational evolution by developing a theory of gravitational reconnection, defined as a change in the connectivity of gravitational field sheets arising from the breakdown of the ideal frozen-in evolution of the gravitational field. We derive quantitative measures of the gravitational reconnection rate, show that gravitational reconnection alters the energy-momentum exchange between the gravitational and matter sectors, and determine how it modifies the evolution of gravitational helicity. Our results provide a quantitative framework for describing changes in gravitational connectivity and their consequences in dynamical spacetimes.

</details>


### [80] [Kerr-Degenerate Shadows and Distinct Strong-Deflection Lensing in Rotating Hayward-like and Bardeen-like Geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25258)
*Chen-Hung Hsiao,Limei Yuan,Yidun Wan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares rotating Hayward-like and Bardeen-like regular black hole geometries within a Kerr-like axisymmetric framework, showing identical photon shadow boundaries but distinguishable strong-deflection lensing signatures—especially in time delays, image separations, and magnifications—that depend on the regularization length ℓ and areal-radius profile, enabling potential observational discrimination from Kerr and between the two regular models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether rotating regular black hole models (Hayward-like and Bardeen-like) can be observationally distinguished from Kerr and from each other using strong gravitational lensing and shadow observables, especially in light of EHT data for M87* and Sgr A*.

Method: Analyzes two rotating regular geometries embedded in a common Kerr-like ansatz differing only in their areal-radius functions R_i(ρ); computes horizon structure, Komar charges, energy condition violations (via Einstein tensor as effective stress tensor), photon sphere properties, black hole shadows, and equatorial strong-deflection limit (SDL) lensing observables—including impact parameter u_m^+, deflection angle coefficients \bar a_+, \bar b_+, image separation s^+, relative magnitude r_{mag}^+, and time-delay correction ΔT_{2,1}.

Result: Shadow boundary is identical across Kerr, Hayward-like, and Bardeen-like geometries at same mass, spin, and inclination—hence EHT shadow size constraints do not bound ℓ. However, prograde equatorial SDL observables (\bar a_+, \bar b_+, s^+, r_{mag}^+, ΔT_{2,1}) depend explicitly on ℓ and the areal-radius profile, breaking degeneracy; notably, this holds even in the one-horizon regime where no inner horizon exists—unlike Kerr.

Conclusion: While shadows cannot distinguish these regular models from Kerr or each other, high-precision strong-deflection lensing measurements—particularly time delays and higher-order image separations/magnitudes—offer viable pathways to test regularity, constrain ℓ, and discriminate between Hayward-like and Bardeen-like spacetimes, including in parameter domains inaccessible to Kerr.

Abstract: We study and compare rotating Hayward-like and Bardeen-like geometries within a common Kerr-like axisymmetric ansatz, with the models distinguished by their areal-radius functions $R_i(ρ)$. On the adopted radial branches, the positive minimum areal radius excludes the Kerr ring locus, and the curvature invariants examined here remain finite. We identify the parameter domains containing two, one, or no horizons. The two geometries have the same asymptotic Komar charges but different finite-radius values. If we treat the Einstein tensor as an effective stress tensor, then for every nonzero regularity length $\ell$, this ansatz violates the null and weak energy conditions, as demonstrated by the equatorial radial-null contraction. For the complete photon family on a black-hole branch, the shadow boundary is identical to that of Kerr at the same mass, spin, and observer inclination, and is also identical for the two regular geometries. The corresponding area-equivalent shadow diameters for M87* and Sgr~A* are consistent with the approximate Event Horizon Telescope (EHT)-based intervals adopted here, and we do not obtain further constraints on $\ell$. By contrast, the prograde equatorial strong-deflection limit (SDL) calculations are only partly degenerate. At fixed spin, $u_m^+$ and $θ_\infty^+$ are common to Kerr and the two regular geometries, whereas $\bar a_+$, $\bar b_+$, the image separation, the relative magnitude, and the subleading time-delay correction depend on $\ell$ and on the areal-radius profile. Within the stated strong-deflection approximation, future measurements of $ΔT_{2,1}$, $s^+$, and $r_{\rm mag}^+$ may help test these rotating geometries and distinguish the Hayward-like and Bardeen-like predictions from Kerr, including in the one-horizon parameter domain where the adopted branch contains no Kerr-like inner horizon.

</details>


### [81] [Circular acceleration in Minkowski spacetime: thermality versus finite size](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25277)
*Cameron R D Bunney,Jorma Louko*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how spatial confinement (a circular boundary) and ambient temperature affect the response of an Unruh-DeWitt detector in uniform circular motion in 2+1D Minkowski spacetime, deriving analytic results for the large-boundary limit and identifying temperature-enhanced resonance peaks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Experimental interest in observing the circular-motion analogue of the Unruh effect—particularly in analogue spacetime systems—necessitates understanding how realistic conditions like spatial boundaries and ambient thermal states modify the detector response.

Method: Analytic calculation of the transition rate (response function) of an Unruh-DeWitt detector on a circular orbit coupled to a massless scalar field confined within a circular boundary in 2+1D Minkowski spacetime; field prepared in either vacuum or thermal state; analysis performed in the large-boundary (low-curvature) regime.

Result: Derivation of closed-form analytic expressions for the detector response; identification of resonance peaks in the excitation spectrum; demonstration that these resonances are enhanced when the scalar field is in a thermal (rather than vacuum) state.

Conclusion: Spatial confinement and ambient temperature significantly alter the operational notion of effective temperature for circularly accelerated detectors; resonance structure becomes richer and more detectable under thermal background, informing design and interpretation of analogue experiments.

Abstract: The Unruh effect predicts that a uniformly linearly accelerated observer with proper acceleration $a$ reacts to the Minkowski vacuum through excitations and de-excitations with the characteristics of a thermal state at temperature $T_U=a/(2π)$. An observer in uniform circular motion will experience similar excitations and de-excitations that we may use to operationally define an effective temperature, which however depends not only on the acceleration but also on the orbital speed and excitation energy. Motivated by the experimental interest in the circular motion Unruh effect, we investigate how spatial confinement modifies the response of an Unruh-DeWitt detector in $2+1$ Minkowski spacetime. We consider a massless scalar field confined within a circular boundary prepared in either the vacuum or a thermal state, probed by an Unruh-DeWitt detector on a circular orbit, a setting that describes proposed analogue spacetime systems for testing the effect, and in which both a boundary and an ambient temperature will necessarily be present. We establish analytic results for the detector response in the large-boundary regime and identify resonance peaks, which are more prominent when the field has an ambient temperature.

</details>


### [82] [Black Hole Perturbation Toolkit: Low frequency and post-Newtonian expansions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25281)
*Jakob Neef,Chris Kavanagh,Adrian Ottewill*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces enhanced weak-field analytic capabilities—specifically post-Newtonian (PN) and post-Minkowskian (PM) expansions—for the Teukolsky and SpinWeightedSpheroidalHarmonics packages in the Black Hole Perturbation Toolkit (BHPT), enabling high-precision, laptop-based computation of MST coefficients, renormalized angular momentum, radial function amplitudes, phase shifts, energy/angular momentum fluxes, and self-force quantities for generic spin weight s and (ℓ,m) modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend BHPT’s analytic reach into the weak-field regime for high-order PN self-force calculations and waveform modeling of small mass-ratio binaries, complementing existing numerical Teukolsky solvers with efficient, high-precision low-frequency expansions.

Method: Implementation of symbolic and series-expansion algorithms for post-Newtonian and post-Minkowskian expansions of Teukolsky equation solutions, including MST coefficients, renormalized angular momentum, homogeneous/inhomogeneous radial functions, fluxes, and self-force related quantities; unified support for arbitrary spin weight s and (ℓ,m) modes.

Result: New analytic functionality in Teukolsky v.1.2 and SpinWeightedSpheroidalHarmonics v.1.1 enabling state-of-the-art PN/PM expansions of key perturbative quantities—computed efficiently on standard laptops—with validated accuracy for generic s, ℓ, m and applicable to circular Kerr orbits and self-force computations.

Conclusion: The upgrades significantly advance BHPT’s utility for analytical gravitational waveform modeling, especially for extreme-mass-ratio inspirals, by bridging high-precision weak-field analytics with robust numerical infrastructure.

Abstract: The Teukolsky and SpinWeightedSpheroidalHarmonics packages of the black hole perturbation toolkit (BHPT) are one of the main public libraries to compute numerical solutions to the Teukolsky equation and related quantities. This work is a companion paper presenting their new and improved weak-field analytic functionality focusing on post-Newtonian expansions. The Teukolsky package now allows users to independently extract multiple useful quantities, such as the so called MST coefficients, renormalized angular momentum, amplitudes of the radial functions or the phase shift as a low frequency/PM expansion. It also provides PN expansions of the homogeneous radial functions or - for circular orbits in Kerr - inhomogeneous amplitudes, as well as energy and angular momentum fluxes at infinity and the horizon. All quantities can be computed to state of the art precision on regular laptops. It also provides a suite of tools for post-Newtonian self force computations and updates the analytic functionality of the SpinWeightedSpheroidalHarmonics package. Computations are possible both for specific and generic spin weight $s$ and harmonic mode numbers $\ell$ and $m$. This package paves the way for intensive high-order post-Newtonian self-force calculations at the forefront of waveform modelling of small mass ratio black hole binaries, as well as giving widely useful low-frequency expansions for black hole perturbation theory. All functions described in this paper are available in BHPT versions Teukolsky v.1.2 and SpinWeightedSpheroidalHarmonics v.1.1.

</details>


### [83] [Thin-Shell Black Bounce](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25354)
*Leandro A. Lessa,Renan B. Magalhães,Gonzalo J. Olmo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies a thin-shell structure at the minimal-area bounce of black-bounce spacetimes sourced by anisotropic fluids, shows it arises from metric degeneracy and distributional curvature, constructs new static spherically symmetric solutions supported by delta-function thin shells violating energy conditions, uses Lorentzian/Gaussian smeared energy densities inspired by quantum gravity minimum length, and analyzes their thermodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the origin of the minimal-area bounce in recently proposed black-bounce solutions, reveal its hidden thin-shell nature due to metric degeneracy, and construct physically motivated models incorporating quantum-gravitational minimum-length effects via smeared matter profiles.

Method: Analysis of metric degeneracy and distributional curvature at the bounce; construction of new static, spherically symmetric black-bounce solutions with thin-shell support using junction conditions; implementation of Lorentzian and Gaussian energy density profiles to model quantum-gravitational smearing; thermodynamic analysis including temperature, entropy, and stability.

Result: Demonstration that the bounce necessarily hosts a delta-function thin shell of exotic matter violating standard energy conditions; derivation of two new families of solutions—regular black holes and wormholes—with well-defined geometric and thermodynamic properties; explicit computation of thermodynamic quantities showing dependence on smearing scale and shell location.

Conclusion: The minimal-area bounce in black-bounce geometries is intrinsically associated with a thin-shell structure arising from metric degeneracy; such shells are unavoidable in effective descriptions incorporating quantum-gravitational minimum length; the resulting objects admit consistent thermodynamic interpretation and bridge regular black holes and traversable wormholes.

Abstract: A recently proposed class of black-bounce solutions sourced by anisotropic fluids, whose exterior reproduces the Reissner-Nordström geometry all the way down to a minimal-area bounce, was put forward by Lessa and Olmo. In this work, we show that this solution conceals a thin-shell structure precisely at the bounce. Motivated by this observation, we construct a new class of black-bounce solutions with static, spherical symmetry featuring the emergence of a minimal-area bounce supported by a thin shell. This nontrivial topological structure is shown to arise from the degeneracy of the metric at the bounce, where the inverse metric becomes ill-defined, leading to curvature tensors with distributional discontinuities precisely at that point. As a consequence, a delta-function thin shell of energy-condition-violating matter is necessarily present at the bounce. To generate such solutions, we draw on proposals from quantum gravity that predict the existence of a fundamental minimum length scale, employing energy density profiles that encode these quantum-gravitational effects in an effective manner, replacing the point-like description of matter at minimal-length scales with a smeared one. We consider two well-motivated profiles, of Lorentzian and Gaussian type, to generate a novel class of solutions exhibiting the features of either regular black holes or wormholes, whose center conceals a thin-shell bounce. In addition, we investigate the thermodynamics of these compact objects.

</details>


### [84] [Operator-Level Quantum-Classical Correspondence in Relativistic Quantum Theory and Curved Spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25360)
*Pankaj Sheoran,Gopal Kashyap,Sanjay Siwach*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends a quantum-classical correspondence—previously established for non-relativistic particles—to quantum field theory and linearized gravity: Heisenberg equations of motion for quantum fields (scalar, electromagnetic, Yang–Mills, gravitational) reproduce their classical counterparts *exactly at the operator level*, without invoking ℏ→0; the result holds in flat and curved spacetimes, including weak-field gravity and semiclassical particle geodesics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the recently discovered operator-level correspondence between quantum dynamics and Newtonian mechanics generalizes to relativistic quantum fields and gravity.

Method: Analysis of Heisenberg equations of motion for quantum field operators in flat and curved spacetime; explicit derivation of operator equations matching classical Klein-Gordon, Maxwell, Yang-Mills, and linearized Einstein equations; examination of semiclassical limits for particle motion.

Result: Heisenberg equations for quantum fields identically reproduce classical field equations at the operator level—no ℏ→0 limit required; this holds for all major fundamental field theories and extends covariantly to curved spacetime; in weak-field gravity, metric perturbations obey linearized Einstein equations operatorially; quantum particle position/momentum operators recover geodesic motion semiclassically.

Conclusion: A robust, universal operator-level quantum-classical correspondence exists across quantum mechanics, quantum field theory, and linearized quantum gravity—suggesting classical dynamics emerge intrinsically from quantum operator structure, not merely as a semiclassical or ℏ→0 approximation.

Abstract: In a recent work \cite{Shaikh:2026nsi}, it was shown that for a non-relativistic particle, the operator equations of motion can be cast in the same differential form as Newton's equation of motion. This establishes a correspondence between quantum operator dynamics and classical mechanics at the level of the equations of motion. In this work, we investigate whether this correspondence extends to quantum field theory and linearized gravity. We explicitly show that the Heisenberg equations of motion for quantum field operators reproduce the corresponding classical equations of motion for a broad class of field theories. The Klein-Gordon equation, Maxwell's equations, Yang-Mills equations, and linearized Einstein equations are shown to arise at the operator level without taking the limit $\hbar\rightarrow0$. We then extend the analysis to curved spacetime and show that the quantum field operators satisfy the corresponding covariant field equations at the operator level. In the weak-field approximation, we show that the Heisenberg evolution of the metric-perturbations reproduces the linearized Einstein equations at the operator level. For a quantum particle in curved spacetime, the Heisenberg equations for position and momentum reproduce classical geodesic motion in the semiclassical limit, thereby extending the operator-level quantum-classical correspondence from fields to particle dynamics.

</details>


### [85] [Light Cone Thermodynamics: Quasi-Local Formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25477)
*Matheus G. Barbosa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a quasi-local thermodynamic framework for general relativity by extending the Bondi-Sachs formalism to past light cones, demonstrating quasi-local Hawking radiation and an irreversible inequality analogous to the second law; it integrates spacetime dynamics into thermodynamics via the Landau-Lifshitz pseudotensor and enables rest-mass density definition independent of particle number dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a quasi-local thermodynamic formalism for general relativity, motivated by deep connections between gravity and thermodynamics—especially black hole thermodynamics—and the need to generalize classical thermodynamics to include dynamical spacetime.
