Method: Generalization of the Bondi-Sachs formalism to past light cones of an arbitrary worldline; derivation of quasi-local energy (via Landau-Lifshitz pseudotensor); analysis of energy fluxes and entropy-like inequalities; illustration via explicit examples exhibiting Hawking radiation in quasi-local settings.

Result: Demonstration of quasi-local Hawking radiation under mild geometric conditions; identification of an 'irreversible inequality' satisfying second-law-like behavior; construction of a postulational thermodynamic framework incorporating spacetime dynamics; definition of rest-mass density purely from thermodynamic variables, decoupled from constituent number evolution.

Conclusion: Classical thermodynamics can be generalized to include gravitational dynamics via a quasi-local framework rooted in light-cone geometry and energy-momentum pseudotensors; this supports a fundamental thermodynamic interpretation of spacetime, with implications for quantum gravity and gravitational entropy.

Abstract: Inspired by black hole thermodynamics and other results suggesting that general relativity has some intrinsic connection with thermodynamics, this work aims to establish a quasi-local thermodynamic formalism in general spacetimes. With this purpose, we generalize the Bondi-Sachs formalism to past light cones associated with an arbitrary worldline. From this generalization, we demonstrate the emission of Hawking radiation in a quasi-local context through a few examples, suggesting that it may occur under mild conditions. In addition, we argue that an "irreversible inequality" is satisfied in typical gravitational systems, indicating that an analogue of the second law may be established. Together with a definition of internal energy derived from the Landau-Lifshitz pseudotensor, these results are used to construct a postulational framework that generalizes classical thermodynamics in order to include the dynamics of spacetime. As a consequence, it is possible to define the rest-mass density from thermodynamic variables, decoupling it from the dynamics of constituent numbers.

</details>


### [86] [Cosmology in sixth-order higher-derivative theories of gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25387)
*Carlos Aguilar,Manuel Asorey,Diego Muñoz,Miguel Pardina*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies cosmological implications of higher-derivative gravity theories (quadratic and six-derivative curvature invariants), analyzing background solutions (including non-singular bounces), stability of tensor and scalar perturbations, and inflationary predictions via EFT around the Starobinsky model—finding controlled deviations in observables while preserving consistency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how higher-derivative gravity evades singularity theorems, supports viable cosmological backgrounds, and yields testable inflationary predictions beyond standard quadratic gravity.

Method: Analytical study of homogeneous isotropic cosmological solutions in both Jordan and Einstein frames; linear stability analysis of tensor and scalar perturbations; EFT treatment of six-derivative operators as corrections to the Starobinsky background; computation of modified primordial power spectra.

Result: Non-singular bouncing solutions exist; singularity avoidance is tied to higher-derivative degrees of freedom; tensor perturbations are analytically stable on generic backgrounds but unstable on singularity-free ones; de Sitter exhibits super-horizon scalar instabilities; sixth-order corrections yield controlled, EFT-consistent modifications to ns and r.

Conclusion: Sixth-order curvature operators provide a consistent UV-sensitive extension of Starobinsky inflation, enabling phenomenological probes of quantum gravity via precision CMB observables—despite inherent scalar instabilities in exact dS and background-dependent tensor instability.

Abstract: We analyze the cosmological implications of theories of gravity with quadratic curvature invariants $R^2$ and $C^{μνρσ}C_{μνρσ}$, and six-derivative operators $R\Box R$ and $C^{μνρσ}\Box C_{μνρσ}$. We first analyze homogeneous and isotropic cosmological solutions, showing that the theory admits expanding, recollapsing and non-singular bouncing solutions. We clarify in the Einstein-frame formulation the mechanism that circumvents the Hawking--Penrose singularity theorems, and identify the role played by the additional higher-derivative degrees of freedom in that mechanism. We show that the complete set of linear equations governing tensor perturbations on standard backgrounds admit analytically stable solutions. The analysis reveals that the stability properties are determined by the pole structure of the propagator and by specific relations among the higher-derivative couplings, while the singularity-free backgrounds are found to be unstable against tensor perturbations. For scalar cosmological perturbations, the higher-derivative theories presents super-horizon scalar instabilities in de Sitter spacetimes for both quadratic and sixth-order gravity. To extract inflationary observables, we adopt an EFT approach to the sixth-order theory, treating these terms as perturbative corrections around the Starobinsky background. Within this framework, we compute the modification of the primordial scalar and tensor power spectra, obtaining predictions for the scalar spectral index and tensor-to-scalar ratio. We find that sixth-order operators produce controlled deviations from the Starobinsky model while preserving the EFT expansion, thereby providing a consistent phenomenological framework to probe ultraviolet gravitational corrections through cosmological observables.

</details>


### [87] [A Systematic Study of Resonance-Driven Flux Modifications in Extreme-Mass-Ratio Inspirals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25392)
*Edoardo Levati,Lennox S. Keeble,Alejandro Cárdenas-Avendaño*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes resonance-induced corrections to energy, angular momentum, and Carter constant fluxes for extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) using frequency-domain Teukolsky equation solutions via pybhpt; it derives an analytical selection rule for radial-polar phase dependence and characterizes how resonance coefficients depend on black-hole spin, eccentricity, and inclination—especially for 3:2 and 2:1 resonances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Transient orbital resonances in EMRIs introduce phase-dependent corrections that can significantly alter long-term orbital evolution and gravitational-wave signals; accurate modeling of these effects is essential for LISA data analysis and strong-field gravity tests.

Method: Solves the Teukolsky equation in the frequency domain using the public code pybhpt; coherently combines degenerate radial and polar harmonics at resonance; analytically derives a selection rule for radial-polar phase dependence; computes resonance coefficients across a broad orbital parameter space including spin, eccentricity, and inclination.

Result: Quantified resonance-induced modifications to all three fundamental fluxes (energy, axial angular momentum, Carter constant); derived a new analytical selection rule; mapped resonance coefficient dependencies for 3:2 and 2:1 resonances on black-hole spin, eccentricity, and inclination; produced the largest set of Teukolsky-based resonance coefficients to date.

Conclusion: Resonance coefficients reflect a competition between symmetry-induced cancellations and orbital phase-space sampling efficiency; the computed coefficients provide critical input for accurate EMRI waveform models incorporating transient resonances, especially for LISA.

Abstract: Transient orbital resonances can introduce phase-dependent corrections to the evolution of extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs), potentially altering their long-term dynamics and emitted gravitational-wave signals. In this work, we quantify the resonance-induced modifications to the energy, axial angular momentum, and Carter constant fluxes and compute the corresponding resonance coefficients across a broad region of the orbital parameter space. Using the publicly available \texttt{pybhpt} code, we solve the Teukolsky equation in the frequency domain to coherently combine the degenerate radial and polar harmonics that arise at resonance. We analytically derive a selection rule governing the relative radial-polar phase dependence of the resonant flux modifications. We argue that the relative strengths of different resonances reflect a balance between symmetry-induced cancellations and the degree to which the resonant orbit samples the underlying two-dimensional orbital phase space. For the dynamically important $3{:}2$ and $2{:}1$ resonances, we also characterize how the resonance coefficients vary with the primary black-hole spin, orbital eccentricity and inclination. Our results constitute the largest set of Teukolsky-based resonance coefficients calculated to date and provide essential input for future studies of transient orbital resonances in EMRIs.

</details>


### [88] [Analytical and spectral characterization of floral patterns in higher-order polynomial pp-waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25459)
*F. L. Carneiro,H. P. de Carvalho,L. A. Cabral,M. P. Lobo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how particle rings deform under higher-order polynomial pp-wave perturbations, showing that transverse tidal eigendirections induce m-fold symmetric stretching/compression sectors; geodesic integration and Fourier analysis confirm dominant n=m angular response, with m=4 loops arising from geometric folding—not harmonic mixing—and temporal pulse moments distinguish displacement vs. velocity memory while preserving angular symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the floral (multi-lobed) deformation patterns of particle rings induced by higher-order polynomial modes of pp-waves—specifically how tidal eigendirections, pulse structure, and geodesic dynamics govern symmetry, mode dominance, and memory effects.

Method: Analytical derivation of weak-pulse first-order ring response using transverse tidal eigendirections; direct numerical geodesic integration for m=2,3,4; Fourier decomposition of ring deformation to quantify angular mode content; analysis of temporal pulse moments to separate displacement and velocity memory effects.

Result: Transverse tidal eigendirections produce m interlaced radial stretching/compression sectors; geodesics confirm predicted orientation and discrete m-fold symmetry; Fourier analysis reveals overwhelming dominance of n=m mode; m=4 'loops' result from geometric folding of the n=4 fundamental mode, not nonlinear harmonic generation; temporal pulse moments distinguish memory types but preserve the n=m angular signature.

Conclusion: Floral deformations of particle rings under higher-order pp-waves are governed linearly by the wave’s angular mode number m, with symmetry, mode purity, and memory type all analytically tractable—challenging assumptions about harmonic mixing in gravitational wave memory signatures.

Abstract: We investigate analytically and numerically the floral deformation of particle rings induced by higher-order polynomial modes of pp-waves. The transverse tidal eigendirections determine $m$ interlaced sectors of radial stretching and compression, while a weak-pulse solution provides the complete first-order response of the ring. Direct geodesic integration for $m=2,3,4$ confirms the predicted orientation and discrete symmetry. A Fourier analysis shows that the response remains overwhelmingly dominated by the angular mode $n=m$; in particular, the loops found for $m=4$ arise from geometrical folding of the fundamental mode rather than strong harmonic mixing. The temporal moments of the pulse further distinguish displacement from velocity memory without changing this angular signature.

</details>


### [89] [Quadratic Gravitational-Wave Scattering by Kerr Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25522)
*Lennox S. Keeble,Hengrui Zhu,Lawrence E. Kidder,Harald P. Pfeiffer,Mark A. Scheel*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates second-order perturbations of Kerr black holes under monochromatic gravitational wave incidence, focusing on quadratic coupling to (4,4) daughter modes; it combines numerical relativity and semi-analytic Teukolsky methods to reveal spin- and frequency-dependent resonance behavior distinct from linear QNM couplings, enabling advances in gravitational-wave modeling and self-force theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance precision gravitational-wave modeling and strong-field gravity tests by characterizing nonlinear (second-order) responses of Kerr black holes—particularly the quadratic coupling between scattering states and quasinormal modes—beyond the well-studied QNM interactions.

Method: Combines high-fidelity numerical relativity simulations of nearly monochromatic (ℓ,m)=(2,±2) incident gravitational waves on Kerr black holes (spin a ≤ 0.95) with a complementary semi-analytic second-order Teukolsky formalism to compute and validate the complex self-coupling to the (4,4) daughter mode at twice the parent frequency.

Result: Identifies a prograde-frequency daughter-mode resonance whose extracted frequency and width match the fundamental (4,4,0) QNM; reveals opposite spin dependence for low-frequency prograde vs. retrograde scattering; shows in-mode coupling near the (2,2,0) QNM frequency increases with spin—contrasting decreasing quadratic QNM coupling—and confirms full scattering-state dependence (not just frequency).

Conclusion: The nonlinear response of Kerr black holes to gravitational perturbations is governed by the complete parent scattering state, not merely its frequency; the combined numerical and semi-analytic framework provides a robust foundation for second-order radiative perturbation theory, with direct implications for dynamical tides, near-extremal dynamics, and gravitational self-force calculations.

Abstract: Second-order black-hole perturbation theory will be an important component of precision gravitational-wave modeling and tests of strong-field gravity with next-generation detectors. Beyond quasinormal modes (QNMs), whose quadratic interactions have been the focus of recent work in black-hole spectroscopy, a generic retarded solution consists of a continuum of real-frequency scattering states whose nonlinear interactions in Kerr remain comparatively less explored. We use numerical relativity to study the quadratic response of Kerr black holes to nearly monochromatic $(\ell,m)=(2,\pm2)$ incident gravitational waves, measuring the complex self-coupling to the outgoing $(\ell,m)=(4,4)$ daughter at twice the parent frequency for black-hole spins up to $a=0.95$. At low frequencies, the coupling is strongly suppressed and exhibits opposite spin dependence for prograde and retrograde scattering. At higher prograde frequencies, we identify a daughter-mode resonance whose frequency and width extracted from the nonlinear response track the fundamental $(\ell,m,n)=(4,4,0)$ QNM. Near the fundamental $(2,2,0)$ QNM frequency, the in-mode coupling grows with spin, in contrast to the decreasing quadratic QNM coupling, demonstrating that the nonlinear response depends on the full parent scattering state rather than on its frequency alone. A complementary semi-analytic second-order Teukolsky calculation reproduces the nonlinear response from our numerical relativity simulations. Our numerical-relativity results and their semi-analytic extension provide a basis for generic homogeneous radiative perturbations of Kerr at second order, with applications to dynamical tides, near-extremal dynamics, and second-order gravitational self-force theory.

</details>


### [90] [Axial Perturbations of Charged Kiselev Black Holes in Rastall Gravity: Matter Response and Quasinormal Modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25565)
*Yan Wang,Yun-tao Gu,Wen-Di Guo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies axial gravitoelectromagnetic quasinormal modes (QNMs) of a charged black hole in Rastall gravity surrounded by Kiselev-type anisotropic dust, deriving coupled Schrödinger-type equations; it finds that full decoupling is obstructed by combined Rastall and matter effects, but a frozen-source approximation yields sub-percent agreement in fundamental frequencies; numerical Chebyshev pseudospectral results show nontrivial dependence of QNM frequencies and damping on Rastall coupling γ, charge, and surrounding-matter strength, with all modes damped (Im ω < 0).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how modified gravity (Rastall theory) and surrounding anisotropic matter affect the stability and dynamics of charged black holes via their axial quasinormal mode spectra—particularly whether standard decoupling techniques remain valid and how observables like frequency and damping depend on model parameters.

Method: Derives perturbation equations for axial gravitoelectromagnetic modes under Kiselev-type anisotropic matter in Rastall gravity; applies a closure prescription neglecting anisotropy-direction perturbations to obtain two coupled Schrödinger-type equations; introduces a frozen-source truncation for comparison; performs Chebyshev pseudospectral numerical computation of fundamental QNMs, validated against Reissner–Nordström limit and spectral-convergence tests.

Result: Full r-independent algebraic decoupling fails when both Rastall modification and surrounding matter are present; the matter-response system exhibits one-way coupling (vs. two-way in truncated reference), yet fundamental frequencies differ by <1%; increasing small γ lowers both oscillation frequency and damping rate, while large γ increases frequency and induces nonmonotonic damping; larger black-hole charge increases frequency; stronger surrounding matter increases mode lifetime; all fundamental axial modes are damped (Im ω < 0).

Conclusion: The interplay between Rastall gravity and Kiselev-type surrounding matter qualitatively alters the structure of axial perturbation equations—preventing standard decoupling—but quantitative impact on fundamental QNMs remains modest; the system remains stable (no growing modes), and parameter trends suggest potential observational signatures distinguishing Rastall gravity from general relativity in astrophysical black-hole ringdowns.

Abstract: We investigate axial gravitoelectromagnetic quasinormal modes of a charged black hole surrounded by dust-like Kiselev-type anisotropic matter in Rastall gravity. Under a closure prescription that neglects perturbations of the anisotropy direction, the system reduces to two coupled Schrödinger-type equations. These equations admit an $r$-independent algebraic decoupling in the Reissner--Nordström and general-relativistic dust-like limits, but such a decoupling is generically obstructed when both the Rastall modification and the surrounding-matter contribution are present. For comparison, we introduce an auxiliary frozen-source truncation, whose potential matrix differs from the matter-response matrix in only two entries. For the axial dipole, the matter-response system has one-way coupling, whereas the truncated reference system has two-way coupling. Nevertheless, representative fundamental frequencies of the matter-response system and the reference system differ only at the sub-percent level. A Chebyshev pseudospectral calculation, validated through the Reissner--Nordström limit and spectral-convergence tests, shows that increasing the dimensionless Rastall coupling $γ$ lowers both the oscillation frequency and the damping rate in the small-$γ$ region. In the large-$γ$ region, the oscillation frequency rises, whereas the damping rate varies nonmonotonically. A larger black-hole charge generally raises the oscillation frequency, whereas stronger surrounding-matter contributions tend to produce longer-lived modes. All surveyed fundamental axial modes satisfy $\operatorname{Im}ω<0$.

</details>


### [91] [Lensing Signatures of a Primary Hair Black Hole Solution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25667)
*Rahul Das,Umananda Dev Goswami*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies gravitational lensing by a 'primary hair' black hole derived via gravitational decoupling from Schwarzschild geometry, analyzing both weak-field (Gibbons-Werner) and strong-field (Bozza) regimes; it finds α dominates leading-order deflection, Q induces repulsive gravity, and constrains Q using EHT Sgr A* shadow data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe how geometric deformations—introduced via gravitational decoupling with dominant energy condition—alter gravitational lensing signatures, particularly distinguishing effects of the primary hair parameter Q and coupling constant α in comparison to Schwarzschild BHs.

Method: Applies Gibbons-Werner method for weak-field lensing (up to second-order deflection angle) and Bozza's strong-field formalism near the photon sphere; uses Sgr A* as astrophysical lens to compute observables (ϑ∞, s, rmag) and constrain Q against EHT shadow radius measurements.

Result: In weak field: α contributes more than Q to leading-order deflection; Q induces effectively repulsive gravity. In strong field: logarithmic divergence of deflection angle yields relativistic images; EHT shadow data yields upper bounds on Q depending on α.

Conclusion: The primary hair Q and coupling α produce distinct, measurable imprints on both weak- and strong-field lensing; current EHT observations of Sgr A* already place nontrivial constraints on Q, demonstrating the viability of lensing as a probe of GD-derived BH hair.

Abstract: We investigate the weak and strong field gravitational lensing of light by a primary hair black hole (BH) solution obtained within the framework of the gravitational decoupling (GD) approach. In this method, the BH solution is constructed by introducing geometric deformations to a known seed spacetime, which, in the present case, is the Schwarzschild geometry. The deformation functions are determined by imposing the dominant energy condition, leading to the emergence of two characteristic parameters, the primary hair $Q$ and the coupling constant $α$, both of which deform the Schwarzschild spacetime. To explore the lensing properties of this BH, we employ the Gibbons-Werner approach in the weak field regime and Bozza's formalism in the strong field regime. In the weak field limit, we derive the deflection angle up to second-order corrections and find that the coupling parameter $α$ contributes more significantly to the leading-order deflection than the primary hair parameter $Q$. The primary hair is also found to exhibit an effectively repulsive gravitational behavior. In the strong field regime, we compute the deflection angle of photons propagating near the photon sphere, where the deflection diverges logarithmically, allowing photons to complete multiple loops around the BH and form relativistic images. Using the supermassive BH Sgr A* as the astrophysical lens, we further investigate the strong lensing observables, namely the asymptotic angular position $\vartheta_{\infty}$, the angular separation $s$, and the relative magnitude $r_{\mathrm{mag}}$. We also constrain the primary hair parameter $Q$ for different values of the coupling constant $α$ using the shadow observations of Sgr A* reported by the Event Horizon Telescope (EHT) Collaboration.

</details>


### [92] [Thermodynamics of arbitrary spherical surfaces in FLRW spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25763)
*Xun Gong,Jun Wang,Chen-Yang Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes a unified thermodynamic framework for arbitrary spherically symmetric surfaces in non-flat FLRW spacetimes, showing that the Clausius relation and unified first law emerge directly from Einstein’s equations and energy-momentum conservation—without ad hoc temperature prescriptions—thereby resolving prior inconsistencies in cosmological event-horizon thermodynamics and affirming gravity-thermodynamics correspondence as generic, not horizon-specific.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve longstanding inconsistencies in cosmological event-horizon thermodynamics—particularly conflicting temperature definitions—and to test whether gravity-thermodynamics correspondence extends beyond causal horizons to arbitrary spherically symmetric surfaces in dynamical spacetimes.

Method: Analytical derivation within general relativity: starting from the Einstein field equations and local energy-momentum conservation (∇_μ T^μν = 0), the authors derive the unified first law and Clausius relation for arbitrary spherically symmetric surfaces in non-flat FLRW spacetimes; they identify the physically consistent matter-flux measure and compute surface temperatures directly from gravitational dynamics (e.g., surface gravity for null horizons, Kodama–Hayward formalism for dynamical cases).

Result: The Clausius relation δQ = T dS and unified first law hold universally for any spherically symmetric surface; cosmological apparent and event horizons emerge as special cases; event-horizon temperature derived purely from gravitational dynamics satisfies both thermodynamic laws identically and reduces to the Gibbons-Hawking temperature in de Sitter space; prior inconsistencies are traced to incompatible phenomenological temperature choices, not structural flaws.

Conclusion: Gravity-thermodynamics correspondence is intrinsic to gravitational dynamics in general relativity—not contingent on horizon causality or stationarity—and applies generically to all spherically symmetric surfaces in FLRW spacetimes, reinforcing its fundamental status in theoretical gravity.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the thermodynamics of arbitrary spherically symmetric surfaces in non-flat FLRW spacetimes within general relativity. Adopting the gravity-to-thermodynamics perspective, the Clausius relation and the unified first law follow directly from the gravitational field equations and matter dynamics, with a central role played by the matter-flux measure derived from energy-momentum conservation. The cosmological apparent-horizon and event-horizon thermodynamics are both recovered as special cases. In particular, the event-horizon temperatures derived from the gravitational dynamics satisfy both thermodynamic relations without introducing phenomenological modifications, and the corresponding temperature reduces to the Gibbons-Hawking temperature in the de Sitter limit. We further show that previous inconsistencies originate from incompatible temperature prescriptions rather than from any intrinsic deficiency in the thermodynamics of the cosmological event horizon. These results demonstrate that the gravity-thermodynamics correspondence is a generic feature of gravitational dynamics rather than a property unique to causal horizons.

</details>


### [93] [Radiation-pressure induced chaos in multipendular Fabry-Perot resonators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25872)
*V. Pierro,I. M. Pinto*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates chaotic behavior in multipendular Fabry-Perot (FP) resonators without light propagation delay, estimates the probability of chaos emergence near stable equilibria, analyzes its parametric dependence, and discusses implications for gravitational wave interferometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and quantify chaos emergence in multipendular FP resonators—systems relevant to high-precision interferometric gravitational wave detectors—where conventional linear or periodic assumptions may break down.

Method: Numerical and/or analytical estimation of the probability of chaotic evolution from initial conditions near a stable equilibrium; parametric analysis of system parameters influencing this probability.

Result: Quantified dependence of chaotic solution probability on key resonator parameters; demonstration that chaos can arise even in absence of propagation delay.

Conclusion: Chaos is a non-negligible dynamical feature in multipendular FP resonators, with potential implications for stability, noise modeling, and control design in gravitational wave antennas.

Abstract: The occurrence of chaos in multipendular FP resonators (without light propagation delay) is illustrated. The probability of evolving a chaotic solution in a given time, starting from initial conditions contained in a neighbourhood of a stable equilibrium point, is estimated, and its dependence on the most relevant system parameters is investigated. The possible relevance of these results for interferometric gravitational wave antennas is discussed.

</details>


### [94] [Spacetimes of maximal proper acceleration and muon mean lifetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25903)
*Ricardo Gallego Torromé*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that spacetimes with maximal proper acceleration reduce the muon's mean lifetime; the effect is small for most candidates but potentially detectable at future muon colliders when maximal proper acceleration equals Schwinger’s acceleration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate quantum-gravitational or high-acceleration effects on particle lifetimes, specifically whether a fundamental maximal proper acceleration modifies muon decay in relativistic spacetimes.

Method: Theoretical analysis of muon lifetime in spacetimes endowed with a maximal proper acceleration constraint; comparison across candidate values—including Schwinger’s acceleration—using relativistic quantum mechanics and effective spacetime geometry.

Result: A reduction in muon mean lifetime arises universally in such spacetimes; magnitude depends on the chosen maximal acceleration scale; only Schwinger’s acceleration yields a potentially measurable effect (~10^{-3}–10^{-2} level) in next-generation muon colliders.

Conclusion: Maximal proper acceleration induces observable time-dilation-like corrections to muon decay, offering a novel phenomenological probe of Planck-scale physics; Schwinger’s acceleration emerges as the most promising candidate for experimental verification.

Abstract: In this paper, it is shown that in spacetimes of maximal proper acceleration, there is a reduction of the mean lifetime of the muon. We consider several candidates for the maximal proper acceleration. For all of them, the induced corrections are small, but for the case when the maximal proper acceleration is identified with the Schwinger's acceleration, the effect is detectable in future muon collider facilities.

</details>


### [95] [Gravitational Imprints of Dark Energy in Neutron Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25910)
*O. P. Jyothilakshmi,Lakshmi J. Naik,V. Sreekanth*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies non-radial f-mode oscillations of neutron stars with a dark energy core using general relativity, finding that dark energy softens the equation of state, reduces maximum mass, and modifies oscillation properties while remaining consistent with current astrophysical constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how a dark energy core affects neutron star structure and dynamics—specifically non-radial f-mode oscillations, tidal deformability, and gravitational wave signatures—under fully general relativistic treatment, motivated by theoretical interest in exotic stellar interiors and observational constraints from pulsars and GW events.

Method: Constructed NS models with a relativistic mean field EoS for nuclear matter and a modified Chaplygin fluid EoS for dark energy; solved TOV equations for stellar structure; computed non-radial f-mode frequencies and damping times via linearized perturbation theory in GR; evaluated tidal deformability, GW energy, and strain; varied DE EoS parameters (especially transition density) to assess impact on observables.

Result: DE core softens EoS and lowers maximum NS mass; mass-radius relations satisfy PSR J0030+0451 and PSR J0740+6620 constraints; lower transition densities yield broader modifications to structure and f-mode properties; f-mode frequency–tidal deformability correlation remains consistent with GW170817/GW190814; normalized f-mode energy distribution is nearly universal; predicted f-mode GW strain falls within sensitivity range of third-generation detectors (e.g., Einstein Telescope, Cosmic Explorer).

Conclusion: Dark energy cores in neutron stars produce quantifiable, observationally accessible imprints on f-mode oscillations and tidal responses without violating current multimessenger constraints—suggesting f-modes could serve as probes of exotic interior physics in upcoming GW astronomy.

Abstract: We investigate, for the first time, the non-radial $f$-mode oscillations of neutron stars (NSs) with a dark energy (DE) core utilising a fully general relativistic treatment. We construct the stellar profile by considering a relativistic mean field model for nuclear matter and a modified Chaplygin fluid like prescription for DE. We compute the stellar structure, $f$-mode oscillations, tidal deformability, and gravitational wave (GW) energy and strain of the NS with DE core by varying the DE equation of state (EoS) parameters. Our study reveals that the inclusion of DE softens EoS and reduces the maximum mass compared to the pure neutron star. The obtained mass-radius values are consistent with observational constraints from massive pulsars such as PSR J0030+0451 and PSR J0740+6620. We find that the extent of the DE core, determined by the transition density, plays a crucial role in modifying the stellar structure and oscillation properties, with lower transition densities producing appreciable changes over a wider mass range. The $f$-mode frequencies and damping times exhibit systematic modifications, for higher mass configurations, while the correlation between the $f$-mode frequency and tidal deformability remains consistent with observational constraints from GW170817 and GW190814. Further, the normalised oscillation energy distribution remains nearly universal, with only minimal deviations for the higher mass configurations. Finally, we estimate the characteristic GW strain associated with the $f$-mode oscillations and find that the predicted signals lie within the sensitivity band of future third generation GW detectors.

</details>


### [96] [Weakly nonlinear strong-field signatures of spontaneously vectorized Einstein-vector-Gauss-Bonnet black holes near the Schwarzschild bifurcation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26266)
*Sardor Murodov,Bekzod Rahmatov,Islom Egamberdiev,Dilnavoz Kamalova,Javlon Rayimbaev,Bobomurat Ahmedov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the static, spherically symmetric electric branch of spontaneously vectorized Einstein-vector-Gauss-Bonnet black holes near the Schwarzschild bifurcation point using a weakly nonlinear expansion around the fundamental vector zero mode; it computes leading-order corrections to key strong-field geometric and orbital features (e.g., photon sphere, ISCO, shadow scale, frequencies, Lyapunov exponent) and shows analytic vanishing of the ADM-mass correction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize the local strong-field geometry of the electric branch of spontaneously vectorized black holes near the Schwarzschild bifurcation, where spontaneous vectorization sets in, and to understand how observables deviate from GR predictions in the weakly nonlinear regime.

Method: Weakly nonlinear expansion around the fundamental vector zero mode; independent determination of critical coupling via shooting and collocation methods; verification of second-order metric corrections against unreduced covariant field equations; derivation of third-order Fredholm solvability condition for leading departure from bifurcation; computation of geometric and orbital quantities (photon sphere, shadow scale, MBO, ISCO, orbital/epicyclic frequencies, 3:2 radius, photon-orbit instability, strong-deflection coefficient).

Result: ADM-mass correction vanishes analytically; photon sphere and ISCO shift outward, while the 3:2 frequency-ratio radius shifts inward; photon-orbit angular frequency decreases and its Lyapunov exponent increases; all corrections are derived to leading order near bifurcation.

Conclusion: The electric vectorized branch exhibits systematic, calculable deviations from Schwarzschild geometry in strong-field observables near the bifurcation, fully characterizing its local geometric structure—though these results are not extrapolable to the strongly nonlinear regime or intended as direct observational constraints.

Abstract: We study the static, spherically symmetric electric branch of spontaneously vectorized Einstein-vector-Gauss-Bonnet black holes close to its Schwarzschild bifurcation. A weakly nonlinear expansion is built around the fundamental vector zero mode. The critical coupling is determined independently by shooting and collocation, the second-order metric correction is checked against the unreduced covariant equations, and the corresponding ADM-mass correction is shown to vanish analytically. The leading departure of the vectorized branch from the bifurcation point follows from a third-order Fredholm solvability condition. Using the resulting metric, we calculate the leading corrections to the photon sphere, shadow scale, marginally bound orbit, innermost stable circular orbit, orbital and epicyclic frequencies, the geometric three-to-two frequency-ratio radius, the instability of the circular photon orbit, and the logarithmic strong-deflection coefficient. Near the bifurcation, the photon sphere and innermost stable orbit move outward, whereas the three-to-two radius moves inward. The photon-orbit angular frequency decreases and its Lyapunov exponent increases. These results characterize the local strong-field geometry of the electric vectorized branch. They are not intended as observational constraints or as an extrapolation to the strongly nonlinear regime.

</details>


### [97] [Area-product universality in multi-horizon black hole entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26289)
*Francisco Tello-Ortiz,Y. Gómez-Leyton,Jean Báez Cuevas,Emmanuel N. Saridakis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper reveals a structural link between black-hole entropy's logarithmic corrections and classical multi-horizon area-product relations: while the Bekenstein-Hawking term depends on the sum of horizon areas, the logarithmic correction depends on their product; thus, mass-independent classical area products imply universal logarithmic corrections—except in cases like Reissner-Nordström-de Sitter, where the area-product rule holds but mass independence fails for physical horizons, showing statistical selection and classical universality are distinct.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover whether the universality observed in black-hole entropy (microscopic) and multi-horizon area-product relations (classical geometric) are fundamentally connected, beyond the leading area law.

Method: Extends a minimal statistical discretization of horizon area to multiple Killing horizons, treating distinct horizon sectors as statistically independent; derives general area-product rules from entropy counting, isolating contributions from leading (sum-based) and logarithmic (product-based) terms.

Result: Logarithmic corrections to black-hole entropy encode the product of horizon areas; hence, when the classical area product is mass-independent (e.g., Reissner-Nordström, Kerr-Newman), the logarithmic correction inherits universality—but this fails generically for the physical horizons in Reissner-Nordström-de Sitter despite validity of the area-product identity.

Conclusion: The logarithmic correction to black-hole entropy carries distinctive global geometric information about multi-horizon structure, revealing a previously hidden bridge between microscopic entropy counting and classical spacetime geometry—beyond what the area law alone can disclose.

Abstract: Black-hole entropy and multi-horizon area-product relations represent two apparently distinct manifestations of universality, arising respectively from microscopic statistics and classical geometry. We show that these structures are unexpectedly connected. Extending a minimum-assumptions statistical discretization to multiple Killing horizons, with distinct horizon sectors treated as statistically independent, we derive a general area-product rule. In particular, while the leading Bekenstein-Hawking entropy probes the sum of the horizon areas, the logarithmic correction probes their product. Consequently, whenever the classical area product is mass independent, the logarithmic entropy automatically inherits this universality. We demonstrate this for Reissner-Nordström and, under an area-based extension to rotating horizons, Kerr-Newman black holes. However, Reissner-Nordström-de Sitter provides a crucial three-horizon counterexample, for which the area-product rule survives while mass independence is generically lost for the physical horizons. Thus, statistical area-product selection and classical universality are distinct properties. Our results uncover a structural connection between microscopic entropy counting and classical multi-horizon geometry that is invisible at the level of the leading area law, suggesting that logarithmic corrections encode distinctive information about the global horizon structure.

</details>


### [98] [Potential-space geometry and scalar rigidity of the Plebański-Demiański ansatz without a cosmological constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26391)
*Leonel Bixano,Tonatiuh Matos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates stationary, axisymmetric Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton (EMD) theory in the conformal Carter class using a generalized Ernst formalism; it establishes coordinate correspondences, derives reconstruction equations, and proves—under physically motivated assumptions (ordinary dilaton, quadratic Stäckel 1-form, smooth Carter structure)—that no local nonconstant solutions exist.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify and constrain local solutions of Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton gravity in the conformal Carter class, particularly to determine whether nontrivial (nonconstant) dilaton configurations can coexist with exact Carter-type separability and physical regularity.

Method: Generalized Ernst formalism on the space of potentials; local correspondence between Plebański-Demiański coordinates and Weyl data; harmonic condition on invariant area density for Λ=0; principal chiral splitting to isolate electromagnetic misalignment and acceleration effects; derivation of Einstein reconstruction equations; imposition of exactness and consistency conditions on quadratic Stäckel scalar 1-forms.

Result: Under the assumptions of an ordinary (non-phantom) dilaton, fixed nonzero coupling α₀, and an exact quadratic Stäckel scalar 1-form, all potential equations and reconstruction constraints force elimination of the only candidate aligned branch—yielding no local nonconstant solution. The result is specific to this class and does not apply to phantom scalars, higher-degree conformal factors, or non-Stäckel 1-forms.

Conclusion: Within the quadratic Stäckel class and standard physical assumptions, there are no local nonconstant solutions of Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton theory with zero cosmological constant in the conformal Carter class; this highlights a rigidity of the dilaton under separability and regularity constraints.

Abstract: We analyze stationary, axisymmetric Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton theory with zero cosmological constant in the conformal Carter class, employing a generalized Ernst approach on the space of potentials. We begin by establishing a complete local correspondence between Plebański-Demiański principal coordinates and Weyl data, covering the electric, magnetic, and twist potentials as well as the 1-forms \(A\), \(B\), and \(C\). In the source-free Weyl reduction, requiring the invariant area density to be harmonic singles out the \(Λ=0\) sector, and the principal chiral splitting isolates both electromagnetic misalignment and the part due to acceleration. Next, we obtain the Einstein reconstruction equations for general local EMD configurations in this setting. In a physical Carter domain with an ordinary scalar, the bilinear factor \(\Om=1-\acc pq\) enforces \(C=0\) and \(B_-=0\). With fixed \(\alphao\neq0\), the scalar field equation further rules out the charged PD Coulomb-type 1-form. Lastly, assuming an exact quadratic Stäckel scalar 1-form, the conditions of exactness together with reconstruction restrict the system to a single aligned branch, but consistency with the full potential equations removes this branch for arbitrary smooth Carter structure functions. Consequently, within the quadratic Stäckel class studied here, there is no local nonconstant solution with an ordinary dilaton. This conclusion does not extend to phantom scalars, conformal factors of higher degree, or non-Stäckel 1-forms.

</details>


### [99] [Photon sphere for a dyonic black hole corresponding to $A_2$ Toda chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26421)
*Vladimir D. Ivashchuk,Evgeny E. Trubach*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies a dilatonic dyon black hole in a 4D gravity model with a scalar field and a 2-form, linked to the A₂ Toda chain; it derives and analyzes the fifth-order photon sphere equation, proves uniqueness and instability of circular null geodesics, and computes black hole shadow observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate geometric optics and observational signatures (e.g., photon sphere and shadow) of an exact dilatonic dyon black hole solution tied to integrable systems (A₂ Toda chain), extending understanding of charged, scalar-coupled black holes beyond standard Einstein–Maxwell theory.

Method: Analytical derivation of the null geodesic equations in the given dilatonic dyon spacetime; reduction to an effective radial potential; identification of circular null orbits via extremality condition leading to a fifth-order algebraic equation for the photon sphere radius R₀; stability analysis via second derivative test of the effective potential; computation of shadow radius via critical impact parameter and celestial coordinates.

Result: The master equation for R₀ is fifth-order but admits a unique physical root R₀ > 2μ; all circular null geodesics are unstable; explicit expressions are obtained for the shadow angular radius and critical impact parameter in terms of μ, Q₁, Q₂, and λ = √(3/2).

Conclusion: The dilatonic dyon black hole exhibits a well-defined, unique photon sphere and unstable light rings, resulting in a calculable black hole shadow—confirming robustness of shadow phenomenology even in integrable, scalar–form-coupled gravity models.

Abstract: We consider a dilatonic dyon black hole solution with a gravitational radius of $2 μ$ and two charges,
  $Q_1$ (electric) and $Q_2$ (magnetic), within the framework of a four-dimensional gravitational model comprising one scalar field and one 2-form. The dilaton coupling constant $λ$ is fixed by $λ^2 = \frac{3}{2}$.
  This solution is related to the $A_2$ Toda chain. The circular orbits of null geodesics are explored, and the fifth-order polynomial master equation governing the photon sphere radius $R_0$ is analyzed. It is shown to admit a unique solution satisfying $R_0 > 2 μ$. The circular null geodesics are proven to be unstable. Finally, the black hole shadow is examined, yielding relations for the shadow angle and the critical impact parameter.

</details>


### [100] [Stochastic motion of massless particles near event horizons in Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26475)
*Yinhan Huang,Lezhen Miao,Xiao Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether stochastic perturbations—modeling quantum effects—allow massless particles to cross the event horizon of Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström black holes in finite coordinate time; it finds that the crossing probability remains zero, preserving a key classical feature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To examine whether stochastic (quantum-inspired) perturbations alter the classical result that radially infalling massless particles require infinite coordinate time to reach the event horizon.

Method: Two stochastic formulations of the ingoing radial null geodesic equation are derived and analyzed in Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström spacetimes, treating quantum fluctuations as stochastic noise.

Result: The probability that a stochastically perturbed massless particle crosses the event horizon in finite coordinate time is rigorously shown to be zero in both spacetimes.

Conclusion: Stochastic (quantum) perturbations do not restore finite-time horizon crossing; thus, the classical causal structure near the horizon—specifically, infinite coordinate-time delay—is robust under such noise models.

Abstract: In classical general relativity, it is well known that radially moving massless particles in the exterior region take infinite coordinate time to reach the event horizon in black hole spacetimes. In this short paper, we use two stochastic versions of ingoing radial null geodesic equation to study the stochastic radial motion of massless particles in Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström spacetimes. We show that the probability that a radially moving massless particle enters the black hole in finite coordinate time remains zero. This indicates the stochastic perturbations of quantum effects preserve certain properties of classical black holes.

</details>


### [101] [Regular Cosmologies: Three Distinct Routes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26566)
*Sergio Bravo Medina,Marek Nowakowski*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores applying the concept of regular black holes—where singularities are replaced by de Sitter cores—to cosmology, deriving position-dependent effective cosmological terms Λ_eff(r) from six regular black hole metrics. It compares three prescriptions (Newtonian/McCrea-Milne, Hubble-scale running vacuum, and Misner-Sharp quasi-local) to obtain cosmological evolution; finds closed-form Friedmann solutions for the Hayward metric and inflationary (not dark-energy) behavior; shows that monotonic core density implies w ≥ −1, ruling out phantom crossing without shells; quantifies prescription differences against DESI DR2; and argues a single regularization length cannot simultaneously explain inflation and dark energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether the regular black hole paradigm—replacing singularities with de Sitter cores—can be meaningfully extended to early-universe cosmology, and to assess its implications for inflation, dark energy, and observational constraints (e.g., DESI).

Method: Derives an effective, position-dependent cosmological term Λ_eff(r) = 8πG ρ_MS(r) from six regular black hole metrics; applies three distinct physical prescriptions to map this to cosmology: (i) Newtonian (McCrea–Milne), (ii) running vacuum evaluated at the Hubble radius, and (iii) quasi-local based on Misner–Sharp mass; solves resulting Friedmann equations analytically (Hayward) or numerically; computes equation of state w(a) and compares predictions with DESI DR2 data.

Result: All prescriptions yield an early de Sitter phase (inflationary), not late-time dark energy; Hayward metric admits closed-form Friedmann solution; monotonic core density ensures w > −1 throughout, excluding phantom crossing; prescriptions converge at early times (de Sitter core) but diverge at late times; quantitative discrepancies with DESI DR2 are assessed; a single regularization length is insufficient to simultaneously fit inflationary and dark-energy scales.

Conclusion: Regular black hole-inspired cosmologies naturally produce early-universe inflation but fail to reproduce phantom-like dark energy without non-monotonic (e.g., shell-type) core densities; the three mapping prescriptions are physically distinct and yield testably different late-time dynamics; thus, regularity alone does not unify inflation and dark energy, and multi-scale regularization is required.

Abstract: Regular black holes avoid the central singularity by replacing it with a de Sitter core. We ask what cosmology follows if the same idea is brought to the early universe. Regular metrics define a position-dependent cosmological term, $Λ_{\rm eff}(r)=8πG ρ_{\rm MS}(r)$, and there is no unique way to carry it over to the expanding universe. We follow three different prescriptions: a Newtonian one following McCrea and Milne, a running vacuum evaluated at Hubble radius and a quasi-local one based on Misner-Sharp mass. We apply each of them to six regular black hole metrics and explore their consequences. In the Newtonian case the Friedmann equations are solved in closed form for the Hayward metric, and in terms of standard special functions or numerically for the others, and describe a universe that starts in a de Sitter phase and ends as dust (i.e. an inflationary model instead of dark energy). We find that in all three prescriptions a monotonically decreasing core density gives an equation of state that never crosses $w=-1$, and thus the phantom behaviour suggested by DESI would require a density shell which a regular core does not have. The three prescriptions agree on the de Sitter core but differ at late times, and we quantify the difference against the DESI DR2 results. We also point out that a single regularisation length cannot serve as both the inflationary and the dark-energy scale.

</details>


### [102] [Radial quantization of the Schwarzschild geometry: relational observables, evaporation, and remnant state](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26575)
*David Brizuela,Leonardo Chataignier,Mikel López-Escondrillas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a canonical quantum theory of the Schwarzschild black hole using a regular minisuperspace foliation, constructs semiclassical intelligent states satisfying the Robertson-Schrödinger uncertainty relation, derives quantum-corrected geometry (e.g., shifted horizon, enhanced surface gravity), obtains logarithmically corrected Bekenstein-Hawking entropy, and proposes a stable remnant state with finite horizon radius.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a well-defined, horizon-regular quantum theory of Schwarzschild black holes within canonical quantum gravity, enabling consistent description of both exterior and interior regions and analysis of quantum geometric and thermodynamic effects.

Method: Canonical quantization in a minisuperspace model with radial foliation; construction of physical Hilbert space solving the radial constraint; definition of gauge-invariant relational observables; derivation of semiclassical intelligent states saturating the Robertson-Schrödinger uncertainty relation for mass and asymptotic Killing norm.

Result: Quantum-corrected spacetime with shifted horizon position, enhanced proper acceleration and surface gravity; logarithmic correction to Bekenstein-Hawking entropy; existence of a minimal-energy remnant state with finite horizon radius.

Conclusion: The framework yields a physically coherent quantum description of Schwarzschild geometry across the horizon, predicts observable quantum gravitational corrections to geometry and thermodynamics, and suggests a viable mechanism—via a stable remnant—for resolving black hole evaporation singularity.

Abstract: We consider the canonical quantization of the Schwarzschild geometry in a minisuperspace description based on a foliation with leaves labeled by the radial variable. This formulation remains regular across the Killing horizon. In this way, the resulting quantum theory admits a physical Hilbert space of states that solve the radial constraint equation and simultaneously describe the exterior and interior regions of the black hole. We discuss the construction of quantum relational observables, which act as gauge-invariant symmetric operators on the physical Hilbert space. In order to analyze quantum effects on the geometry, we construct a family of semiclassical intelligent states that saturate the Robertson-Schrödinger uncertainty relation for both the black-hole mass and the asymptotic Killing norm, while exhibiting small relative fluctuations. The expectation values of relational observables in these states admit a clear geometric interpretation, and they lead to an effective, quantum-corrected spacetime. In particular, we find that the position of the horizon may be shifted, depending on the correlation of the state. In addition, both the proper acceleration of a static observer and the horizon surface gravity are enhanced with respect to a classical Schwarzschild black hole with the same horizon area. Using the relation between surface gravity and temperature, we then obtain corrections to the thermodynamic properties of the black hole, leading to a logarithmically corrected Bekenstein-Hawking entropy. Finally, we discuss the definition of a state of minimal energy that could be interpreted as a stable remnant characterized by a finite horizon radius, suggesting a possible mechanism for preventing the complete evaporation of the black hole.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [103] [String-Monodromy Rigidity from Six-Point Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25025)
*Jianqi Sheng*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether the monodromy properties of string scattering can be derived solely from S-matrix consistency—without invoking a worldsheet—and finds that finite-particle consistency, combined with analyticity, crossing symmetry, and dispersion constraints, tightly restricts the possible monodromy to the trigonometric (Veneziano-like) branch, identifying the missing principle for a first-principles derivation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether string scattering monodromy—a hallmark of worldsheet theory—can be derived purely from S-matrix consistency (analyticity, unitarity, crossing), bypassing assumptions about underlying worldsheet geometry.

Method: Analyzes finite-multiplicity factorization constraints at six points in a massless doubly ordered identity model; derives differential equations for four-point null kernels from exact residues; imposes positive unsubtracted dispersion, heavy spectral weight, and moment determinacy on crossing-symmetric amplitudes in the KLT module; constructs a two-scale counterexample to isolate module entrance as an independent input.

Result: Finite-particle consistency forces all analytic four-point null kernels to satisfy q'' = κq, admitting only linear, trigonometric, or hyperbolic solutions; dispersion + spectral positivity + moment determinacy uniquely selects the trigonometric branch—the Veneziano germ—up to scale; the two-scale counterexample confirms module structure is not implied by finite consistency alone.

Conclusion: Finite-to-infinite rigidity emerges: low-point S-matrix consistency strongly constrains high-order monodromy, and the missing principle for a worldsheet-free derivation of string scattering is the specification of the KLT module (i.e., how left- and right-moving degrees are paired), which must be independently imposed.

Abstract: Can the characteristic monodromy of string scattering be derived from S-matrix consistency alone, without assuming a worldsheet? We show that finite-particle consistency fixes it to a sharply defined extent. In a massless doubly ordered identity model, two exact six-point residues generate a scalar pentagon and the odd Fay identity, forcing every analytic four-point null kernel to satisfy $q''=κq$ and leaving only the linear, trigonometric, and hyperbolic branches. This converts finite-multiplicity factorization into an all-orders constraint normally associated with worldsheet geometry. For a crossing-symmetric target in the opposite KLT module, positive unsubtracted dispersion with nonzero heavy spectral weight selects the trigonometric branch, and moment determinacy fixes the Veneziano germ up to scale. A two-scale counterexample proves that module entrance is an independent input. Our result therefore identifies both a finite-to-infinite rigidity mechanism for string scattering and the precise missing principle needed for a first-principles derivation.

</details>


### [104] [Eigenvectors and Eigenvalues of $p$-Curvature Operators for KZ--type Differential Connections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25269)
*Vitaly Tarasov,Alexander Varchenko*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs eigenvectors and eigenvalues of p-curvature operators for KZ-type connections in characteristic p using a Bethe-ansatz-type method based on characteristic-p hypergeometric integrals; the resulting eigenvectors extend to flat eigensections over Frobenius neighborhoods of Bethe points.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the spectral structure of p-curvature operators—commuting endomorphisms associated with KZ-type connections in positive characteristic—and to establish a constructive, geometrically meaningful framework for their eigenvectors.

Method: A Bethe-ansatz-type construction is employed, where eigenvectors are realized as values of characteristic-p hypergeometric integrals; the extension to flat eigensections relies on Frobenius neighborhood geometry.

Result: Explicit construction of simultaneous eigenvectors and eigenvalues of the commuting p-curvature operators; proof that each such eigenvector extends uniquely to a flat eigensection over the Frobenius neighborhood of the corresponding Bethe point.

Conclusion: The p-curvature spectral problem for KZ-type connections admits a concrete, integral-based solution analogous to the classical Bethe ansatz, with deep ties to arithmetic geometry and Frobenius structures.

Abstract: A KZ-type connection in characteristic $p$ has $p$-curvature operators. These operators are commuting endomorphisms of the connection. We present a Bethe-ansatz-type construction of their eigenvectors and eigenvalues. The eigenvector is constructed as the value of a characteristic $p$ hypergeometric integral and extends to a flat eigensection over the Frobenius neighborhood of the corresponding Bethe point.

</details>


### [105] [Asymptotics for partition functions of random normal matrix models with singularities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25307)
*Kohei Noda*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives explicit asymptotic expansions of partition functions for determinantal planar Coulomb gases under various geometric and potential conditions, unifying prior results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize earlier asymptotic analyses of partition functions for planar Coulomb gases under diverse boundary and potential conditions (e.g., hard/soft edges, radial perturbations, hard wall geometries).

Method: Asymptotic analysis of determinantal planar Coulomb gases using potential-theoretic and orthogonal polynomial techniques; derivation of partition function expansions up to constant (O(1)) term.

Result: Explicit asymptotic expansions of partition functions—up to and including the O(1) term—for Coulomb gases with smooth radial potentials, radial perturbations, jump-type singularities (bulk, semi-hard, hard, soft-edge), and hard wall regions (disk, disk complement, annulus).

Conclusion: The derived expansions provide a unified framework that subsumes and extends previous results across multiple edge and confinement regimes in 2D Coulomb gas ensembles.

Abstract: We study determinantal planar Coulomb gases with a smooth radial potential, a smooth radial perturbation, jump-type singularities (including the bulk, semi-hard, hard, and soft-edge regimes), and a hard wall region given by a centered disk, a disk complement, or an annulus. We obtain explicit asymptotic expansions of the partition functions up to and including the term of order $1$. Our results unify and generalize many of the earlier works.

</details>


### [106] [Lee-Huang-Yang Energy for Dilute Bose Gases: an Upper Bound for General Potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25314)
*Giulia Basti,Morris Brooks,Serena Cenatiempo,Alessandro Olgiati,Benjamin Schlein*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives an upper bound for the ground state energy density of a dilute Bose gas with general short-range repulsive interactions, extending a prior method developed for hard spheres.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously estimate the ground state energy density of dilute Bose gases beyond the hard-sphere model, enabling broader applicability to realistic repulsive potentials.

Method: Extension of the variational and trial-state method introduced in [1] for hard spheres, adapted to general short-range repulsive two-body potentials using appropriate many-body trial wavefunctions and energy estimates.

Result: An explicit, rigorous upper bound on the ground state energy density, consistent with the Lee–Huang–Yang expansion up to second order in the scattering length.

Conclusion: The extended method successfully generalizes the hard-sphere energy bound to arbitrary short-range repulsive potentials, confirming universality of the low-density energy asymptotics in dilute Bose gases.

Abstract: We establish an upper bound for the ground state energy density of a dilute Bose gas, interacting through a general short-range, repulsive potential. To reach this goal, we extend the approach recently introduced in [1] for hard spheres.

</details>


### [107] [Comparing the Lee-Yang gap with the mass gap in the sub-critical planar Ising model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25348)
*Noam Borgnia,Jui-Hui Chung,Jacob Shapiro*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a quantitative relationship between the subcritical mass gap and the Lee-Yang gap in the Ising model on free-boundary square boxes, showing they scale as the Lee-Yang gap to the power 8/15; upper bound is derived via random-current moment bounds, a correlation inequality, and finite block expansion, while the lower bound uses the Lee-Yang product, susceptibility, and critical magnetization; additionally, uniform factorial bounds on pressure field derivatives are obtained.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the relationship between the subcritical mass gap and the Lee-Yang gap in the planar Ising model, motivated by spectral gap conjectures and the role of Lee-Yang zeros in phase transitions.

Method: Combines random-current representation techniques, a new correlation inequality, finite block expansion for upper bound; leverages Lee-Yang zero product structure, susceptibility estimates, and critical magnetization asymptotics for lower bound; applies cluster expansion and analyticity arguments for pressure derivative bounds.

Result: Proves that the subcritical mass gap ∼ (Lee-Yang gap)^{8/15} on free-boundary square boxes; establishes uniform factorial bounds on all derivatives of the pressure with respect to external field.

Conclusion: The mass gap and Lee-Yang gap are quantitatively linked with exponent 8/15 in the subcritical 2D Ising model, confirming a nontrivial scaling relation; the analytic properties of pressure are rigorously controlled, supporting full analyticity of the free energy away from criticality.

Abstract: We prove that the subcritical mass gap is comparable to the Lee-Yang gap over free-boundary square boxes raised to the power $8/15$. Random-current moment bounds, a correlation inequality and a finite block expansion give the upper bound. The lower bound follows from the Lee-Yang product, the susceptibility and the critical magnetization. We also obtain uniform factorial bounds on the field derivatives of the pressure.

</details>


### [108] [On soliton asymptotics for Maxwell--Lorentz equations with rotating particle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25391)
*Elena Kopylova*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves asymptotic stability of soliton solutions for the 2D Maxwell–Lorentz equations with an extended rotating charged particle.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous stability properties of soliton-type solutions in classical electrodynamics models involving extended, rotating charged particles.

Method: Mathematical analysis of the 2D Maxwell–Lorentz system, including spectral analysis, dispersive estimates, and modulation techniques to control perturbations around soliton manifolds.

Result: Proof of asymptotic stability: small perturbations of solitons decay over time, with the solution converging to a nearby soliton (possibly with shifted parameters) as t → ∞.

Conclusion: Soliton solutions—representing uniformly translating and rotating extended charged particles—are asymptotically stable under the 2D Maxwell–Lorentz dynamics.

Abstract: We consider the 2D Maxwell--Lorentz equations with an extended charged rotating particle. The system admits soliton solutions corresponding to a particle moving with a constant velocity and rotating with a constant angular velocity. Our main result is the asymptotic stability of the solitons.

</details>


### [109] [Finite Rodriguez-Villegas Approximants to the Riemann $ξ$-Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25564)
*Alisson M. Silva*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a sequence of finite Rodriguez-Villegas transforms—built from Bernstein polynomials of a specially constructed Stieltjes profile derived from the Riemann theta kernel—that converges locally uniformly to the Riemann ξ-function in the critical strip; it establishes exact finite functional equations and connects the numerators to fermionic supertraces and the polynomials to normalized Gibbs traces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a constructive, finite-approximation framework for the Riemann ξ-function that preserves symmetry and functional equation structure, leveraging classical approximation theory (Bernstein polynomials) and statistical–quantum analogies (Gibbs traces, supertraces).

Method: Constructs a positive symmetric Stieltjes profile on [0,1] via convolution of the Riemann theta kernel with the hyperbolic-secant kernel and logistic coordinate; applies Bernstein polynomial approximation; derives finite Rodriguez-Villegas transforms from reciprocal numerators of these polynomials; interprets numerators as fermionic supertraces and polynomials as normalized Gibbs traces.

Result: A sequence of finite transforms converging locally uniformly to ξ(s) in the critical strip; each approximant satisfies an exact finite functional equation; the reciprocal numerators are realized exactly as fermionic supertraces; Bernstein polynomials correspond to normalized Gibbs traces.

Conclusion: The Riemann ξ-function admits a canonical approximation by finite, symmetric, functionally coherent objects rooted in Stieltjes theory, Bernstein approximation, and quantum-statistical trace structures—offering new analytic and conceptual bridges between number theory, approximation theory, and mathematical physics.

Abstract: We construct a sequence of finite Rodriguez-Villegas transforms converging locally uniformly to the Riemann $ξ$-function in the critical strip. The input is a positive symmetric profile on the unit interval obtained from the Riemann theta kernel through convolution with the hyperbolic-secant kernel and the logistic coordinate. The profile is a Stieltjes function of $x(1-x)$. Its Bernstein polynomials produce reciprocal numerators and exact finite functional equations. The same numerators admit an exact realization as fermionic supertraces, while the Bernstein polynomials are normalized Gibbs traces.

</details>


### [110] [Finite images of braid group representations and algebraic solutions of KZ-type equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25805)
*Haru Negami*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies parameter values ensuring finite monodromy in the Katz-Long-Moody construction—linking group representations to algebraic solutions of KZ-type and Fuchsian differential equations—and establishes necessary/sufficient conditions for algebraicity of all solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand when representations produced by the Katz-Long-Moody construction have finite image, thereby characterizing algebraicity of solutions to associated KZ-type and Fuchsian differential equations—classical problems tied to hypergeometric functions.

Method: Classification of parameter values yielding finite-image representations on the semidirect product of a free group and a braid group, and its subgroups, under the Katz-Long-Moody construction; analysis of monodromy finiteness and its implications for regular-singular KZ-type equations and Fuchsian systems.

Result: Finite image of the braid-group representation is independent of the admissible parameter; full classification of parameters ensuring finite image on the semidirect product, free-group, and braid-group subgroups; necessary and sufficient conditions for algebraicity of all solutions to corresponding KZ-type and Fuchsian equations.

Conclusion: The Katz-Long-Moody construction yields algebraic solutions to KZ-type and Fuchsian equations precisely when the associated monodromy representations have finite image, with braid-group finiteness being parameter-independent—thus unifying representation-theoretic finiteness with classical algebraicity criteria.

Abstract: Finite monodromy provides a bridge between group representations and algebraic solutions of differential equations. We study this connection for the Katz-Long-Moody construction, which transforms representations of the semidirect product of a free group and a braid group into new representations of the same group and is related to Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov (KZ)-type equations. For a fixed finite-image input, we classify the parameter values for which the resulting representations have finite image, both on the semidirect product and on its free-group and braid-group subgroups. In particular, finiteness of the braid-group image is independent of the admissible parameter. These results give necessary and sufficient conditions for all solutions of the corresponding regular-singular KZ-type equations to be algebraic. On restriction to the free group, they also characterize finite monodromy and algebraicity of all solutions of the associated Fuchsian systems, connecting the classification to classical questions about algebraic hypergeometric functions.

</details>


### [111] [Disentangling Measurement Protocol from Material Physics in Isotypic and Anisotypic SiC/Si Heterojunctions: A Metrology-Driven Study for Betavoltaic Energy Conversion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26277)
*Mikhail V. Dolgopolov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares isotypic (n-SiC/n-Si) and anisotypic (n-SiC/p-Si) heterojunctions for betavoltaic energy conversion, demonstrating that anisotypic junctions exhibit superior diode behavior, higher open-circuit voltage (~1.2 V under beta irradiation), and significantly better charge-storage performance (0.25 V vs. 0.05 V after 30 min; 25× energy ratio), provided measurements use four-wire Kelvin sensing and proper electrical configuration to eliminate contact artifacts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies in reported performance of SiC/Si heterojunctions for betavoltaics by identifying measurement artifacts (e.g., contact resistance, interface states) and determining the optimal junction type and metrology protocol.

Method: Combined Kelvin probe force microscopy (KPFM), scanning tunnelling spectroscopy (STS), two- and four-wire electrical characterization (Keithley 2450), and supercapacitor charge-storage testing with a patented charge-pump converter under beta irradiation; introduced a measurement-interpretation framework emphasizing four-wire Kelvin sensing, generator polarity (cathode to LO), and high-impedance output-off state.

Result: Isotypic junctions show artificially enhanced low-bias current due to contact/lead resistance—eliminated by four-wire Kelvin sensing—revealing bulk/interface-limited transport; high interface state density (~5×10¹² cm⁻² eV⁻¹) in dislocation-rich regions explains local conduction anomalies; anisotypic junctions yield genuine rectification, ~1.2 V Voc under beta irradiation, and 25× higher stored energy in capacitor tests.

Conclusion: Anisotypic n-SiC/p-Si heterojunctions are the superior platform for betavoltaic conversion, but only when evaluated using artifact-free metrology—specifically four-wire Kelvin sensing and appropriate circuit configuration—to avoid misattribution of contact effects to junction properties.

Abstract: We compare isotypic (n-SiC/n-Si) and anisotypic (n-SiC/p-Si) heterojunctions using Kelvin probe force microscopy, scanning tunnelling spectroscopy, precision electrical measurements with a Keithley 2450 SourceMeter, and a 0.1 farad supercapacitor charge-storage test. Two-wire probing shows an enhanced low-bias current in the isotypic structures; four-wire Kelvin sensing removes the voltage-drop contribution of external leads and contacts and collapses this current to a much lower bulk and interface limited level. The high density of interface states detected by KPFM and STS, up to five times ten to the twelfth per square centimetre per electronvolt in dislocation-rich regions, provides a plausible origin for the enhanced local response. Anisotypic junctions exhibit a genuine diode-like current-voltage characteristic and an open-circuit voltage of about 1.2 volts under beta irradiation. In the capacitor test with the patented charge-pump converter, the anisotypic structure reaches 0.25 volts versus 0.05 volts for the isotypic one after 30 minutes, giving an energy ratio of about 25. We formulate a measurement-interpretation framework based on four-wire Kelvin sensing, generator polarity with cathode to LO, and high-impedance output-off state. Our results show that anisotypic SiC/Si heterojunctions are the preferred platform for betavoltaic conversion when measured correctly.

</details>


### [112] [Asymptotics of Hankel determinants for potentials with singular edge points](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26412)
*Dan Dai,Jia-Hao Du,Chenhao Lu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives large-n asymptotics for Hankel determinants in unitary random matrix ensembles with higher-order edge singularities, explicitly computing the constant term via a regularized Painlevé I hierarchy Hamiltonian integral, and proves universality of the eigenvalue correlation kernel near such singular edges using Deift-Zhou Riemann-Hilbert analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend asymptotic analysis of Hankel determinants to unitary ensembles with higher-order edge singularities (vanishing density order 2k+1/2), where classical Airy or Pearcey universality fails and new Painlevé I hierarchy behavior emerges.
